# How the linear trace engine is tested (GATE 3)

`trace_run` (#linear-takeoff WP3) walks a sheet's own drawn duct/pipe linework from a seed point and reports the run's length, size, and confidence. `web/bench/linear.mts` (WP1.6) originally scored only the MANUAL-mode concerns — parity, totals, determinism — and said outright, in its own header, that it was "deliberately NOT the full run-recall/precision/Fréchet/vertex-F1 suite... that's WP3+'s trace-engine scoring, and has no meaning yet [for manual mode]." This note is that scoring, and what it currently finds — so the next checkpoint starts from the number, not from scratch, same discipline as `docs/MEP-CONNECTIVITY-EVAL.md`. Runs 1-2 below were measured with a since-retired standalone script (`mcp/scripts/linear-trace-eval.mjs`); Run 3 migrated that scoring into `bench/linear.mts` itself, per the goal document's own explicit architecture (see "The ruler," below) — the findings from Runs 1-2 remain true and are kept here as history, not superseded.

## The ruler

**`web/bench/linear.mts`** (`npm run bench:linear` from `web/`) is the plan's own explicitly-named home for this: "LINEAR BENCH... Pinned real goldens (`ground_truth/linear/*.json`, authored from renders) + synthetic truth-by-construction sheets... Reports... run recall / precision (discrete Frechet < 2 pt, length overlap >= 80%)..." — ONE bench, not a separate ad-hoc script, scored against BOTH corpora:

- the synthetic truth-by-construction corpus (`bench/linear/corpus/*.json` + `bench/linear/synthetic/*.pdf`, WP1.6's own generator) — reported, not gated (see Finding 5 on why);
- the real hand-traced goldens, split by each file's own `tier` field (`opentakeoff-corpus/ground_truth/linear/*.json`, WP1.7 + WP3.8's "ground truth v2"; the split itself is frozen in `opentakeoff-corpus/reports/LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt`, per the plan's own explicit HELD-OUT TIER sheet list): `tier:"development"` — gated; `tier:"held_out"` — reported only (see Run 4 on why not gated yet).

| metric | question | where it lives |
|---|---|---|
| **run recall** | seeded from a point ON the golden run (not an endpoint), does `trace_run`'s own path match the golden's SHAPE over the golden's own span — discrete Fréchet < 2pt AND length overlap ≥ 80%, the plan's own literal criterion? | `score.ts`'s `scoreTraceRecall`/`scoreTraceShapeMatch` |
| **precision** | of the length actually WALKED, what fraction is real (length-weighted `min(golden,traced)/traced` across cases)? A trace that wanders onto unrelated linework or over-traces dilutes this. | `score.ts`'s `scoreTracePrecision` |
| **length error / over-trace** | `\|traced−golden\|/golden`; over-trace is the same delta counted only when traced > golden | `score.ts`'s `TraceRunRow` |
| **size accuracy** | the traced `size` at the seed vs. the golden's own `size_overrides` for the segment the seed sits on, length-weighted — split into a MATCH, a WRONG label (traced something, just not the golden's value — the worse failure) and NO label (`trace_run` correctly declined to guess), per the plan's own explicit spec ("no-label vs wrong-label separated") | `score.ts`'s `aggregateTrace` (`sizeAccuracyPct`/`sizeWrongLabelPct`/`sizeNoLabelPct`) |
| **build / query ms** | wall-clock around the FIRST `trace_run` call per sheet (index build + first query) vs. every subsequent call (warm query only) | `bench/linear.mts`'s own `traceOneRun` |
| **refusal correctness** | seeded from a point KNOWN (independently of `trace_run`) to sit on non-linework (title block text, a room label, blank margin) — does `trace_run` correctly refuse rather than confidently walk something? `opentakeoff-corpus/ground_truth/linear/refusals.json`, a labeled NEGATIVE corpus, gated at 100% (see Run 3) | `score.ts`'s `scoreRefusalCorrectness` |

Recall's Fréchet/overlap comparison **clips the traced polyline to the golden's own arc-length span first** (each golden endpoint projected onto the trace) — over-trace beyond that span is invisible to recall BY DESIGN, exactly so an otherwise-perfect shape match isn't penalized twice for the same failure over-trace already measures separately (mirrors `mep-trace-eval.mjs`'s own "reach accuracy, refusal correctness and false-confident rate scored apart from each other on purpose" doctrine).

```
cd opentakeoff/web && npm run bench:linear
```

## What this does NOT score, and why

- **Precision against an EXHAUSTIVE negative set.** `scoreTracePrecision` measures wasted walked length on the CASES THIS BENCH ALREADY RUNS (the crossing-run synthetic case is exactly this: does the walker wander onto the crossing line?) — it is not the same as a labeled `expect_status:refused` corpus the way `mep-trace-eval.mjs`'s `.mep.csv` key carries. `refusals.json` (Run 3) closes the first slice of this gap (4 unambiguous non-linework seeds); it does NOT cover a seed ON real linework of an EXCLUDED family (schedule gridlines, dimension lines, hatching) — that "no stroke family" refusal path specifically is still untested, see that corpus's own `scope` field.
- **Held-out tier at scale.** Run 4 authored the FIRST held-out golden (n=1) — enough to report a held-out number, not yet enough for "within 5 points of development" to mean anything (see that run's own writeup on why it isn't gated).
- **Click-to-proposal < 16ms as a gate.** `buildMs`/`queryMs` report Node-process `trace_run` timing, not the browser worker's own steady-state claim — treat the number as a sanity check, not the gate's own evidence.

## Run 1 (2026-09-17, first run)

**2/7 golden runs reach at all (28.6% recall)** — well under GATE 3's ≥85% target. Every miss traces to one of TWO limitations this project already knew about and disclosed before this eval script existed; this run found zero NEW failure modes.

```
run                                    status     LF gold→traced   len err   size
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
bessemer-m101-unit103-supply-trunk     refused    No routed linework under the cursor
bessemer-m101-unit102-supply-trunk     refused    No routed linework under the cursor
bessemer-p101-cw-main                  reached    18.96→1.63        91.4%   pipe:1 != null
bessemer-p101-san-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor
federal-m3.1-chws-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor
federal-m3.1-chwr-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor
itd-p5-hc3-branch                      reached    7.22→7.78          7.8%   pipe:1.25 != round:1.25
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
run recall        2/7 reached (28.6%)
length error      max 91.4%, mean 49.6%
over-trace        1 case over golden length, worst 7.8%
size accuracy     0.0% length-weighted (2 runs with a golden size)
build+first-query 306ms, 397ms (per distinct sheet, cold)
```

### Finding 1 — wall-vouch false-positive exclusion (5 of 7 misses)

Confirmed by direct segment-index inspection, not assumed: `bessemer-m101`'s both supply trunks, `bessemer-p101`'s SAN riser, and `federal-m3.1`'s CHWS/CHWR risers all sit on segments with `candidate:0, family:-1` in `ensureLinearIndex`'s own output — excluded before stroke classification ever runs. This is the SAME limitation WP3.4 already documented: `wallnetwork.ts`'s geometric wall-vouching false-positives on long, dead-straight duct/pipe runs when a sheet's own layer signal isn't strong (every one of these five is a 40–730px arrow-straight run). Per the goal document's own "what you never touch" list, `wallnetwork.ts`/`mepconnectivity.ts` internals are off-limits — this is an inherited, accepted limitation to work around when tracing (as WP3.7's own live-verification already did, deliberately clicking a DIFFERENT real segment), not something this checkpoint attempted to fix.

### Finding 2 — no same-family dash-gap continuation in `walk.ts` (1 of 7 misses) — FIXED, see Run 2

`bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s golden seed lands exactly on a real, correctly-classified candidate segment (family 6, `pen-weight-prior` evidence) — the walker DOES find real linework here, unlike Finding 1. But this CW main is drawn as a dash-dot line: many short (3–26px) individual dash segments with small gaps between them, not one continuous stroke. `walkOneDirection` stopped `dead_end` after only 58.56px (three touching/near-touching dashes), because the next dash sits ~21px away — outside whatever collinear-continuation tolerance `walk.ts` currently applies. This is NOT the same mechanism as `mepconnectivity.ts`'s `bridgeDanglingGaps` (checked directly: that function requires a fitting/equipment symbol sitting IN the gap — "never bridged on proximity alone" — and would not fire here even if wired in, since a print-style dash gap has no fitting in it). Flagged as the single highest-leverage next fix, since unlike Finding 1 it is NOT on the "never touch" list. Fixed the same day — see Run 2 below.

### Finding 3 — pipe vs. round-duct diameter ambiguity (surfaced, not a recall failure)

`itd-p5-hc3-branch` reached correctly (7.8% over-trace, a real geometric extension past the golden's original arbitrary sub-span — see that golden's own `review_basis` for the same-day fix) but read its own size as `round:1.25` (round duct, 1.25″ diameter) instead of the golden's `pipe:1.25` (1¼″ nominal pipe). Both notations share the same `⌀`/`ø` glyph; `sizes.ts`'s grammar does not disambiguate by sheet content (an all-piping hydronic sheet vs. an all-ductwork one). Not fixed here — a real, disclosed gap for size-accuracy work, not a `walk.ts` or wall-vouch issue.

### A ground-truth-authoring lesson Run 1 also surfaced

`itd-p5-hc3-branch`'s FIRST version (same day, before this eval script ran against it) traced only a 2.2 LF sub-span of a longer real segment — chosen for label-verification convenience, not because it was a real drawn stop point. Running the eval against it produced a 257% "over-trace" that was really just a golden that stopped short of where the geometry itself stops (the walker correctly found the FULL 196.6px straight run plus its elbow, exactly matching independent `extractVectorGeometry` forensics done before this golden was re-traced). Fixed by re-tracing to the run's own real dead-end-to-elbow extent. Lesson for future goldens: a run's endpoints must be real geometric features (dead end, fitting, family change) the walker could plausibly also find — not an interior point picked for narrative or crop-framing reasons — or length-error/over-trace numbers measured against it are not meaningful.

## The fix: `walk.ts`'s own dash-gap continuation (`bridgeDashGap`)

Built the same day as Run 1, in `web/src/lib/linear/walk.ts` (see that file's own header for the full design). Summary: when a walk dead-ends with nothing else welding at that point either, and the dead-ending segment is short (≤1 ft — real drawn duct/pipe RUNS between meaningful features essentially never are, so an isolated short stroke there is almost always a print-artifact fragment, not a real run), it searches for the nearest same-family segment endpoint within 0.5 ft whose approach direction AND own onward direction both continue straight through (±10°), and if found, jumps the gap and continues the walk. New `WalkResult.dashBridges` field; a new `bridged_dash_gap(N)` confidence factor in `receipt.ts` (confidence 0.8, following the same "minimum over named factors" doctrine every other factor here uses — not a per-bridge compounding penalty).

**Checked directly against the real P101 case before assuming the trigger should be the `dash` flag**: it isn't. `ensureGeometry`'s own `dash` array reads `0` on every one of the CW main's individual dash marks — this PDF's CAD export flattened the dash-dot linetype into many separate SOLID short strokes rather than using a native PDF dash-array stroke. Segment LENGTH, not the `dash` flag, is what actually distinguishes a dash-mark fragment from a real run in this corpus (the flag is OR'd in as a second, real-PDF-dash-array path, but length is what fires on the one real case that motivated this).

7 new tests in `test/linear/walk.test.ts` (bridged/not-bridged/too-wide/off-axis/wrong-family/real-elbow-unaffected/both-directions-summed, plus the exact `dash:0`-but-short shape that matches the real P101 case) and 2 in `test/linear/receipt.test.ts` (factor + confidence, and "no bridge means no factor at all").

```

bessemer-p101-cw-main                  reached    18.96→35.02       84.7%   pipe:1 != pipe:0.75   (Finding 4, new)
bessemer-p101-san-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor      (Finding 1, unchanged)
federal-m3.1-chws-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor      (Finding 1, unchanged)
federal-m3.1-chwr-riser                refused    No routed linework under the cursor      (Finding 1, unchanged)
itd-p5-hc3-branch                      reached    7.22→7.78          7.8%   pipe:1.25 != round:1.25   (Finding 3, unchanged)

Recall is unchanged (Finding 1's five wall-vouch exclusions are a completely separate mechanism the dash-gap fix was never going to touch), but `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own reading changed completely — worse-looking on paper (84.7% vs 91.4% is actually a smaller raw error, but the SIGN flipped from under-trace to over-trace, and the size mismatch is now against a DIFFERENT real label). This is Finding 4, and it's a real discovery, not a regression in the fix.

### Finding 4 — the CW main golden is a representative SPAN of a much longer, heavily-branched trunk, not an isolated run

Before the dash-gap fix, `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own seed died on its first dash fragment (58.56px), which accidentally hid this. With bridging working, the SAME seed now walks the real trunk in both directions — roughly 35 LF each way — passing FIVE real `tee` branch points before finally stopping `ambiguous` at both ends (not `dead_end`, not a size-change point). The golden's own 18.96 LF, 2-segment extent (chosen by the original WP1.7 pass because it's where "the main turns 90° and drops into a vertical branch riser") is a real, correctly hand-traced, meaningful SPAN — genuinely useful for `bench/linear.mts`'s own manual-mode parity/totals purposes, and for a human tracing this exact region — but it is NOT a fair single-`trace_run`-call comparison target: the human's stop point is a judgment call about which branch matters, not a hard geometric feature the walker could independently rediscover without a human/agent choosing a specific candidate at an `ambiguous` fork, exactly the plan's own §6.3 "offer the candidate fan, not block on it" design intent. The golden's own `review_basis` now documents this finding directly. Unlike Findings 1–3, this isn't a gap in the ENGINE at all — it's a gap in what "seed a golden's own centerline and compare total length" can honestly measure once a trunk is this densely branched.

## Run 3 (2026-09-17, same day) — migrated into `bench/linear.mts`, real Fréchet/overlap scoring

Migrating the ad-hoc script's logic into `bench/linear.mts` (per "The ruler," above) surfaced two more real, previously-undiscovered issues before it ever produced a trustworthy number — caught by direct inspection, not assumed:

### A coordinate bug in the SCORER itself, not the engine

The first `scoreTraceShapeMatch` call (on `itd-p5-hc3-branch`, a case Run 2 already knew reaches cleanly) reported `lengthOverlapPct: 0` and a `clippedLenFt`/`goldenLenFt` of **5264 feet** for a ~7ft run. Root cause: `upp` (feet-per-pixel) was passed to `scoreTraceShapeMatch` INVERTED (`1/upp`, i.e. pixels-per-foot) at both call sites. Fixed by passing `session.sheet(sheetKey).upp` directly. Caught before trusting a single aggregate number from the new scorer — the same discipline this whole file's own history depends on.

### Finding 5 — the synthetic corpus's own coordinate convention needed a real conversion, and its random paths confound wall-vouch

Seeding the SYNTHETIC corpus needs REAL absolute pixel positions (unlike the pre-existing manual-mode loop, which only ever compares relative distances and never needed to know where the drawn ink actually sits). The naive `x_ft * ptPerFt` conversion the manual-mode loop uses is NOT the real pixel position: `bench/linear/synthesize.mts`'s own `toPdf` adds an 80pt drawing margin AND places content in PDF's native bottom-up Y axis, which pdf.js's viewport then flips on render. Fixed with a `syntheticFtToPx` helper mirroring `toPdf` exactly (margin duplicated rather than imported — `synthesize.mts` has top-level side effects, so importing it would regenerate fixtures on every bench run). Confirmed the fix is real, not another guess: case `10-arc-as-polyline` went from a wildly wrong seed to a real, in-bounds trace once corrected.

With seeding now genuinely correct, 9 of the synthetic corpus's own 10 cases STILL refuse, and direct segment-index inspection confirms the SAME Finding 1 mechanism (`candidate:0, family:-1`) — this IS wall-vouch, on synthetic data, not a new engine bug. First hypothesis, TESTED AND DISPROVEN rather than left as a guess: that the random walk's own paths frequently trace near-closed rectangular loops (two roughly-parallel, span-overlapping legs on each axis), the same shape wall-vouching exists to catch on real sheets. Built `looksLikeWallOutline` + a retry-until-clear wrapper in `synthesize.mts`, regenerated the whole corpus, re-ran the bench — recall stayed 0/10, and `02-pen-thick-solid`'s new (confirmed non-rectangular) path was excluded MORE aggressively than before (all 5 segments vs. 4 of 5). Reverted the change rather than leave disproven complexity in the generator.

The real cause looks more fundamental: these synthetic PDFs carry NO PDF layers at all (pdf-lib draws plain content streams, no OCGs), so `mepLayerSignal` reads as `"none"` for every one of them, and — with no surrounding architectural context at all (no walls, no rooms, nothing to contrast against) — `wallnetwork.ts`'s wall-vouch fallback appears to exclude essentially ANY sufficiently long, straight, axis-aligned segment on such a bare sheet, independent of the overall path's shape. A real fix likely needs the generator to emit an actual named OCG layer (e.g. something `classifyLayerName` would read as duct/pipe-like) so layer classification short-circuits wall-vouch entirely, the same way `ensureMepGraph`'s own fallback only fires "whenever the result isn't strong." That's real, separately-scoped work (pdf-lib's OCG support is low-level, not a one-line change) — not attempted here. Meanwhile, most of the synthetic corpus's own intended test dimensions (pen weight, label placement, crossings) stay confounded by Finding 1 rather than isolated; its trace numbers are reported for visibility but NOT gated, for exactly this reason.

```
run                                    status     LF gold→traced   len err   size
────────────────────────────── real ground truth (gated) ──────────────────────────
bessemer-m101-unit103-supply-trunk     refused    (Finding 1)
bessemer-m101-unit102-supply-trunk     refused    (Finding 1)
bessemer-p101-cw-main                  reached    18.96→35.02  84.7%  pipe:1!=pipe:0.75   (Finding 4 — fails recall's Fréchet/overlap too, correctly)
bessemer-p101-san-riser                refused    (Finding 1)
federal-m3.1-chws-riser                refused    (Finding 1)
federal-m3.1-chwr-riser                refused    (Finding 1)
itd-p5-hc3-branch                      reached    7.22→7.78     7.8%  pipe:1.25!=round:1.25   (Finding 3 — now a CONFIRMED recall hit)
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
recall 1/7 (0.143)   precision 0.612   maxLenErr 84.7%   sizeAccuracy 0%   coldBuild 442ms

────────────────────────────── synthetic corpus (reported only) ───────────────────
9/10 refused — Finding 5 (wall-vouch, likely no-layer-signal + no context, not path shape)
10-arc-as-polyline reached, but 61% length error — a real, separate finding
   about arc-chord seeding worth its own follow-up, not chased further here
recall 0/10 (confounded — see Finding 5)   precision 1.0   coldBuild ≤4ms
```

Recall itself is now MORE HONESTLY measured (Fréchet/overlap instead of a loose "did it merely reach" check) — this is why `bessemer-p101-cw-main` now correctly reads as a recall MISS (Finding 4's own shape genuinely diverges from the golden's, not just runs long) while `itd-p5-hc3-branch` is confirmed a clean HIT. **CORRECTED by Run 6, same day**: `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own `frechetPx` at the time this paragraph was written was inflated by the SAME vertex-density scorer bug Run 6 found and fixed — the corrected value is 3.17px, not the much larger number this measurement originally carried. "Genuinely diverges" overstated it: the within-span shape match is actually quite close (barely over the 2px gate), and the real, still-true part of Finding 4 is narrower than first framed — see Run 6's own closing paragraph for the corrected picture. `TRACE_THRESHOLDS` in `bench/linear.mts` is set to today's own measured real-corpus floor (recall≥0.1, precision≥0.5) — ratcheted from measurement, not chosen for comfort, and NOT GATE 3's own targets (see that constant's own header).

### Refusal correctness — closing the labeled-negative-corpus gap this file's own "what this does not score" section named

`scoreTracePrecision` only ever sees seeds ON a real golden run — it can never catch a seed that should refuse outright but instead confidently (wrongly) traces something. `opentakeoff-corpus/ground_truth/linear/refusals.json` is a small, hand-curated negative corpus (4 cases across the same three real PDFs the other goldens use) chosen to be UNAMBIGUOUSLY non-linework — title block text, a room-label text run, a scale callout, and blank page margin — so there is no judgment call about whether a stroke "counts," only whether `trace_run` correctly refuses. Each seed was independently verified (direct `session.traceRun()` calls, before being written into the corpus) to actually throw. Wired into `bench/linear.mts` as a third scored pass (`scoreRefusalCorrectness`), reported and gated at 100% — unlike `TRACE_THRESHOLDS`'s other ratchet-point values, this one IS the real target, not a measured floor, since every case is unambiguous by construction.

```
── refusal correctness (negative corpus) ──
bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3 — title block text            OK
itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#5 — room-label text run               OK
federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6 — scale callout text       OK
bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3 — blank page margin            OK
aggregate (refusal): { correct: 4, total: 4, rate: 1, misses: [] }
```

One real bug caught wiring this in: the existing real-goldens loop enumerates every `*.json` file in `ground_truth/linear/` and parses each as a `RealGolden` — `refusals.json` (a different schema) landed in that same directory and was silently picked up, producing `source_pdf: undefined` and a hard crash rather than a skip. Fixed by excluding it by name in that loop's own filter, not by relying on shape-sniffing.

Not covered by this corpus: a seed ON real linework of an EXCLUDED family (schedule table gridlines, dimension lines, hatching), which would test the "no stroke family" refusal path specifically rather than "no linework at all" — disclosed in the corpus's own `scope` field as real follow-up work, not attempted here.

## Run 4 (2026-09-17, same day) — the first held-out golden, plus a second development-tier hit

Two goldens added this pass, on two DIFFERENT real PDFs never used to author any prior `ground_truth/linear/*.json` or tune the engine:

**A correction caught before it shipped.** A candidate search picked `weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#7` as a held-out candidate — genuinely unused before this pass, but the goal document's own explicit HELD-OUT TIER section pins that exact sheet ("weld-county p7") to the DEVELOPMENT tier by name. The plan's own pre-declared list is authoritative over a search that didn't know to check it first; retagged `development` before commit (see `weld-county-m1-0.json`'s own `review_basis` for the full correction). Its content stood regardless of tier: a 16" round supply-air duct riser, from a real reducer (14"→16") to a real tee into an 18" main, both ends independently confirmed via a marked render crop. Reached cleanly: **LF 21.82→21.82 exact, size OK** — the SECOND confirmed development-tier recall hit (after `itd-p5-hc3-branch`), moving development recall from 1/7 (0.143) to **2/8 (0.25)** and precision from 0.612 to **0.743**. Notably this sheet carries real named PDF OCG layers (`M-HVAC-DUCT` etc.) — `stroke-family:layer-name`, not a wall-vouch fallback — a different, cleaner code path than most of the existing development goldens.

**The actual first held-out golden**, chosen correctly this time: `opentakeoff-corpus/reports/LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt` freezes the plan's own 7-sheet held-out list (5 pinned by the plan verbatim; the 2 `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf` sheets are this checkpoint's own one-time pick, made STRUCTURALLY — first sheet in each of the PDF's two real plan series — with zero `trace_run` probing beforehand, so the pick itself carries no engine-behavior bias). Authored one golden off that list: `federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#7` (M4.1, a mechanical-room enlarged piping/duct plan), a 2½" HHWS pipe stub from a real tee off pump HWP-1's discharge riser to a real junction with the vertical header bundle feeding the boilers/chiller. Found via a grid-sweep of `trace_run` seeds (the label's own text position sat just off the actual drawn line), confirmed via a marked render crop showing both ends on genuine CAD geometry, and — unlike weld-county — with NO named OCG layer on this sheet at all (`stroke-family:pen-weight-prior`, `layer-unclassified`): a real Finding-1-adjacent code path, not the cleaner layer-name one.

```
── trace engine (real ground truth, held-out tier) ──
federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#7   reached LF 3.57→3.57 size OK
aggregate (held-out trace): recall 1.0   precision 1.0   maxLenErr 0%   sizeAccuracy 100%
```

A clean first result — but n=1, and this bench's own per-case recall criterion is binary (Fréchet<2pt AND overlap≥80%, pass or fail per case), so a one-case tier can only ever read 0% or 100%. `bench/linear.mts` reports this tier fully but does NOT hard-gate CI on the plan's own "within 5 points of development" rule yet — at this sample size that gate would just be gating on which single case got authored, not on whether the engine generalizes (see the code's own comment above `heldOutTraceAgg`). The other 6 declared-but-unauthored held-out sheets (`itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#4`, `baker-county-eoc-bidset.pdf#38`, `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14`, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#6`/`#18`) remain real, disclosed follow-up work — enough cases for the "within 5 points" gate to mean something is the actual bar, not one clean hit.

## Run 5 (2026-09-17, same day) — a second held-out golden, and an initially-misdiagnosed shape "miss"

Second entry off `reports/LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt`: `itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#4` (M1.1), an 8" round duct segment from a real wall-penetration dead end to a real elbow/damper assembly. Found the same way as Run 4's held-out golden (a `trace_run` seed grid-sweep), but this pass caught something the sweep's first hit would have missed if trusted blindly: a 3.11 LF candidate with BOTH stops reading `dead_end` looked ideal on paper, but its own `confidence:0` / `stroke-family:unclassified` factors were a flag, and a marked render crop confirmed it — the walk had followed a wall/shaft outline into a text-leader stub, not real ductwork at all. Rejected before authoring, not after; documented in the golden's own `review_basis` as a caught mistrace rather than silently discarded. The SECOND candidate (3.02 LF, round:8) held up under the same visual check: a real double-line duct symbol, dead-ending at a wall penetration on one side and meeting a real elbow/damper on the other.

This run initially scored a recall MISS: `lenErrPct: 0` (LF matched exactly, 3.02→3.02) and `lengthOverlapPct: 1` (100%) but `shape.frechetPx: 9.2` against the plan's own `<2pt` criterion. First write-up of this checkpoint guessed the cause was "real shape noise from the double-line duct's own edge-following near the elbow/damper transition" — that guess was WRONG, and Run 6 (immediately below) found and fixed the actual cause the same day, before this note was ever left standing as the final word. See Run 6 for what was actually going on and the real, corrected held-out numbers.

## Run 6 (2026-09-17, same day) — the sub-2pt "miss" was a scorer bug, not engine noise

Run 5's own guess ("double-line duct edge-following") was never verified against the actual traced points before being written down — a lapse against this project's own repeatedly-stated discipline (verify before trusting, the same rule that caught Run 3's two coordinate bugs and disproved Finding 5's first hypothesis). Caught immediately after, this same checkpoint: dumping the FULL-PRECISION `trace_run` points for `itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#4` showed every single point sits at EXACTLY `y=860.4` — a perfectly straight line, not a zigzag at all. Reproducing `scoreTraceShapeMatch` directly against `golden=[[1554.2,860.4],[1635.8,860.4]]` and `traced=[[1554.2,860.4],[1563.4,860.4],[1626.7,860.4],[1631.3,860.4],[1635.8,860.4]]` (both exactly collinear) still returned `frechetPx: 9.2` — proving the bug lives in the SCORER, not the engine.

Root cause: **discrete Fréchet distance is a per-VERTEX metric, not a per-CURVE one.** It requires a monotone index-correspondence between the two point sequences; when one curve (the golden, 2 vertices) is far sparser than the other (the traced polyline, 5 vertices, all genuinely on the same line but at uneven spacing from a fitting symbol's own tiny kinks), the DP's own monotone-advance constraint has to walk through every extra vertex on the denser side before it can advance the single step on the sparser one — and the worst intermediate gap along that forced walk becomes the reported "distance," even though the underlying GEOMETRIC line is identical. This is a well-known pitfall of the discrete (as opposed to continuous) Fréchet formulation, and it means ANY golden authored with few vertices, compared against a `trace_run` polyline that happens to pick up extra near-collinear vertices along the same real line (a fitting symbol, a snapped-grid rounding step), can read a large fake "shape mismatch" — a scoring artifact that would only get MORE common as the bench grows, not less.

Fix: `score.ts` gained `simplifyPolyline` (iterative Douglas-Peucker, same "no recursion" discipline as `discreteFrechet` itself), applied to BOTH the golden and the clipped/reversed traced polyline in `scoreTraceShapeMatch` before the Fréchet call, at a fixed `0.5px` tolerance — well under the recall gate's own 2px threshold, so a genuine elbow (a real perpendicular deviation an engineer would call a corner) is never mistaken for noise and simplified away. 6 new tests in `test/benchScore.test.ts`: collinear points drop, a real corner survives, sub-tolerance jitter drops, `<3`-point input is a no-op, the EXACT `itd-p4-ea-duct-stub` reproduction now scores `<2px`, and — the test that actually matters here — a genuine mid-span detour (an out-and-back real shape mismatch) still reads a large Fréchet distance after simplification, proving the fix doesn't just loosen the gate to hide real misses.

```
── trace engine (real ground truth, held-out tier) ──
federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#7   reached LF 3.57→3.57 size OK
itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#4            reached LF 3.02→3.02 size OK
aggregate (held-out trace): recall 1.0   precision 1.0   maxLenErr 0%   sizeAccuracy 100%
```

Held-out is now a genuine, correct 2/2. Development-tier's own AGGREGATE recall/precision are unchanged (still 2/8 and 0.743 — this artifact only ever moves a MISS's frechetPx number, and every development case that was already a miss is still a miss, already a hit is still a hit), but diffing every row's own `frechetPx` before vs. after (not just trusting the aggregate, the same lapse Run 5 already made once this same day) turned up a real surprise: `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own `frechetPx` dropped from **260.5px to 3.17px** — meaning MOST of Finding 4's own originally-reported "shape genuinely diverges" number was this exact same scorer artifact, not real geometric divergence, even though that case's own conclusion (a recall miss, real over-trace past the golden's own span) happens to still hold at the corrected, much smaller 3.17px. Corrected in "Run 3"'s own writeup above rather than left standing. `itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#5`'s frechetPx moved by floating-point noise only (0.6270→0.6266, both already well under the 2px gate). `itd-d1-lab-m1-1-heldout.json`'s own `review_basis` was left as originally written (it correctly describes the golden's OWN authoring and verification, which was never wrong) — the correction lives here and in `PROGRESS.md`, where the wrong guesses were actually made.

## Run 8 (2026-09-17, same day) — resolving `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s residual 3.17px

Run 6 left an open question: is the corrected 3.17px frechetPx genuine or one more instance of vertex-density noise `simplifyPolyline`'s own tolerance didn't fully absorb? Diagnosed directly: dumped the golden's own 3-vertex span (simplifies to 2, since all 3 are collinear) alongside the clipped/simplified traced polyline for the exact same case. The golden's LEFT endpoint (`x=2659.854623014566`, a real geometric point — the 1¼" water-service riser drop) matches the traced clip's own left endpoint to full floating-point precision, `0px` apart. All of the 3.17px lives at the RIGHT endpoint: golden `x=3342.47, y=1385.59` vs. the traced clip's own `x=3339.4, y=1384.8` — about 3px apart, decomposing to `frechetPx` almost exactly (`√(3.07² + 0.79²) ≈ 3.17`).

This golden's own `review_basis` already documents that its right endpoint is NOT a hard geometric feature — it's "the point the main turns 90° and drops into a riser," the human annotator's own judgment call on a much longer real trunk (Finding 4). A few-pixel discrepancy landing exactly at a self-disclosed soft stop, and nowhere else along the span, is the ordinary noise level of hand-tracing near a judgment call, not a shape defect or a remaining scorer artifact — `simplifyPolyline`'s fix is doing its job correctly here; there's nothing further to chase. No code change; this closes the open question Run 6 left rather than finding a new bug.

## Run 9 (2026-09-17, same day) — size accuracy's own "no-label vs wrong-label" split

The plan's own §2 metric spec asks for size accuracy "exact + length-weighted, no-label vs wrong-label separated" — `sizeAccuracyPct` alone only ever answered "did it match," collapsing a WRONG guess (`trace_run` bound a real size, just not the golden's own value) and NO guess (`trace_run` correctly declined — the UI's own honest "size unknown, verify manually" state) into the same "not a match" bucket. These are very different failures: a wrong guess is silently misleading; no guess is the tool being honest about its own limits. `aggregateTrace` gained `sizeWrongLabelPct` and `sizeNoLabelPct` (both length-weighted over the same `sized` population `sizeAccuracyPct` uses; the three sum to 1 whenever `sizeAccuracyPct` is non-null). 6 new/extended tests in `test/benchScore.test.ts`, including one built specifically to prove a wrong-label case and a no-label case land in different buckets rather than both being lumped together.

Running the split against the real corpus surfaced something worth flagging, not just a metric upgrade for its own sake: **development-tier's own size misses are 100% wrong-label, 0% no-label** (`sizeWrongLabelPct: 0.545`, `sizeNoLabelPct: 0`) — every real case where `trace_run`'s traced size doesn't match the golden is a CONFIDENT wrong guess (Finding 3's `round:1.25`-vs-`pipe:1.25` grammar ambiguity, and `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s real over-trace into a different actual pipe size), never an honest "I don't know." The ONE case in the whole bench that DOES show a clean no-label result is the synthetic corpus's own `10-arc-as-polyline` (`sizeNoLabelPct: 1` there). This is a small sample (n=2 wrong, n=1 no-label) — not enough to claim `trace_run`'s "decline to guess" path is broken or rare, only that it's under-exercised by the real corpus so far. Worth watching as more real goldens get authored, not something to chase further on this one measurement alone.

## Run 10 (2026-09-17, same day) — Finding 5's real fix: a named OCG layer, synthetic recall 0/10 → 7/10

Finding 5's own corrected hypothesis (Run 3) named the real cause of the synthetic corpus's own near-total refusal rate: these PDFs carried NO PDF Optional Content Groups at all (`pdf-lib` draws plain content streams), so `mepLayerSignal` read `"none"` on every one of them, and with zero surrounding architectural context, `wallnetwork.ts`'s wall-vouch fallback excluded almost any sufficiently long, straight, axis-aligned segment — confounding most of what this corpus was built to test (pen weight, dash, label placement, crossings). The likely real fix was named at the time but not attempted ("pdf-lib's OCG support is low-level, not a one-line change"). Attempted and landed this pass.

`pdf-lib` has no built-in OCG helper — `PDFPage` only exposes low-level `pushOperators`. Built one from the primitives it does expose (`doc.context.obj`/`.register`, `page.node.Resources()`, `PDFOperator.of(PDFOperatorNames.BeginMarkedContentSequence, ...)`): a real `/OCProperties` catalog entry, a `/Properties` resource on the page, and `BDC /OC ... EMC` wrapping each case's own draw calls. Prototyped standalone FIRST, against a throwaway 2-line test PDF, before touching `synthesize.mts` itself — caught a real bug in the prototype's own first attempt: `context.obj({Name: "M-HVAC-DUCT"})` coerces a plain JS string to a `PDFName`, not the PDF STRING type an OCG dictionary's `/Name` entry requires per spec, and pdf.js's own `getOptionalContentConfig()` silently reads that back as an EMPTY name — confirmed directly (a `sheetInfo().layers` call showed a real, present, but blank-named layer) before finding the fix (`PDFString.of(name)`, not a bare string). Re-verified after the fix: the SAME test PDF's tagged line reads `stroke-family:layer-name` / `systems:["ductwork"]`; an untagged control line on the same page reads the ordinary `stroke-family:unclassified` fallback — direct proof the technique works before spending it on the real generator.

Wired into `synthesize.mts`: each case's own `system` (SA/RA/EA/OA/MA → `"M-HVAC-DUCT"`, else → `"M-PIPE-HYDRONIC"`) picks a layer name carrying a token `mepsystems.ts`'s own `DUCTWORK`/`PIPING` sets recognize outright, wrapping that case's entire `build()` call (linework + its own label/leader — harmless, since `layerOf` is indexed by stroked/filled path segment, not by text-show operators, so a label drawn under the same tag never enters that array at all).

```
── trace engine (synthetic corpus) ──
01-pen-thin-solid            reached  LF 50.54→50.54  size rect:12x6!=null
02-pen-thick-solid           reached  LF 61.5→61.5    size rect:16x8!=null
03-dashed-existing           reached  LF 36.74→36.74  size round:10!=null
04-dashdot-demo              reached  LF 46.11→46.11  size pipe:2!=null
05-double-line-duct          refused  (see below — a different, pre-existing gap)
06-label-inside              reached  LF 27.81→27.81  size rect:14x8==rect:14x8 (exact)
07-label-beside              reached  LF 50.61→50.61  size round:8!=null
08-label-leader              reached  LF 33.48→26.85  size pipe:1.5!=null  (NEW finding, not yet chased)
09-crossing-run              reached  LF 36.13→36.13  size rect:10x6!=null
10-arc-as-polyline           reached  LF 13.41→5.18   size pipe:3!=null   (Run 3's own already-disclosed arc-chord finding)
recall 7/10 (0.7)   precision 1.0   sizeAccuracyPct 0.078   sizeNoLabelPct 0.922   sizeWrongLabelPct 0
```

Recall went from **0/10 to 7/10** in one change — this corpus can finally test what it was built to test, exactly Finding 5's own predicted outcome. `sizeNoLabelPct: 0.922` (vs. `sizeWrongLabelPct: 0`) is itself a good sign: on the cases that reach, the engine mostly declines to guess a size rather than guessing wrong — consistent, not contradictory, with Run 9's real-corpus finding that wrong guesses cluster on the REAL corpus specifically (a different population; not yet enough synthetic no-label data to say more).

Two things this pass surfaced and explicitly did NOT chase further, each disclosed rather than silently patched:
- **`05-double-line-duct` still refuses**, and this is NOT the same bug the OCG fix targets: `drawDoubleLine` draws only the two parallel OFFSET rails, never the CENTERLINE itself, while the golden's own truth (and `traceOneRun`'s own seed) is authored AT the centerline — there is genuinely no ink there to click on. The same shape this project's own real `weld-county-m1-0.json` golden hit and solved by picking a specific RAIL to trace, not the centerline; the synthetic generator's own seeding convention would need the same treatment. Investigated further in Run 11 — see below for why it's harder than "just pick a rail."
- **`08-label-leader`'s own new length mismatch** (33.48→26.85 LF, previously invisible behind a flat refusal) is a genuinely new data point this fix exposed. RESOLVED same day — see Run 11.

Measured: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bench:linear` passes (synthetic still reported-only, never gated, per Finding 5's own established reason — see "The ruler"); development/held-out tier numbers UNCHANGED (this pass touches only the synthetic generator); all 215 `benchScore.test.ts`/`test/linear/*.test.ts` tests pass (no scoring-function code changed, only the synthetic PDF generator).

## Run 11 (2026-09-17, same day) — `08-label-leader` fixed; `05-double-line-duct` understood more precisely, not yet fixed

**`08-label-leader`'s own new length mismatch, root-caused and fixed.** The leader line takes off from the SAME vertex the golden's own path turns a real 90° corner at (`mid = pts[Math.floor(pts.length/2)]`, which for this case's own 4-point path IS the corner). First attempt: leave the leader line OUTSIDE the duct's own OCG tag (untagged), reasoning that an untagged stroke wouldn't be mistaken for duct linework. That did NOT work — re-diagnosed rather than assumed fixed: `walk.ts`'s own `sameFamilyContinuity` only excludes a candidate on a CONFIRMED layer mismatch (`lFrom >= 0 && lTo >= 0 && lFrom !== lTo`); an UNTAGGED segment (`layerOf === -1`) reads as compatible BY DEFAULT, not as a confirmed non-match — so the leader still counted as "same family" as the duct and the walk still stopped `ambiguous` at that corner, completely unchanged from before. The real fix: give the leader its OWN distinct OCG (`"M-ANNO"`) rather than no OCG at all — `registerOcgLayer` extended to MERGE additional OCGs onto the same page (a real `/OCProperties`/`/Resources/Properties` array append, not a second document) rather than assume one-OCG-per-page. Re-diagnosed after the fix: the walk now reaches the golden's full 4-point path, both ends `dead_end`, no `ambiguous` — confirmed via the same direct point-dump/re-trace method this project uses throughout rather than trusting the aggregate number alone. Synthetic recall: **7/10 → 8/10**.

**`05-double-line-duct` investigated further, not fixed.** The obvious fix ("trace whichever rail has real seedable ink, the way `weld-county-m1-0.json` did") turns out to be genuinely harder for this specific case than for a real single-turn CAD duct: this case's own truth path has FOUR segments with alternating turn directions (a zigzag, not one 90° bend), and `drawDoubleLine` offsets each segment's own two rails INDEPENDENTLY per segment, with no mitering at corners — meaning whichever rail is picked, some corners get a real ink OVERLAP (fine to trace through) and others a real ink GAP (the outer side of a turn), and which corners fall which way flips with each turn's own direction. There is no single "always-outer" or "always-inner" rail choice for a path that turns both ways. A real fix needs either: mitered/continuous rail drawing in the generator (closing each corner's own gap so a single rail is walkable end to end), or a per-segment seed/stitch convention in the bench's own scoring rather than the generator. Deferred, disclosed with this more precise understanding rather than the prior "just pick a rail" framing, which undersold the real difficulty.

Measured: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bench:linear` passes, synthetic recall now 8/10; all 215 tests still pass (no scoring-function code changed, only the synthetic generator).

## Run 12 (2026-09-17, same day) — `05-double-line-duct` reaches now; a deeper, real limit found, not a bug

Implemented the fix Run 11 scoped: `drawDoubleLine` rewritten to draw each rail as a properly MITERED continuous polyline (`offsetRailMitered` — the offset lines of two adjacent segments extended to their own real intersection at each interior vertex, a standard polygon-offset miter join) instead of independent per-segment offsets, closing every corner gap regardless of turn direction. `synthesize.mts`'s own `CaseSpec` gained an optional `seedPointFt(pts)` hook so a case can supply a real point on a rail (rather than the inkless centerline) for `trace_run` to seed on; `bench/linear.mts`'s `CaseTruth`/`traceOneRun` thread an optional `seed_point_ft` through the SAME `syntheticFtToPx` transform every other point already uses (not a second hand-rolled conversion — this project has already caught two real flip/margin bugs from exactly that kind of duplication this same day).

Result: **`05-double-line-duct` now REACHES** (both ends `dead_end`, no more refusal) — confirmed via the same direct point-dump method used throughout. But length comes back real and short: **43.59 → 37.59 LF (13.8% error)**, and recall still misses (`lengthOverlapPct: 0`, `frechetPx: 67.8`). Diagnosed rather than left as "still broken": this is NOT a bug in the mitering, the seeding, or the scorer — it is a genuine, previously-unrecognized geometric property this fix's own investigation surfaced. Offsetting a polyline at a 90° corner and mitering it is NOT length-preserving the way offsetting a single straight segment is: the INSIDE rail of every turn is shorter than the centerline by the offset distance at that corner, the OUTSIDE rail longer, by simple 90°-miter trig (trim/extension = the offset distance itself). This path's own four turns happened to put the traced rail (`side: 1`) on the inside at enough corners that the total comes up systematically short — real duct geometry, not an artifact (an actual sheet-metal duct's own inner and outer sheet edges genuinely ARE different lengths than centerline at a real elbow; a takeoff professional's own LF convention is centerline specifically because of this). A rail is also, unavoidably, offset ~1 ft (the half-width) from the centerline at every point along its length — comfortably beyond the recall scorer's own `overlapTolFt=0.5` default, so `lengthOverlapPct` reads 0% not because the shape is wrong, but because a rail and a centerline are never within tolerance of each other by construction, regardless of how well the rail itself is walked.

This means recall for a double-line case most likely CANNOT cleanly pass under the current centerline-vs-traced-rail scoring convention, independent of any further seeding or mitering work — a real, disclosed limit of what this specific hard case's own recall number can honestly measure, not a bug queued for a future pass. What DID land is real and worth keeping regardless: the case no longer refuses outright (Finding 1's own wall-vouch-bypass intent now extends to it too), the mitering fix is a genuine generator-quality improvement useful for any FUTURE double-line case (a continuous, gap-free rail is correct CAD-drafting behavior on its own merits), and the length/overlap failure mode is now precisely understood rather than mysterious.

Measured: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `bench:linear` passes, synthetic recall unchanged at 8/10 (this case still misses recall, for the reason above, not a regression); all 215 tests still pass (no scoring-function code changed, only the synthetic generator).

## Run 15 (2026-09-17, same day) — a genuine excluded-family refusal case, refusal correctness 4/4 → 5/5

Run 14's own "Honestly scoped as remaining" item (2) named a real gap in the refusal (negative) corpus: every case so far was a seed with NO ink of any kind at all (title block text, a room label, a scale callout, blank margin) — none tested a seed sitting ON real linework that belongs to an EXCLUDED family (an annotation symbol, a dimension line, a schedule-table gridline), which is a materially different refusal path through `trace_run`'s own logic than "there is nothing here to click."

Two candidates were pursued and one set aside, disclosed rather than silently dropped, before the case that landed. First, `weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#6`'s own DUCT SCHEDULE table: `extractVectorGeometry(ops, vp.transform, pdfjs.OPS)` — reading the page's own operator list directly rather than guessing a seed from nearby text, the same lesson this project's earlier mistrace incidents already taught — found real, confirmed gridline segments (e.g. `(957.68, 2644.64)`→`(2109.68, 2644.64)`). But this sheet has no detected drawing scale, and `bench/linear.mts`'s shared refusal loop unconditionally calls `session.setScale(c.sheet_id, { use_detected: true })` for every case, which THROWS on a scale-less sheet rather than producing a `refused` result — adding this case as-is would break the shared loop, not exercise it. Not worked around here: modifying shared refusal-loop code to special-case one sheet was judged the wrong trade for a single corpus entry.

Pivoted to `weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#7` (M1.0 — already scaled, already this project's development-tier sheet for the OTHER four goldens on this PDF). The same direct-vector-extraction method found short (5–40px) diagonal (30–60°) segments; `trace_run` was called against three candidate seeds near them and all three refused. A marked render crop (`ring` marks over the candidate coordinates) confirmed what the strokes actually were before authoring: not hatching, but the circular outline of a circled keynote/reference balloon symbol (a circled "2"). Added as the corpus's 5th case (`seed_norm: [0.5874, 0.1778]`, `weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#7`).

```
── refusal correctness (negative corpus) ──
bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3          (title block text)         refused OK
itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#5               (room-label text)          refused OK
federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6      (scale callout text)       refused OK
bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3          (blank margin, no ink)     refused OK
weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#7       (keynote balloon symbol)   refused OK
aggregate (refusal): { correct: 5, total: 5, rate: 1, misses: [] }
```

Refusal correctness: **4/4 → 5/5**, `minRefusalRate = 1.0` gate still holds, and the corpus now covers a genuinely different refusal path (real ink of an excluded family) alongside the original no-ink cases. Dimension lines and schedule-table gridlines remain untested, for the concrete reasons above (no dimension-string text exists on any of this project's own mechanical sheets — dimensions live on the architectural set, not sampled here — and the one confirmed schedule gridline sits on a scale-less sheet the shared refusal loop can't safely exercise as-is): real, disclosed follow-up work, not silently worked around.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes, refusal 5/5, development/held-out/synthetic trace numbers unchanged (this pass touches only `ground_truth/linear/refusals.json`, no engine or scorer code); all 215 `benchScore.test.ts`/`test/linear/*.test.ts` tests pass (no scoring-function code changed, only a ground-truth fixture).

## Run 16 (2026-09-17, same day) — a third Bessemer M101 golden (recall 2/8 → 3/9), and Finding 6: real false-confident mistraces on non-MEP linework

Finding 1's own five wall-vouch exclusions sit on `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6` (2) and `federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6` (2) plus one more — every long, straight, axis-dominant real duct/pipe run on these two unlayered sheets. Rather than accept those as permanently untestable (Finding 1 itself is out of scope — `wallnetwork.ts` internals are on the "never touch" list), this pass asked a narrower question: are there OTHER real duct/pipe runs on these same sheets that DON'T trip wall-vouch's own straight-network-of-walls signature (too short, or with no perpendicular partners to form a "network"), and so could still reach on a single `trace_run` call?

A full-page grid sweep of `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6` (`trace_run` at every 60px on an 87×58 grid) found 162 distinct reaching seeds — far more than expected, and a useful signal on its own: this sheet is dense enough that almost every non-vouched candidate lands somewhere. Most were interior spans of heavily-branched networks (`ambiguous` at both ends, the same "not a fair single-call target" category Finding 4 already named) — set aside without individual review, same as Finding 4's own trunk. A handful had BOTH ends `dead_end` — real single-call-friendly candidates — and each was checked before trusting it, not after:

- One (a two-point straight run from a circled "T" symbol down to a piece of drawn equipment labeled "EBB-1") looked like a clean real stub on the render crop alone. It was NOT authored: `mcp/scripts/graph-render.mjs`'s own ELECTRIC BASEBOARD HEATER SCHEDULE render (page 8) confirms EBB-1 is a QMARK electric baseboard heater (500W, 240V) — the "T" is a line-voltage thermostat, and the traced line is electrical control wiring, not duct or pipe routing at all. **This is a real, disclosed engine gap, not just a rejected candidate**: `trace_run` returned a confident `reached` result (blank size/system, `stroke-family:unclassified`) on a genuinely non-MEP stroke family. `strokeExclusionMask` has no notion of "this is electrical," only wall-vouch (a geometric wall-network shape test) and a small set of role/hatch/tag-box checks — none of which fire on an isolated diagonal-free electrical run. On an unlayered sheet with no other signal, the engine cannot currently tell a thermostat-to-heater control line from a real pipe stub. Not fixed here (a real heuristic to add — e.g. excluding known-electrical pen/dash signatures — is separate, uncertain follow-up work, not a quick patch); disclosed as **Finding 6** rather than silently worked around.
- A second (running along the top+right edge of a dashed "DW" dishwasher casework outline) was also rejected — a mistrace onto architectural/casework dashed linework, the same category of error Finding 6 names, just a different non-MEP source (furniture outline dashes rather than electrical wiring).
- A third (a lone horizontal line crossing between two rooms, no confirming label or distinguishing context found nearby) was set aside as genuinely unidentifiable rather than guessed.

One candidate held up under the same scrutiny and was authored: a 5.64 LF, dead-end-to-dead-end run that turned out to already be named — but deliberately excluded — in this exact sheet's own existing golden. `bessemer-m101.json`'s original `scope` field (WP1.7, GATE 1) explicitly calls out "the short 14x3½ stub serving Bedroom 2" as real, identified duct linework that was out of scope for that golden's own "supply mains" purpose (a branch stub, not a horizontal trunk). That is precisely this candidate: a T-off from the vertical 8″ø riser to a stop just short of the TG-1 transfer grille's own transition boot. Cross-checked against the DIFFUSER, GRILLE, REGISTER SCHEDULE render: TG-1's own NECK SIZE is listed as "14 x 6" — a DIFFERENT value than the "14x3½" callout near the traced stub, confirming "14x3½" is the duct run's own size (not a mislabeled copy of the register's neck size) and giving real, independent support for authoring it as `size_overrides`. `system: "TA"` (transfer air) is a plain descriptive label, not one of this corpus's usual SA/RA/EA/OA/MA tokens — a transfer grille moves air between two interior spaces, a real category this corpus's existing vocabulary has no slot for; confirmed harmless to leave honestly labeled since `run.system` on a real golden is documentation only, never read by any bench scoring code (checked directly in `bench/linear.mts`/`score.ts` before relying on that). Added as `bessemer-m101.json`'s third run (`bessemer-m101-bed2-transfer-stub`), not a new file — same sheet, same existing golden.

## Run 18 (2026-09-17, same day) — the same method tried on Bessemer P101; no clean candidate this time, four more Finding 6 instances

Tried the Run 16/17 playbook on the third remaining fully-or-partly-refused development sheet: `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3` (the SAN riser golden refuses; the CW main golden reaches but over-traces per Finding 4). A full-page grid sweep found only 5 both-`dead_end` candidates (fewer than either mechanical sheet's own sweep) — this dense domestic-plumbing sheet packs its real risers immediately against fixture symbols and rotated labels, which turned out to matter: every candidate checked here was a mistrace onto something OTHER than real pipe, a noticeably worse hit rate than Runs 16-17's one-real-candidate-per-several-false-positives.

- Two candidates traced along light-gray FIXTURE OUTLINE edges (a bathtub/sink alcove boundary in each case) — the same non-MEP-linework category Finding 6 already names, just from casework/fixture footprints rather than a dishwasher cabinet this time.
- One candidate's four points sat EXACTLY on the four corners of the "FD-1" (floor drain) tag/label's own drawn callout box — a plain rectangular text-frame outline, not linework at all. Notable on its own: `strokeExclusionMask` already runs a dedicated `classifyTagBoxSegs` check meant to exclude exactly this kind of frame, and this one wasn't caught by it. Root-caused, not left a mystery: `classifyTagBoxSegs` requires ONE subpath with `closed:true` and `i1 > i0` (multiple segments in a single drawn path) — direct inspection of this exact box's own `ensureGeometry().subpaths` shows its four sides are FOUR SEPARATE subpaths, each a single unconnected segment, each `closed:false` (`i0`/`i1` a single-index pair per side). This matches an already-known fact about this exact PDF (`mepsystems.ts`'s own `mepLayerSignal` doc comment already names `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf` as Ghostscript-flattened): flattening a CAD rectangle's original single closed path into four independent stroke operations is consistent with exactly this kind of loss. A real fix exists in principle (recognizing several open, mutually-perpendicular, endpoint-chained subpaths that together close a rectangle, not just one subpath that already says `closed:true`) but was NOT attempted here: `classifyTagBoxSegs` lives in `oneclick.ts`, a shared classifier the wider One-Click room/area flood-fill path also depends on (a second call site at that file's own line ~2024, independent of `strokes.ts`'s usage) — broadening its own closed-shape recognition risks a wider blast radius than this trace-engine-scoped investigation has budget to validate against. Disclosed with its real root cause, not attempted as a fix.
- The remaining two (a matched pair at different y-bands, same shape) ran along a rotated "2″ SAN UP/DN" text label's own edge and into an adjoining fixture-outline corner — ambiguous enough (running through text, not clearly a real pipe hidding behind it) to set aside unidentified rather than guessed either way.

No new golden authored here. This is the honest result of applying this checkpoint's own method to a third sheet, not a search-budget shortfall — logged per this project's own standing discipline (an honest "nothing here" is data, not a gap to paper over). Development-tier recall stays **4/10 (0.4)**, unchanged from Run 17. Finding 6 (Runs 16-17-18) now has SEVEN confirmed real instances across three sheets and at least four distinct non-MEP-linework sources: electrical control wiring, casework/fixture outlines (three separate instances now), a dashed reference-line series, a title-block rule line, a wall-face outline, and (new) a tag/label frame box that a dedicated exclusion check was supposed to catch but didn't.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes, all numbers unchanged from Run 17 (no ground-truth file was touched this pass — no code, no fixture, purely documentation of a negative result).

## Run 19 (2026-09-17, same day) — `bldg5406`'s own pathological hang, root-caused to `symbollabels.ts`'s leader-chase, not the index build

Run 14 confirmed `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`'s own `trace_run` call didn't finish in 60s, but didn't localize WHERE — it was framed as "whatever in this PDF's own vector data makes `trace_run`'s own index build pathological." That framing turns out to be WRONG in its specifics, corrected here rather than left standing: the index build is fast. Measured directly, stage by stage, by importing `strokes.ts`'s `classifyStrokes` and `index.ts`'s `buildSegmentIndex` and calling them on this exact sheet's own `ensureGeometry()` output (96,292 real segments — close to GATE 3's own "100k segs" scale target): `textSpans` 1ms, `mepLayerSignal` 0ms, `classifyStrokes` 146ms, `buildSegmentIndex` 72ms — 221ms total, comfortably under the 400ms gate. `walkBothDirections` itself, called directly against the freshly-built index at a real seed, returned in **1ms**. None of WP3.2/WP3.3/WP3.4's own build-or-walk path is the problem.

The actual hang lives AFTER the walk, in `session.ts`'s own `traceRun`: the per-span label-binding loop (`for (const sp of spans) { const leaderPoints = leaderTerminalPointsForLabel(sp, spans, geo.segs, geo.lum); ... }`, `session.ts:3846-3850`) calls into `web/src/lib/symbollabels.ts`'s `leaderTerminalPointsForLabel` for EVERY text span on the sheet, every single `trace_run` call (this loop is unconditional, not specific to trace mode — every sheet pays it). Timing that function directly against this sheet's own 165 real spans: the very FIRST call did not return within 60 seconds on its own. `leaderSheetContext` (which builds and caches a spatial dark-segment index, keyed by the `segs`/`lum` array references so it's built once per sheet, not once per span) is not the cost — the cost is inside `chase()`, the bounded-BFS leader-line search that walks outward from each label's own candidate start point, hop by hop (`LEADER_HOPS = 4`), gathering nearby "dark" (heavily-inked) segment endpoints via `nearDark()`'s own 3×3-grid-cell lookup at each step. `chase`'s own `seen` set does cap total work at roughly the sheet's own dark-segment count, but on a sheet dense/cluttered enough that `nearDark` returns many candidates per query, the BFS frontier can reach a large fraction of that count within just a few hops — and this happens fresh for potentially every one of a sheet's own spans (the per-token result is cached, but the BFS work computing it is not shared across different labels). Bldg5406's own geometry is evidently dense/cluttered enough (a "demo" HVAC sheet — real drawings this project's other real corpus PDFs don't exhibit, since none of them show this symptom despite comparable or larger segment counts) to make that per-label BFS itself the pathological cost, not anything in WP3's own trace-engine build/walk path.

Not fixed here: `chase`/`nearDark` are performance-sensitive, widely-shared label-association code (`symbollabels.ts` backs every symbol/label-leader association across the app, not just `trace_run`), and a real fix (e.g. capping total BFS work across the whole per-sheet loop, or making `nearDark`'s own dedup sub-linear) needs careful validation against the label-association test suite before landing — genuinely separate, scoped follow-up, not attempted in this trace-engine-focused pass. This DOES mean `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14` staying BLOCKED (Run 14) is the right call, and reframes WHY: not an index-build gate failure at all, but a pre-existing, sheet-density-triggered cost in shared label-leader code that trace_run's own unconditional per-call label-binding loop pays on every sheet, just usually cheaply.

Measured: no code changed, no ground truth touched — purely diagnostic, narrowing an already-disclosed blocker's own root cause rather than leaving it as a vague "index build" guess. `reports/LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt`'s own bldg5406 history note still correctly describes the SYMPTOM (a bare `session.traceRun()` call not completing in 60s); this run corrects and narrows the CAUSE.

## Run 20 (2026-09-17, same day) — the schedule-gridline refusal case, refusal correctness 5/5 → 6/6

Run 15's own disclosed gap named a schedule-table gridline as found but unusable: `weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#6`'s own DUCT INSULATION SCHEDULE has real, confirmed gridline geometry, but that sheet carries no detected drawing scale, and `bench/linear.mts`'s refusal loop unconditionally called `session.setScale(c.sheet_id, { use_detected: true })` for every case — which THROWS (not refuses) on a scale-less sheet, so adding the case as-is would have broken the loop rather than tested it.

Fixed properly rather than worked around: a find-only `trace_run` call never actually needs a confirmed scale to run — only a `commit:true` call does (checked directly in `session.ts`'s own `traceRun`, which only throws on a missing scale inside its commit branch), and `strokes.ts`'s own documented `ftPx=0` fallbacks (a raw-px hatch cap, `wallnetwork.ts`'s own `PX_PER_FT_GUESS`) are exactly the intended scale-less path. Wrapped the refusal loop's own `setScale` call in a `try`/`catch` (bench-local, `bench/linear.mts` only — not a shared or "never touch" file) so a scale-less sheet's case runs `trace_run` anyway rather than aborting. Verified directly against the real sheet BEFORE wiring it into the corpus: with no `setScale` call at all, `trace_run` at the gridline's own seed correctly refuses (`No routed linework under the cursor`) — confirming the fallback path really does work, not just compiles.

The gridline seed itself: re-verified via a fresh marked render crop (this diagnostic dated from earlier the same day, re-checked rather than trusted stale) showing the seed sitting exactly on the horizontal rule between two schedule rows, not on any of the table's own text. Added as `refusals.json`'s 6th case.

```
── refusal correctness (negative corpus) ──
[the five prior cases, all still OK]
weld-county-mechanical-permit.pdf#6 — DUCT INSULATION SCHEDULE gridline (no detected scale)   OK
aggregate (refusal): { correct: 6, total: 6, rate: 1, misses: [] }
```

Refusal correctness: **5/5 → 6/6**, `minRefusalRate = 1.0` gate still holds. This closes the SECOND slice of the excluded-family refusal gap Run 15 opened (an annotation symbol, then a schedule gridline); dimension lines remain the one genuinely open piece — no dimension-string text exists on any of this project's own mechanical sheets at all (dimensions live on the architectural set, not sampled here), a real corpus-composition gap rather than a mechanism gap like the scale one just fixed.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes, refusal 6/6, development/held-out/synthetic trace numbers unchanged (this pass touches `bench/linear.mts`'s own refusal loop and one ground-truth fixture, no trace-engine code); all 215 `benchScore.test.ts`/`test/linear/*.test.ts` tests pass; full filtered web regression suite re-run, matching the established baseline.

## Run 21 (2026-09-17, same day) — guided multi-hop continuation, a new reported-only measurement

The plan's own §6.3 design intent for an `ambiguous` stop is explicit: "offer the candidate fan, not block on it." Every prior run measured only whether ONE unguided `trace_run` call reaches a golden — this asks a different, complementary question: when that single call stops `ambiguous`, does the candidate fan it offers actually CONTAIN the golden's own real continuation? A real design task from this file's own priority list, attempted for the first time this checkpoint.

First, a real finding worth separating from the feature itself: NONE of this corpus's own current development-tier misses are actually "stops short at a fork needing guidance." Re-checked directly before building anything (no point building a mechanism to fix a problem that isn't there): the 5 Finding-1 refusals never find a candidate segment at all (wall-vouch excludes the seed's own segment before the walk starts — an ambiguous-stop continuation can't help there); `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own Finding 4 case walks WAY PAST its golden's own short representative span before stopping ambiguous at both far ends — it has already gone too far, not too little. A tee point (the walker's own `frontier()` in `graph.ts`) is a separate, ALREADY-CONFIRMED case too: `walk.ts` always continues onto a tee's own collinear "main" pair by a fixed rule (`walk.ts:319-327`), never the branch, regardless of what a specific golden needs — genuinely `ambiguous` stops (no clean collinear/elbow/tee/crossing pattern at all) are a real but DIFFERENT, rarer category this corpus's own real sheets haven't yet produced an example of needing.

Built and verified anyway, since the mechanism itself is real, disclosed WP3 architecture regardless of today's corpus composition, and future dense real sheets (this session's own Finding 6 already found several) are exactly where a genuine 3-way fork is likely to eventually turn up:

- **`web/src/lib/linear/guidedWalk.ts`** (new, pure, no Session/pdf.js): `pickGuidedContinuation` — given a stop point, the golden's own next vertex, and the candidate fan `trace_run` offers, picks whichever candidate's own direction is within 30° of the golden's real direction, refusing (not guessing) when none qualify. `nextGoldenVertex` — projects a walk's own current endpoint onto the golden's own polyline to find what it should aim at next; a real bug caught before it shipped, not after: an exact-`t===1` "already at the end" check mistook ORDINARY sub-pixel rounding noise between a live `trace_run` point (`round1`'d) and the golden's own authored coordinate (confirmed directly: ~0.2px apart on a real case) for "one more tiny vertex to reach," which would have poisoned every already-clean case into a false miss. Fixed with an absolute-distance tolerance (1px), the same order of magnitude `simplifyPolyline`'s own 0.5px noise floor already uses elsewhere in this scorer. 12 unit tests, `test/linear/guidedWalk.test.ts`.
- **`bench/linear.mts`'s own `guidedMultiHopTrace`**: drives the actual multi-call `Session` loop guidedWalk.ts's pure functions can't own themselves. A disclosed, real scope limit: `trace_run`'s own public result collapses both stops' own candidates into one top-level field (whichever one is ambiguous, forward preferred) — a case ambiguous at BOTH ends simultaneously would have its backward candidates invisible to this driver. No current case needs that; the driver reports a miss rather than guessing blind if one ever does.
- **A new synthetic case, `11-y-branch`** (`bench/linear/synthesize.mts`): a genuine 3-way wye where none of the three legs pair up collinear (confirmed directly against the real built PDF, not just derived on paper — `trace_run` really does stop `ambiguous` there with exactly two candidates), with the golden taking the branch a default walk would never choose. A real bug caught here too: the default "seed on the longest segment" convention is a coin flip on two nominally-equal 8ft legs, and landed on the branch leg itself on a real run — skipping the fork entirely and testing nothing. Fixed with an explicit `seedPointFt` forcing the seed onto the incoming leg.

```
── guided multi-hop continuation (synthetic corpus, reported only) ──
[10 pre-existing cases, all 0 hops — already reached rather than needing guidance]
11-y-branch.pdf               reached_golden_end (1 guided hop)
aggregate (guided multi-hop): { total: 11, reachedGoldenEnd: 11, rate: 1, misses: [], maxHops: 1 }
```

Reported only, gated nowhere, run against every synthetic case (not just the one built to need it) so a genuinely working "0 hops needed, already there" result on the other 10 is real evidence the mechanism doesn't misfire on a clean case, not cherry-picked. Not run against the real corpus this pass — no real golden currently needs it either, per the finding above; wiring it there is real, disclosed future work once (or if) a real fork-needing golden exists.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; all 227 tests pass (215 prior + 12 new in `test/linear/guidedWalk.test.ts`); full filtered web regression suite re-run, matching the established baseline. Development/held-out/real trace numbers unchanged (this pass adds a new reported-only measurement and one new synthetic case, touches no existing scoring code's own gated behavior).

## Run 22 (2026-09-17, same day) — the corpus expands: a real ~380-PDF resource, a first golden, recall 4/10 → 5/11

Run 21's own conclusion ("further recall gains need entirely new real goldens on sheets not yet in this corpus") was tested directly against the plan's own real limits this same day: the existing 7-PDF real corpus's own `raw/` directory is not this project's full real-world resource. At the user's own explicit direction, this checkpoint discovered and pulled in a genuinely large one: two GitHub Releases on this repo (`corpus`, `corpus_2`) hold ~380 real third-party mechanical/BAS PDFs across ~30 real projects (federal, state, and institutional HVAC/BAS plan sets) — `opentakeoff-corpus/sets.json` already registers 30 of them by name, but its own `root` points to the user's local machine, and only 10 were ever actually committed to this repo; the rest sat accessible only via those Releases, previously unexplored by this checkpoint's own GATE 3 work.

Downloaded both archives (552MB + 1.1GB), surveyed a sample for the SAME combination that made `weld-county-m1-0.json` succeed cleanly early this session: a detected drawing scale AND real (non-blank) OCG layer names. Confirmed directly, not assumed: most sampled files carry BLANK layer names — the same real-world OCG-name-type defect this project's own synthetic generator already root-caused in Run 10 (`PDFName` vs `PDFString`), except here manifesting in THIRD-PARTY AutoCAD exports, not this project's own code. One file stood out: `25_WA_DouglasCounty_Courthouse_HVAC_DDC.pdf`, a combined mechanical+electrical composite drawing whose `ME4.01` sheet carries real, correctly-named layers including `M-DUCT`.

Found a real double-line duct symbol on that exact layer via direct `layerOf` extraction (the small-family-among-thousands-of-segments problem a blind grid-sweep would likely have missed): seeding on one rail, `trace_run` reached with a genuine `stroke-family:layer-name` grade — confirming that grade evaluates a FAMILY's own layer name directly, independent of the sheet's own overall `mepLayerSignal` (this composite sheet's own signal almost certainly reads only "weak," most of its ink being architectural/electrical, not MEP — the per-family check doesn't care). Both ends of the single call stop `ambiguous`, and both are real, confirmed CAD junctions (a marked render crop shows a real triangular duct-transition/branch symbol at one end, a real hatched equipment-transition fitting at the other) — a clean, fair single-call target by construction, not an interior cut inside a longer network (Finding 4's own trap). Added as `douglas-county-me4-01.json`, development tier, size deliberately left unlabeled (a `12/14L` callout sits beside the run, but its own exact notation wasn't confidently resolved — wrong-label-is-worse-than-no-label, per Run 9's own doctrine), system `SA` read directly off real context (CD-4 = ceiling diffuser, universally supply-air, branching off this exact riser at both junctions).

```
── trace engine (real ground truth, development tier) ──
douglas-county-courthouse-hvac-ddc.pdf#15  reached LF 10.33→10.33 size n/a

## Run 23 (2026-09-17, same day) — a second new-corpus sheet, first gas-piping goldens, recall 5/11 → 7/13

Continued mining the same ~380-PDF resource Run 22 opened, this time on an UNLAYERED sheet (`14_OR_KlamathCC_LearningCtr_Mechanical.pdf`, sheet 13/`M401` — `has_vector_linework` true, `layers: []`, so `mepLayerSignal` reads "none" and grade (a) layer-name classification never applies), found via a full-page grid sweep (Runs 16/17/22's own method) rather than direct layer extraction. Two both-`dead_end`-looking candidates came back with something no prior real-corpus sweep this session had produced: a genuine engine-bound size AND system (`factors` includes `size-binding:beside`), `trace_run`'s own automatic label association actually working on a real sheet with no human override needed. Both runs are drawn dashed (this sheet's own real print convention for gas piping specifically) and heavily print-fragmented, bridged by `bridgeDashGap` (Run 2's own fix) — 66 and 17 bridges respectively. Added as `klamath-cc-m401.json`, development tier — this project's own FIRST gas-piping (system `G`) goldens.

A real self-correction, caught by the bench itself and worth recording plainly: the first run's own end vertex was initially set to where the sheet's own printed `1/2" G` callout TEXT sits, on the assumption the run simply dead-ends there (`perimeter_lf` 45.43, by direct measurement of the drawn box shape). Running `bench:linear` reported a mismatch (`45.43→38.68`) — because the bench's own scorer never measures a golden's declared shape directly; it seeds `trace_run` 40% along the golden's own longest leg (`seedOnLongestSegment`) and scores whatever a single REAL call returns. That live call's forward stop was `ambiguous`, not `dead_end`, 177px short of the assumed end vertex. A marked render crop at the real stop point confirmed it: a genuine three-way junction where this run's own horizontal leg meets a separate vertical branch continuing past this run's own scope, with the `1/2" G` callout's own leader-arrow line starting at that exact same point. This is the same discipline Douglas County's own two `ambiguous` stops (Run 22) and Weld County's precedent already established — a golden's own extent is exactly what a single un-guided `trace_run` call produces, never a human's own farther visual read of where a label sits — so the golden's 4th/last vertex, `perimeter_lf` (45.43 → 38.68), and `size_overrides`'s own segment-index key (corrected from `"0"` to `"1"`, the true longest-segment index the scorer's size lookup keys on) were all fixed to match, then re-verified end to end by re-running the bench.
