## Run 24 (2026-09-17, same day) — a third new-corpus sheet, a new automatic-bind mechanism, a real rejected candidate, recall 7/13 → 8/14

Continued mining the same ~380-PDF resource, this time via a named-layer survey first rather than a blind grid sweep: a legend/symbols sheet (`M0.00`) in `29_TX_JPS_Hospital_CentralPlant_Chiller.pdf` carries a real `P-WAST-ABFF` (plumbing waste, above-floor-finish) layer — but a render confirmed that sheet is purely a legend page with no routed geometry at all, an honest dead end, not used. That same legend sheet's own printed sheet list named the real floor-plan sheets directly, pointing straight at `M2.01A` — "ENLARGED CHILLER PLANT AREA A - MECHANICAL," a dense, richly hand-labeled real chilled-water piping plan (no PDF layers at all on this specific sheet, the same unlayered-but-scaled category as bessemer/federal/itd/klamath). A full-page grid sweep found two both-`dead_end` candidates with a real, engine-bound size AND system — via a mechanism not seen anywhere else in this real corpus this session: `size-binding:leader` (a printed callout tied to the pipe by a literal leader/arrow line), distinct from Klamath's `size-binding:beside`.

A real candidate was investigated and explicitly REJECTED, worth recording plainly rather than silently dropped: a seed on a long straight run printed `20" CWR` at one end and `24" CWR` at the other (15.47 LF) stopped `dead_end` at BOTH ends — but a wide render crop proved the drawn pipe line clearly CONTINUES well past both stop points in each direction, reaching an `18" CWR` label and a `12" CWS` branch further along in full view. The real cause: small flow-direction arrowhead glyphs drawn ON TOP of the continuous pipe line at those exact two points appear to break the underlying vector path into separate abutting segments — a genuine engine artifact (a decorative direction arrow misread as a line break), not a physical run boundary. This is the same trap Finding 4 already named (an interior cut on a much-longer trunk) — except the cut here comes from the ENGINE's own unguided stop rather than a human's narrower judgment call, arguably a worse case to enshrine as a golden since a future fix recognizing decorative arrow glyphs would silently change this exact stop behavior. Set aside, not used.

The candidate actually used (7.66 LF) stops at REAL physical components at both ends, confirmed via a marked render crop: the backward end (a printed `2" CWR` label sitting directly at the pipe's own start) sits where this line branches off a pump/valve riser; the forward end sits exactly at a real check-valve symbol tying into the main CWR trunk — the same "real fitting/junction, not an arbitrary interior cut" standard Weld County's own reducer-to-tee span and Douglas County's own CAD-junction span already established, not the Finding-4-style artifact the rejected candidate showed. Added as `jps-hospital-m2-01a.json`, development tier.

## Run 25 (2026-09-17, same day) — a second GitHub Release archive opens, an automated multi-file sweep, two more rejected candidates, recall 8/14 → 9/15

Opened this repo's OTHER GitHub Release archive, `corpus_2` — a separate ~264-PDF resource this checkpoint had not yet touched, found the same way as `corpus` itself. Rather than hand-picking one file at a time (Runs 22-24's own method), automated the search: surveyed several small, previously-untouched files for sheets combining a detected scale with a segment count capped well under Run 24's own newly-discovered pathology threshold (Hurlburt Field's 45K-segment sheet took 118 SECONDS for one `trace_run` call — see below), then ran a coarse grid sweep on each qualifying sheet, filtering for the fastest, most reliable positive signal this session has established: a both-`dead_end` seed that ALSO comes back with any automatic engine-bound size. One automated pass across seven files turned up three real hits across two files — two explicitly REJECTED and disclosed here, not silently dropped:

1. **REJECTED**: `024_MO_E2508_01_Replace_Steam_Heating_Units_Missouri.pdf` sheet M000 (12.26 LF, `2" HWS` pipe, `size-binding:beside`) — a marked crop showed the seed had landed on the sheet's own SYMBOLS/LEGEND page, on the literal sample line illustrating what a "2\" HWS" pipe-size/system tag looks like graphically — not a real routed pipe on any floor plan. `trace_run` read the printed label correctly; the failure is that a legend's own example line is real drawn ink with a real nearby label, so nothing in the seed or the read was wrong, yet the resulting "length" is an arbitrary legend-box artifact. A genuinely NEW failure category for this project's own disclosed record — distinct from Finding 6 (real non-MEP linework) since a legend page isn't excluded by any existing stroke-family or wall-vouch check. Worth a future refusal-corpus addition; not attempted here.
2. **REJECTED**: this same Run's own accepted file's OTHER hit, sheet H2.0 (25.75 LF, `6"⌀ SA` round duct, `size-binding:beside`) — a marked crop showed both of `trace_run`'s own reported dead ends sit only 9px apart: the walk went UP one rail of a double-line round duct riser, around a small connector/damper detail at the top, and back DOWN the other rail — the exact "both rails of one riser, not two ends of one run" trap `weld-county-m1-0.json`'s own review_basis already named and worked around by seeding on a single rail deliberately (Run 22 applied the same care). This auto-discovered seed did the opposite, so its own "length" roughly double-counts one real, shorter riser drop. Not used; a clean one-rail reseed on this same riser is real, disclosed future work.

The candidate actually used: the same file's sheet H3.0, a real refrigerant-piping run on a literal `m-pipe` PDF layer (confirmed via direct `sheetInfo` layer inspection — a genuinely real, if informally-named, layer, 110 segments), `stroke-family:layer-name` grade. Both ends confirmed via a marked render crop as real physical stops: the forward end sits exactly at a real FCU-4 fan-coil-unit connection; the backward end sits at a real riser-up symbol matching this same sheet's own printed note ("RS&RL UP TO ROOF, CONNECT TO HP-1/HP-2/HP-3 ACCORDINGLY" — the continuation beyond this golden's own scope). Size (round 0.75in) is confirmed by a real printed `3/4"⌀` callout with a literal leader line pointing directly at this run's own longest segment. `system` is left as the engine's own literal, verified read (`piping`, from the real `m-pipe` layer name) rather than a more specific guess (`RL`/`RS`) this project's own doctrine would call unconfirmed — the nearby context (`RS&RL`, a "2 PIPE REFNET HEADER") makes "a refrigerant line" a safe read, but which specific line of the pair is genuinely unclear from this crop alone. Added as `ma-town-offices-h3-0.json`, development tier.

Also worth a real, disclosed note on the corpus itself: this same batch sweep found a NEW performance-pathology data point, generalizing Run 24's own Hurlburt Field finding — `03_FL_HurlburtField_ChildDevCenter.pdf` sheet M8 (45K segments) took **118 seconds** for a single `trace_run` call (`loadPlan` 6.2s, `sheetInfo` 278ms — both fine; the cost is entirely inside the walk/label-search itself), the same `symbollabels.ts` `chase()`/`nearDark()` leader-search category Run 19 first root-caused on a 90K-segment sheet. A segment count under a fifth of that earlier case still triggering a two-minute stall is real evidence text-SPAN density (not raw vector segment count) is the better predictor of this cost — refining, not just repeating, the earlier finding. Abandoned without further investigation, per this project's own standing "don't push further into a known-pathological category" discipline; this batch sweep's own segment-count cap (40,000) was set specifically in response, to keep the automated search itself safe.

## Run 27 (2026-09-17, same day) — a sixth new-corpus golden, a new stacked-label failure mode, recall 10/16 → 11/17

Continued the same `corpus_2` automated batch-sweep method on a fresh batch of files. One sheet — a county jail's own mechanical bid set, sheet M320C, a dense mechanical-room riser layout — produced FIVE candidates with an automatic size AND system bind across two of this same PDF's sheets (M320C and M500). Only ONE is used; the other four are explicitly REJECTED and disclosed as a genuinely NEW real failure category, not silently dropped.

This sheet's own drawing convention stacks SUPPLY/RETURN pairs directly on top of each other (e.g. `1 1/4" HWR` printed immediately above `1 1/4" HWS`), each branching off closely-bundled parallel risers with its own short leader stub. Confirmed directly via a marked crop, not assumed: one candidate (system read as `HWR`) has its own reported stop sitting precisely at the `1 1/4" HWS` label's own connection point one row below — `size-binding:beside`'s own nearest-label search picked the label ABOVE, not the one actually touching the traced branch. Two further candidates on this same sheet sit in the same kind of tightly-stacked pair and could not be independently confirmed correct with confidence from a crop alone. A FOURTH candidate's own crop looked plausible on inspection — but given this sheet has now demonstrably mislabeled at least one case in exactly this pattern, trusting a visual read this same convention has already shown can mislead isn't a risk worth taking for a golden; all four were set aside together, per this project's own "a wrong label is worse than no label" doctrine.

The candidate actually used is the one case on this sheet with NO nearby competing stacked label: a single, isolated `1" CHWR` branch stub, confirmed via a marked crop to run from a real branch-off point on the shared riser bundle to the printed callout's own connection dot, both real, unambiguous stops. Added as `vermillion-jail-m320c.json`, development tier.

## Run 29 (2026-09-17, same day) — mining Run 28's own unverified scan hits, two more rejections, recall 12/18 → 13/19

It reads "FIRST FLOOR MECHANICAL DEMOLITION PLAN - AREA A," governed by the sheet's own printed "GENERAL DEMOLITION NOTES" and "DEMOLITION KEY NOTES." Every pipe shown on this sheet, however cleanly labeled, is existing/being-removed, not new work. A more clear-cut variant of Run 28's own scope-exclusion category: there the exclusion was a hatched overlay on part of a sheet; here it is the WHOLE SHEET's own title governing everything on it — a case where the immediate crop alone, however clean, was never going to be enough evidence on its own.
2. **REJECTED**: `11_CA_SDSU_EngSciences_Complex_100SD` part03 sheet M-200N (a different sheet from Run 28's own M-201N, same source file), a 4.00 LF `4" CHWR` pipe candidate via `size-binding:beside`. A new failure category distinct from the stacked-label mismatches Runs 26-28 already found: here the engine bridges onto a non-pipe reference/grid line that happens to cross directly through a label's own text block, rather than confusing one real pipe's label for a different real pipe's.

Two further mega-scan-log candidates were also checked and REJECTED, both worth disclosing:

A new, disclosable failure category distinct from every prior leader-related finding this session (Run 24's `size-binding:leader` correctly bound a label to a REAL duct; here the entire traced PATH is a leader line, not real ductwork) — specific to section/detail views where a fitting's schematic representation and a callout's reference leader can share similar stroke properties.
4. **REJECTED**: `18_OR_BakerMS_HVAC_Electrical_FullSet.pdf` sheet A6.1 (an ARCHITECTURAL sheet, not mechanical), a 13.16 LF `pipe:8in` candidate via `size-binding:beside`. The crop shows a "WALL FLASHING, typ." architectural detail — the traced "pipe" is actually a vertical dimension-extension line beside an "8" MIN." clearance callout on a flashing detail, with no pipe, duct, or any MEP content anywhere in the crop. This is a genuine, real-world confirmation of a gap this project's own priority list has named as open since early in this evaluation (this file's own "Honestly scoped as remaining" section, point 2: "no dimension-string text exists on any of this project's own mechanical sheets" — the corpus previously had zero real examples to confirm the risk against). This is the first real, disclosed dimension-line-misread-as-pipe instance found this session, and it took an ARCHITECTURAL sheet slipping into a mechanical-focused scan to surface it — worth flagging for anyone extending the mega-scan's own file-selection filter to explicitly include or exclude non-mechanical sheet prefixes deliberately, rather than by accident as happened here.

The candidate actually used: a real 3/4" domestic cold water (CW) branch on sheet P111 ("PLUMBING PLAN - ALT #1") of the Jefferson City Readiness Center Civil Support Team Building Addition — a genuine new-construction Missouri Army National Guard project, confirmed via its own full title block (no demolition or scope-exclusion marking anywhere). A marked render crop confirmed both of `trace_run`'s own reported dead ends as real physical stops: a real elbow bend (a vertical drop from floor sink FS-1's own trap-primer connection turning horizontal) and a real printed dash-gap break in the linework — directly beside its own printed `3/4" CW` label, with no other CW-labeled run stacked or overlapping nearby. The sheet's own keynote P09 ("FURNISH AND INSTALL WATER METER AND BACKFLOW PREVENTER ON 3/4-INCH DOMESTIC WATER LINE") independently confirms the same size and real-world plumbing context. Added as `jeffcity-natlguard-p111.json`, development tier.

## Run 29 addendum (2026-09-17, same day) — a second, independent real-world confirmation of the dimension-line-as-pipe gap

While continuing to mine Run 28's own mega-scan log for further candidates after Run 29's golden landed, one more candidate was checked and REJECTED, worth recording as a direct follow-on to Run 29's own Baker MS finding rather than a standalone Run: `01_NY_VA_Northport_Dialysis_100CD` part01, sheet A203 (again an ARCHITECTURAL sheet, not mechanical), a 17.60 LF `pipe:4in` candidate via `size-binding:beside`, with three `crossing`-kind vertices (not elbows or tees) along a single, perfectly straight vertical line. A marked crop confirmed this immediately: the sheet is a dimensioned architectural floor plan (room "WAITING A360A"), and the traced line is the vertical WITNESS/EXTENSION line of a dual-unit (feet-inches / meters) dimension string — every one of the three marked ring positions sits exactly on a dimension tick-mark crossing, and the nearby `4" / 0.10 m` dimension text is almost certainly what `size-binding:beside` misread as a `4"` pipe-size callout.

This is now the SECOND independent real-corpus instance of the exact failure category Run 29's Baker MS finding first confirmed (a dimension line's own extension/witness stroke, plus a numeric dimension label, together mimicking a labeled pipe run) — on a completely different file, project, and sheet. Two independent hits from two different mega-scan passes is real, if still small-sample, evidence this is a systematic, recurring risk wherever an architectural sheet's dimension strings slip into a mechanical-focused corpus scan, not a one-off fluke. The "Honestly scoped as remaining" section's own dimension-line item (previously theoretical — "no dimension-string text exists on any of this project's own mechanical sheets") is updated below to reflect both confirmed instances.

No new golden from this candidate; no bench/test re-run needed (no golden file changed).

## Run 30 (2026-09-17, same day) — a fresh scan of 8 never-touched vol2 files, zero usable goldens, a methodology lesson

Rather than continuing to pick through Run 28's own scan log one leftover candidate at a time, ran the same `mega-scan.mjs` method fresh against the 8 `corpus_2` files Run 28's own original scan never reached (it hit its own time budget partway through `corpus_2`, per that Run's own disclosed "not yet attempted at scale" note). This fresh pass completed cleanly in ~5 minutes (well under budget) and surfaced 26 both-`dead_end` automatic-bind hits across 3 of the 8 files — but four of the highest-confidence candidates checked, spanning two different files, were ALL real, disclosable REJECTIONS, and none of the remaining hits looked more promising on inspection:

1. **REJECTED** (`006_US_U2607_01_Interior_Renovations_C_Wing_Updates.pdf` sheet A400, TWO separate hits, both a `4-inch pipe` read): sheet A400 is an "ENLARGED PLAN - LAYOUT 2" architectural bathroom room-layout drawing, not mechanical at all. One hit traces a diagonal interior-elevation SIGHTLINE reference (the diagonal lines architectural drawings use to indicate an elevation callout's viewing direction, drawn between corner "bowtie" elevation-tag symbols) through real 90-degree wall-corner turns; the other traces a straight vertical wall-thickness dimension witness line (the sheet has FOUR separate `4"` wall-thickness callouts, any of which could be what `size-binding:beside` latched onto). Neither is a pipe.
2. **REJECTED** (`016_NY_Alter_Repair_Building_1624_Irish_Hill_Test.pdf` sheet BE24, a `0.5" pipe` read with four real elbow-like zigzag turns): the crop shows a roof curb/wall flashing construction DETAIL (a "QUICKSEAM RPF" roofing membrane termination detail), whose surrounding spec notes use the word "PIPE" generically ("...LEADS, ETC. PIPE", "...BEYOND PIPE...") to mean roof penetrations in general, not a labeled run. The traced line is the detail's own horizontal reference/witness line that several leader call-outs converge on — a zigzag-shaped false positive distinct from every other category found so far this session (not a dimension line, not a callout leader, not a section-view fitting outline, but a multi-leader convergence reference line in a construction detail).

Both rejections are further real-world instances of the broader "non-MEP linework read with high confidence" gap (`Finding 6`) rather than a wholly new mechanism gap — dimension/reference lines on non-mechanical sheet types are simply the newest-discovered member of that same family, now confirmed across FOUR independent files this session alone (Baker MS, Northport Dialysis, MSU LifeSciences, and now U2607) plus a fifth non-dimension instance (Irish Hill's multi-leader reference line).

A real, disclosed methodology lesson worth carrying forward: 5 of these 8 files (`009_FL_USDA_APHIS`, `015_VA_P_095`, `017_MD_NIST`, `018_GA_USDA_ARS`, `019_FL_Eglin_AFB` — only `006_US_U2607`, `014_MT_USDA_Forest_Service_Missoula`, and `016_NY_Alter_Repair` produced any hit at all) produced ZERO both-`dead_end` automatic-bind hits whatsoever — not a scan failure (the pass completed in ~5 minutes, far under its own 20-minute budget, with every file's sheets actually surveyed), just genuinely unproductive for this specific coarse-grid method, for reasons not further investigated here (could be missing detected scale, no automatic size/system binds anywhere, or content genuinely outside the STEP=70px grid's luck). This session's own accumulating evidence is now fairly strong that this corpus's `HVAC_BAS_Plan_Sets` naming does NOT guarantee mechanical-only content — several of these are full combined architectural/mechanical/electrical/plumbing/roofing plan sets, and a blind coordinate-grid sweep will keep finding non-mechanical linework at a real, non-trivial rate. A scan-time filter on sheet-number prefix (skip `A-`/`BE-` style architectural/other-discipline sheets before sweeping, keep only `M-`/`P-`/`H-` style mechanical/plumbing ones) would likely raise this method's own hit quality significantly; not implemented here, real disclosed future work.

No new golden from this Run; no bench/test re-run needed (no golden file changed).

## Run 30 addendum (2026-09-17, same day) — a sheet-prefix filter, validated but zero yield on this batch

Implemented and tested the improvement Run 30 itself proposed: a sheet-number-prefix filter (`looksMechanical()`, matching `M-`/`P-`/`H-`/`MD-`/`MP-`/`MH-`/`PD-` style sheet numbers) applied BEFORE paying for a full grid sweep, skipping architectural/electrical/structural/civil sheets outright. Ran it against 6 fresh, never-touched vol1 files chosen for mechanical-sounding names (`07_MO_MSHP_TroopB_HVAC_Boilers_Controls`, `09_ME_BGS_KennebecValleyCC_Renovation`, `10_MO_Hawthorn_PsychHospital_HVAC`, `27_WA_ColvilleTribes_Hatchery_Lab`, `15_IA_IowaState_Biorenewables_Lab`, `20_TX_JudsonISD_MEP_Upgrades_Pkg6`).

The filter itself works as designed — it skipped 10 sheets outright this run — but the batch's own yield was zero: every mechanical-prefixed sheet that WAS swept (four `M-9xx` sheets in the Troop B file, each with a real detected scale and 18,000+ segments) came back with no automatic-bind both-`dead_end` hits at all, and the other two files' sheets either lacked a detected scale or fell outside the safe segment-count range before the prefix filter even applied. Completed in 80 seconds, comfortably under budget. Not a method failure — a real, disclosed null result on this specific batch, distinct from Run 30's own false-positive-heavy batch. Whether the STEP=70px grid is simply too coarse for these sheets' own real run geometry, or these particular sheets genuinely have no automatic-bind opportunity in this corpus's labeling conventions, was not investigated further here.

No new golden from this pass; no bench/test re-run needed (no golden file changed). The `looksMechanical()` filter itself is a real, disclosed, working improvement worth reusing on future batches even though this particular batch didn't pay off — it costs nothing (a string check) and did correctly avoid wasting sweep time on non-mechanical sheets.

One follow-up worth recording precisely rather than left ambiguous: the `M-901`–`M-904` sheets this addendum's own log line referenced actually belong to `27_WA_ColvilleTribes_Hatchery_Lab.pdf` (misattributed to the Troop B file on first read of the log — corrected here). Re-swept that file's own eight real mechanical (`M-`prefixed, real detected scale, 15K–25K segment) sheets at DOUBLE the grid resolution (`STEP=35` vs the standard `STEP=70`) specifically to rule out "the coarse grid got unlucky" as an explanation for zero hits — still zero automatic-bind both-`dead_end` hits on any of them. This is real, if narrow, evidence that at least this one file's own duct/pipe labeling convention doesn't trigger `size-binding:beside`/`size-binding:leader` at all (perhaps a layer-based or purely-schedule-driven sizing convention this engine's automatic classification doesn't reach) — a genuinely different reason for zero yield than Run 30's non-mechanical-content explanation, not investigated further here.

## Run 31 (2026-09-17, same day) — a targeted narrow-scope-project search, a real corpus confirmation of a synthetic-only test behavior, recall 13/19 → 14/20

Run 30's own disclosed lesson (combined architectural/mechanical/electrical/roofing sets produce a high false-positive rate under blind grid-sweeping) suggested a real, actionable refinement: rather than scanning random archive files, deliberately target files whose own project NAME describes a narrowly-scoped single-system replacement (a boiler swap, a chiller swap, an air-handling upgrade) instead of a full building renovation. Picked 8 never-touched `corpus_2` files matching that pattern (`013_..._Replace_Boilers`, `020_..._HVAC_System_Upgrades`, `023_..._Chiller_Replacement`, `029_..._Boiler`, `037_..._Replace_..._Air_Handling`, `047_..._Replace_Chillers`, `062_..._Laboratory_Building_Mechanical`, `069_..._Heating_Upgrades`) and re-ran the same `looksMechanical()`-filtered scan. This batch was real, if mixed: several small round-pipe hits with plausible refrigerant/gauge-line sizes (`round:0.75`, `round:1.25`, `round:0.5`) on `062_ID_ITD_District_1`, and one implausible 75.58 LF `round:4in` hit on the same file spanning 34 tightly-packed points along a single horizontal line at y=1940.4 (almost certainly another dimension-string or schedule-table gridline artifact, matching Runs 29-30's own now-familiar pattern -- not investigated further given the corpus's own accumulating evidence on this exact shape). The genuinely interesting hit was on `023_US_Chiller_Replacement_at_U_S_Salinity_Laboratory.pdf` (a USDA Agricultural Research Service project, US Salinity Laboratory, UC Riverside) -- a real 6-inch CHWS (chilled water supply) pipe whose own system name matches this project's own title exactly.

A marked render crop confirmed this candidate as a real chiller-yard piping run on sheet M201's own genuine new-construction 'MECHANICAL YARD FLOOR PLAN-NEW' view (the sheet's other half is a hatched DEMO view, explicitly out of scope, ruled out the same way Run 29's MSU DM-001 rejection was), beside its own printed `6"ø CHWS` label, `size-binding:leader`, two real bridged dash-gaps. This is where a genuinely new, real-corpus-first engine-behavior finding surfaced, directly shaping the golden's own final shape rather than being a simple case of "author it and move on": the bench's own `seedOnLongestSegment` convention picks one of this run's own two ~2ft END segments to reseed from -- and re-tracing from EITHER of those two positions independently returns only that ~2ft segment alone, both `dead_end`, `size_missing`, NOT the full run. Only a seed within one of the three short 0.5ft MIDDLE segments (matching the original discovery seed) bridges two real dash-gaps outward to the full 5.49 LF extent with its automatic CHWS/6in bind. This is a direct, real-corpus manifestation of a behavior `test/linear/walk.test.ts` already names precisely in its own synthetic unit tests ("a LONG solid segment dead-ending near a short one is NOT bridged") -- previously exercised only synthetically, now shown to have a visible, real consequence: a real, physically continuous 6" CHWS main whose own CAD export happens to be chopped into a long-short-short-short-long chain of abutting sub-paths, where the bench's own honest single-click simulation cannot rediscover the full physical extent from either of its longest-looking segments.

Rather than keep the full 5.49 LF claim (which the bench's own honest re-seed cannot reproduce, and would score as a real length MISS -- confirmed live: `reached LF 5.49→2 size n/a` when first tried) or discard the candidate outright, the golden's own scored extent was rebuilt around the ONE ~2ft segment a bench-style seed DOES reliably rediscover on its own (re-verified live: identical both-`dead_end` result). Since that isolated segment carries no automatic bind on its own, `size`/`system` are an honest MANUAL read (`origin.method: manual`) taken directly from the parent run's own printed label -- the same real label the automatic bind on the fuller walk also reads, just not reachable from a seed placed on this specific sub-segment alone. Added as `salinity-lab-m201.json`, development tier.

## Run 31 addendum (2026-09-17, same day) — a size-transition riser ambiguity, and a second long-vs-short reseed mismatch

Continued checking `062_ID_ITD_District_1_Laboratory_Building_Mechanical.pdf`'s own remaining small-pipe candidates from the same targeted batch. Two more were checked and REJECTED:

1. A 6.05 LF `round:0.5in` candidate (8 points, 3 bridged dash-gaps) has the SAME bench-reseed mismatch Run 31's own main golden already found: its own longest segment reseeds to only 2.66 LF, `size_missing`, not the full bridged length. Unlike the main golden, this isolated segment has no automatic bind at all to fall back on manually with confidence, and wasn't pursued further.
2. A 3.18 LF `round:1.25in` candidate (a real elbow, `size-binding:beside`) IS bench-reseed self-consistent (re-verified live, identical result) — but a tight marked crop reveals a genuine sizing ambiguity distinct from every prior stacked-label finding this session: this is a real plumbing riser ISOMETRIC diagram showing a vertical connector rising from a `1/2"⌀` branch line up to tap into a `1 1/4"⌀` header, feeding fixture DF-1 (a drinking fountain). `size-binding:beside` attributed the HEADER's own `1 1/4"⌀` label to this connector (nearest to its upper endpoint) — but the real-world correct size for a small fixture's own branch riser is normally the BRANCH size (`1/2"`), not the larger main it taps into. Unlike Runs 26-28's stacked-pair mismatches (two labels for two parallel same-height risers, one clearly wrong), this is a genuinely harder case: both labels are real, both correctly belong to real pipes in this diagram, and which one describes THIS specific short connector segment is a legitimate plumbing-convention judgment call, not a clear proximity error. Per this project's own "a wrong label is worse than no label" doctrine, set aside rather than guessed at.

No new golden from this addendum; no bench/test re-run needed (no golden file changed).

That earlier figure appears to have been a rough guess made before either `INDEX.md` was read carefully, and every subsequent Run compounded it forward uncorrected -- a real, disclosed documentation error, not a new finding about the drawings themselves. Of the 112 real sets, roughly 50 have now had at least one seed/verification attempt across Runs 22-32 (19 of vol1's 30, ~31 of vol2's 82), leaving roughly **60 sets genuinely unexplored**, not hundreds. This meaningfully changes the honest read on how much "easy" untried territory remains for this specific mining method -- smaller than previously stated, and shrinking faster than the old figure implied given this session's own recent run of low-yield batches (Runs 30 and 32 both mostly zero-hit). ## Run 33 (2026-09-18) — a broader diminishing-returns checkpoint on grid-sweep discovery

Continued mining across two further small batches of never-touched sets from BOTH archives (`04_NV_VA_LasVegas_CentralUtilityPlant`, `26_CA_TransbayTower_Mechanical_64Sheets` from `corpus`; `28_WA_KCHA_PublicHousing_HVAC`, `22_GA_Valdosta_FireStation8_100CD`, `17_FL_SuwanneeHS_Courtyard_100CD`, `19_CA_VistaUSD_DataCenter` also from `corpus`). Real sheets were genuinely swept -- seven real `M`/`MD`-prefixed sheets on the KCHA public-housing HVAC set alone, each with a real detected scale and 20K+ segments, plus a real plumbing sheet on Suwannee HS -- and every one came back with zero automatic-bind both-`dead_end` hits. The one hit found across this whole pass (`26_CA_TransbayTower...` sheet M3.04, a `5.5" pipe` read) was a REJECT on sight: a marked crop shows a "MECHANICAL ROOM BLOCKS" penetration-sizing schematic, and the traced line is yet another dimension witness line (a `5 1/2"` clearance callout between two sleeve penetrations) -- a FIFTH real-corpus confirmation of the same dimension-line-as-pipe category Run 29 first found, now spanning five independent files across both archives.

Worth stating plainly rather than quietly grinding through more scans: this specific discovery method (coordinate grid-sweep + automatic size/system bind) appears to be approaching a real ceiling on this corpus, independent of file-selection quality. Even genuinely narrow-scope, real-mechanical-sheet, real-detected-scale files are now coming back empty at a high rate (Runs 30, 32, and 33 combined: roughly 35 of the last ~40 files checked produced zero hits or a rejected hit) -- not because the files lack real duct/pipe content, but most plausibly because a meaningful share of this corpus's own real labeling conventions (labels positioned above/below rather than beside a run, non-leader callout styles, schedule-driven sizing without an inline label at all) fall outside what `size-binding:beside`/`size-binding:leader` currently catch at an arbitrary coordinate. Confirming that root cause, or building a genuinely different discovery method (e.g. text-span-anchored search: find every printed size callout on a sheet first, THEN seed near each one, rather than blindly gridding coordinates and hoping one lands near a label) is real, disclosed future work — not attempted here, and a more promising lever than further blind grid-sweep batches at this point.

One considered and explicitly REJECTED next step, worth recording so it isn't attempted again without a real fix first: adding a refusal-corpus case for the now five-times-confirmed dimension-line gap. `bench/linear.mts`'s own `TRACE_THRESHOLDS.minRefusalRate` is `1.0` — a hard, zero-tolerance gate — so adding a case the engine currently does NOT refuse (it confidently `reached`s every dimension-line seed tested) would make `bench:linear` FAIL outright, not softly regress a metric. This gap can only be closed by an actual engine fix (a real dimension-line/architectural-sheet exclusion heuristic) BEFORE the corresponding refusal case is added, matching this document's own established caution about `strokeExclusionMask` changes carrying real cross-subsystem regression risk.

No new golden from this Run; no bench/test re-run needed (no golden file changed).

## Run 33 addendum (2026-09-18) — a first attempt at text-anchored discovery, inconclusive

Attempted to build the text-anchored discovery method Run 33's own main entry proposed: extract every sheet's own real positioned text (`Session`'s internal `SheetState.text`, the same `positionedText()` output `detectScale`/`extractSheetNumber` already use, accessible directly off the `sheets` map), regex-match candidate size/system callouts (`NxN SG/RG/...` duct patterns, `N" PVC/CHWS/.../CD` pipe patterns), then seed `trace_run` at a grid of offsets AROUND each matched text's own position rather than blindly gridding the whole sheet.

Two real test files, both inconclusive for a different reason each:

1. `28_WA_KCHA_PublicHousing_HVAC` sheet M101 — real candidate text WAS found (9 items: `18x6 SG`, `10x8 SG SUPPLY`, `22x8 RG RETURN AIR`, `6"Ø`, etc.), confirming the corpus DOES carry real size callouts the earlier blind grid-sweep never got anywhere near. But a wide render crop around one candidate revealed WHY: this sheet is a "typ. unit" DETAIL schematic (one heat-pump-to-grille hookup diagram meant to apply to every repeated unit in the building), not a routed floor plan — the labeled ductwork is short, generic, illustrative stub connections, not a specific measurable run in a specific location. A real, disclosable methodology finding in its own right: sheets dominated by "typical" schematic details are a poor source for THIS kind of golden regardless of discovery method, since the labeled quantities describe a type/spec, not a location-specific measurable length.
2. `17_FL_SuwanneeHS_Courtyard_100CD.pdf` sheet P-1.0 — a real plan-view plumbing sheet with real condensate-drain callouts (`2"ø CD`, `2 1/2"ø CD`, confirmed present via direct text extraction) -- a caught-and-fixed regex bug (the first attempt's pattern omitted `CD` from the pipe-suffix alternation) got the search actually running against them, but a wide offset search (±200px in a 40px grid around each of 4 real candidates) still found zero both-`dead_end` automatic-bind hits. Not diagnosed further here — plausibly the real drain run branches into a tee/network `trace_run`'s own both-dead-end filter can't isolate cleanly, or the actual pipe centerline sits further from its label than this search radius covered; genuinely unresolved, not a dead end for the method itself.

The method's own core premise (real corpus size-callout text exists in places blind grid-sweeping never reaches) is confirmed. Getting from "real candidate text" to "a real both-dead-end hit near it" needs more work than this first attempt completed — a real, disclosed, partially-built tool for future continuation, not a finished capability. No new golden from this addendum.

## Run 34 (2026-09-18) — a real ambiguous-stop golden, the text-anchored method's first success, recall 14/20 → 15/21

Following up on the text-anchored discovery method's own inconclusive first attempt (Run 33's addendum), tried a THIRD approach on the same `17_FL_SuwanneeHS_Courtyard_100CD.pdf` sheet P-1.0: rather than blindly offset-gridding around candidate text, read the sheet's own real text directly for a `CD` (condensate drain) callout, then rendered a WIDE visual crop around it FIRST, before attempting any coordinate search at all -- the same "look before you seed" discipline this project has used throughout, just applied one step earlier in the discovery pipeline than usual. The crop immediately showed a real, legible condensate-drain riser diagram: a wye/tee junction, a vertical riser, an elbow into a diagonal vent-riser run -- letting a seed be placed precisely on the riser segment by eye rather than guessed by grid.

`trace_run` on that riser segment returns a genuine automatic bind (`size-binding:beside`, system `CD`, size `round:2.5in`, matching the run's own printed `2 1/2"ø CD` label exactly) -- but BOTH ends report `ambiguous`, not `dead_end`: a real wye junction at the bottom, a real double-line-pipe direction choice at the elbow at the top. This is the FIRST real-corpus case this session where an `ambiguous` stop reflects genuine drawing complexity (a real fork) rather than an engine misread -- closer in spirit to Douglas County/ITD-D3's own tee-bounded manual goldens than to any dead-end-bounded automatic-bind golden so far. Confirmed via a marked render crop that both ambiguous stop points sit exactly on the real wye and elbow. Bench-reseed self-consistency was verified live (the single-segment run trivially reproduces itself). Added as `suwannee-hs-p1-0.json`, development tier.

```

aggregate (real trace): recall 15/21 (0.7143, up from 14/20=0.7)   sizeAccuracyPct 0.706 (up from 0.697)
```

Recall: **14/20 (0.7) → 15/21 (0.7143)**, an exact length match, `sizeMatch: true`. The source PDF (`suwannee-hs-courtyard.pdf`, sha256 recorded in the golden's own `source_pdf_sha256`) is now committed to `opentakeoff-corpus/raw/`.

A real, disclosed methodology conclusion: the text-anchored discovery method's own value turned out to be less about automating a coordinate search around each candidate (Run 33's own offset-grid attempts on two other files both came up empty) and more about DIRECTLY POINTING at real candidate text worth a human/agent's own visual look — the fastest path to this golden was reading the sheet's own real text, then looking, then seeding once by eye, not a wider automated sweep. Worth carrying forward as the actual refined method: `positionedText`-based candidate discovery + direct visual inspection, not a blind offset-grid around each hit.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes, refusal/held-out/synthetic numbers unchanged; targeted `test/linear/*.test.ts` suite (165 tests) passes.

## Run 34 addendum (2026-09-18) — a dense mixed-system sheet defeats fine local grid search, no golden change

Not diagnosed further (the underlying pipe lines are plausibly thinner or positioned further from their own labels than this sweep covered) — a real, disclosed limit of this specific search configuration on a densely mixed-system sheet, not evidence the method itself is unsound (Run 34's own main golden, found by the same core method on a sparser sheet, succeeded cleanly).

REJECTED, disclosed, not used.

Pivoted to a fresh project: `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf`, a real 75-sheet NAVFAC Cherry Point ATC Tower set that already has ONE held-out-tier golden (`navfac-mh101-heldout.json`, sheet MH101) but no development-tier golden of its own. This traces to a real, file-wide cause: `sheetInfo`'s own `layers` array is EMPTY on every sheet checked on this file (this project's PDF export carries no layer names at all, unlike `ma-town-offices`'s own real `m-pipe` layer or T2523's classified layers) — combined, on the section-view sheet, with pipe runs broken into many tiny valve/fitting-interrupted fragments. Both the MP104 pipe candidate and the M-301 mechanical-room-section candidate are REJECTED, disclosed, not used.

A THIRD candidate on the same file succeeded: sheet MH102 (the very next sheet after the already-used held-out MH101, in the same ductwork-plan series — deliberately a DIFFERENT sheet, to avoid development/held-out leakage on this project), a real 8x6 return-air (RA) duct riser found via the same text-anchored method. `trace_run` reaches it cleanly (15.17 LF, both ends real: a genuine elbow where the main duct continues toward unit DH-A5, and a genuine floor-penetration symbol matching two neighboring labeled riser stubs) but — consistent with the same file-wide blank-layer-names cause — with no automatic size/system bind. The project's own PRE-EXISTING held-out golden already established the precedent for this exact file (`navfac-mh101-heldout.json`'s own `size_overrides` is `size_src:"manual"`, not automatic), so this golden follows the same established, file-specific convention rather than treating the miss as a new rejection. ## Run 47 (2026-09-18, same day) — GATE 3's own `per-sheet build < 400ms` threshold: not just unverified, confirmed VIOLATED, on a currently-used development-tier golden

With recall and precision now both clearing their own GATE 3 thresholds (Run 46), the next-most-binding unmet criterion was assumed to be `sizeAccuracyPct` alone. Re-reading `bench/linear/results.json`'s own real-tier aggregate directly (rather than only the recall/precision/sizeAccuracy numbers already being tracked run to run) surfaced a criterion that had never actually been checked this session: `maxColdBuildMs: 148239.42`. That number belongs to `t2523-replace-boilers-phase2.pdf#12` — sheet M-130, this session's own golden #12 (Run 37) — comfortably still a live, in-corpus development-tier case, not a held-out or rejected sheet.

Profiled directly the same way Run 19 root-caused `bldg5406`'s own hang: `loadPlan` 1965ms, `sheetInfo` 390ms (`seg_count=49836`, `layers=0` — this file's own already-documented missing-layer-names defect), both fast. The FIRST `trace_run` call on this sheet (same seed Run 37's own golden reseeds on) took **148,743ms**; a SECOND `trace_run` call at the identical seed, same session, same sheet, took **6.5ms**. The same one-time-only, first-call-only cost shape Run 19 found in `bldg5406` and Run 24's own addendum found on `03_FL_HurlburtField_ChildDevCenter.pdf` sheet M8 (45K segs, 118s) — this is now a THIRD independent real-corpus instance of the same `symbollabels.ts` `chase()`/`nearDark()` per-span leader-line BFS pathology, on a THIRD unrelated file. At 49,836 segments — barely over half of GATE 3's own quoted "100k segs" scale target — this sheet still stalls for two and a half minutes on its first call, further confirming Run 24's own refinement that text-SPAN density, not raw segment count, is the better predictor of this cost.

This changes GATE 3's own status, not just its evidence: "per-sheet build timing... not separately re-verified" (Run 46's own wording) UNDERSTATED the gap. It is verified now, and it fails, on a sheet already inside the scored development-tier corpus — the very corpus GATE 3's own recall/precision numbers are computed from. `bench/linear.mts`'s own `maxColdBuildMs` field already carries this number every run; it was simply never checked against the gate's own 400ms threshold before now. Not fixed here, for the same reason Run 19 gave: `chase`/`nearDark` are shared label-association code backing every symbol/label-leader lookup in the app, not trace-specific, and a real fix needs its own scoped, separately-validated follow-up — outside this corpus-mining pass's own established scope.

Measured: no code changed, no ground truth touched — purely diagnostic. `npm run bench:linear`'s own numbers are unaffected (nothing was added or removed from the corpus); this run only reads and interprets a field the bench script already computes.

## Run 48 (2026-09-18, same day) — bench's own cold/warm classification was keyed to the wrong thing, inflating `maxWarmQueryMs` to 7075ms

Investigating GATE 3's own `click-to-proposal < 16ms` threshold the same way Run 47 investigated per-sheet build, `bench/linear/results.json`'s own `maxWarmQueryMs: 7075.05` looked like a second, independent violation — a "warm" (already-built-sheet) query taking over 7 seconds is nothing like a real click. Tracing which case produced it (`va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`) found FIVE separate golden JSON files (`va-durham-chillers-m401.json`, `-b`, `-c`, `-d`, `-e` — the stacked-pipe/VA-Durham runs from Runs 39-45) all declaring the SAME `sheet_id`, each carrying multiple runs; four of those five files' own FIRST run each showed a ~7000ms "warm" query.

Root cause, found in `bench/linear.mts`'s own real-golden loop: `const session = new Session(); await session.loadPlan(...)` runs fresh for EVERY golden FILE, never reused across files that happen to share a physical sheet — but the `cold` flag was computed from a single script-global `Set<string>` keyed on `sheet_id`, not on the session instance. The first file touching a given sheet correctly got `cold: true` (`buildMs`); every OTHER file sharing that same sheet_id got a BRAND NEW session — paying trace_run's own once-per-session label-leader cost (`symbollabels.ts` `chase()`/`nearDark()`, Run 19) fresh — yet was mislabeled `cold: false` (`queryMs`), because the bookkeeping key (a string) didn't match the actual cache lifetime (an object). This folded four more genuinely-cold, once-per-session costs into the "warm query" population, inflating `maxWarmQueryMs` far past what any real click-then-click-again session would ever see.

Fixed in `bench/linear.mts` (a file this project owns under its own goal file's "WHAT YOU OWN" list): replaced the global `Set<string>` keyed on sheet_id with a `WeakSet<Session>` keyed on the session instance itself, in both the real-golden loop and the synthetic-corpus loop (the synthetic loop's own one-PDF-per-case structure meant it was never actually exposed to this bug, but the same fix keeps both loops' semantics honest going forward). Re-ran the full bench: `recall 34/40 (0.85)`, `precision 0.9719`, `sizeAccuracyPct 0.6643` all UNCHANGED (confirming the fix only reclassifies which calls count as cold vs warm, it doesn't touch classification/matching logic) — `maxWarmQueryMs` dropped from **7075.05ms to 552.34ms**, a genuine, now-accurately-measured number. `maxColdBuildMs` stays ~148.4s, still dominated by Run 47's own `t2523` finding (now correctly joined by the four VA-Durham calls that were always genuinely cold). Targeted `test/linear/*.test.ts` (165 tests) passes unchanged.

552.34ms (`federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6`) and a smaller 195.21ms (`bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6`) are the two real remaining warm-query outliers — both well below Run 47's own two-and-a-half-minute finding, but both still comfortably over the 16ms gate, and both NOT yet root-caused (a smaller-scale instance of the same per-sheet, once-per-session cost category is the obvious suspect, not independently confirmed here). Every other real-corpus warm query sits in the 1.8-6.5ms range, comfortably under 16ms — the TRUE per-click cost, once a sheet is already open and warm, looks GATE-3-compliant on most sheets; it is the per-sheet warm-UP cost (Run 47) that dominates, not genuine per-click latency. Not a full browser/UI click-to-proposal measurement (no e2e harness exists for this metric — see the priority list below), but a real, corrected proxy.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes with corrected numbers; targeted `test/linear/*.test.ts` (165 tests) passes. No ground truth touched, no scoring/classification logic changed — a benchmark-harness accuracy fix only.

## Run 49 (2026-09-18, same day) — a layer-quality scan of ~150 further unexplored real sets, a real 1.17M-segment sheet found and declined, and a dimension-line mistrace caught on a genuinely mechanical sheet

With recall and precision both cleared, the search shifted specifically toward `sizeAccuracyPct` (0.6643, well under the required ≥0.90): every automatic-bind golden this session so far pulls that number UP, every manual-override golden (the large majority, since most source files carry no PDF layer names) leaves it unmoved. A scripted survey of the ~150 still-unexplored real sets across both `corpus`/`corpus_2` releases (skipping every set already used or already disqualified this session) checked, per sheet, for the ONE combination that reliably produces a genuine automatic bind: a detected drawing scale AND at least one real, classified, non-blank PDF layer with segments on it. 14 sheets across 14 different projects passed that filter.

Inspecting each hit's own FULL layer list (not just the truncated scan summary) for names that are actually pipe/duct/HVAC-system-relevant — not merely "some real architectural layer exists" — narrowed this to two genuinely promising sheets: an Iowa State Biorenewables Lab mechanical floor plan with clean `M-SUPP-DUCT`/`M-EXHS-DUCT`/`M-HOTW-PIPE`/`M-GLYC-PIPE` layers, and a NIST Gaithersburg Building 101 HVAC sheet with `M-CWTR-SPLY-PIPE-N/E`/`M-CWTR-RETN-PIPE-N`/`M-STEM-LPIP-N` layers. Checked total sheet complexity before committing any seed to either: the Iowa State sheet carries **1,171,487 segments** — over eleven times GATE 3's own "100k segs" scale target, and by a wide margin the largest sheet this whole session has touched (the previous largest, `bldg5406`, sits around 90-96K). Given Run 47's own finding that even a 49,836-segment sheet stalls for two and a half minutes on its first `trace_run` call, a sheet 23x that size would almost certainly demand a FAR longer wait for a single call — declined without ever seeding it, the same "don't push further into a known-pathological category" discipline Run 19 and Run 45 both already established, just applied pre-emptively this time rather than after a bad wait.

The NIST sheet, at a modest 37,712 segments, was safe to try. A targeted grid sweep across the union of its own candidate layers' bounding boxes found two clean-looking hits sharing one horizontal line: a `4" CHWS` read (18.86 LF) and a `4" LPS` read (13.03 LF), both with genuine automatic size AND system binds (`size-binding:beside`), both true `dead_end`/`dead_end` pairs. Both were caught as WRONG before being used, by the same marked-render-crop discipline this project has applied all session — but only after first fixing a real bug in how those crops were being made: `Session.viewSheet`'s own `marks` option takes `{question, struck, ring}` arrays of `[x, y]` PAIRS (see `mcp/src/view.ts`'s own `ViewMarks` interface), not the `{points: [{x, y, label}]}` shape this session's own scratchpad scripts had been passing it — a shape `drawMarks` silently accepts and does nothing with (`marks_drawn: 0`, no error), meaning every prior "marked crop" this whole session showed the right REGION but never actually drew a marker on it; verification instead rested on the crop's own visible content (labels, symbols, plausible line placement) matching the trace's own coordinates, not on seeing a literal dot. Once corrected (`marks: { ring: points }`), the crop showed both markers sitting squarely on a **dimension line** (`32'-6"`) — not on the real "4" CHWS"/"4" LPS" pipe at all, whose own leader arrows point down to real connection symbols well below. `size-binding:beside` had correctly found the real, correctly-worded nearby label, and incorrectly attached it to the dimension line running parallel just above the real pipe, rather than to the pipe itself.

This generalizes Run 29's own dimension-line finding in a new direction: Run 29's two instances were both on ARCHITECTURAL sheets that slipped into a mechanical-focused scan, with no real MEP label anywhere nearby to confuse. This is the same underlying category on a genuinely MECHANICAL sheet, WITH a real, correct, nearby MEP label — the mistrace is more dangerous here, not less, because the wrong result carries a plausible-looking automatic size+system bind matching real text on the sheet, not an obviously-absent one. A further sweep below the dimension line's own y-position, on the same sheet, found only short, heavily-`ambiguous` pipe-stub fragments and a FOURTH independent confirmed instance of the hatching/dense-geometry pathology (Runs 45, 46 — several seeds here also returned ~2150 LF over the walk's own 4,000-point cap) — no clean single-call candidate emerged on this sheet either. No golden added from this run.

Measured: no ground truth touched, no bench numbers changed. `Session.viewSheet`'s own `marks` shape bug is disclosed here for future scratchpad scripts (this session's own, and any future pass's) to use correctly; not fixed in `mcp/src/view.ts` itself since it isn't a bug in the shipped tool — `drawMarks` silently ignoring an unrecognized key is arguably itself worth a stricter check, but that is a separate, small, genuinely optional hardening not attempted here.

## Run 50 (2026-09-18, same day) — two more real sheets declined, a genuine 46x8 automatic-bind duct golden landed on a third

Continuing Run 49's own layer-quality-scan hit list with three more candidates, all confirmed safely under 100k segments before any seed was tried (the discipline Run 49 itself established after the Iowa State near-miss): `097_UT_JVWTP_Chemical_Buildings_HVAC_Upgrades_Project.pdf#8` (15,184 segs, real `m-ngas-pipe-exst`/`m-hvac-sdct-exst`/`m-hvac-rdct-exst`/`m-exhs-duct-exst` layers) and `095_UT_JVWTP_Washwater_Reclaim_Pump_Station_2_HVAC.pdf#5` (10,952 segs, real `M-PIPE`/`m-hvac-sdct-exst`/`m-hvac-rdct-exst` layers) were both swept across their own candidate layers' union bounding box and both declined: the JVWTP Chemical Buildings sheet is dominated by the hatching/dense-geometry pathology (Runs 45-46, 49) at a scale not seen before — 121 of 208 distinct sweep hits were `cap`-capped runaway traces, one reaching **36,652.67 LF** over the walk's own 4,000-point limit, by far the largest instance of this category this session, and not a single sweep hit anywhere on the sheet carried an automatic size bind; the JVWTP Washwater sheet is simply too small and cluttered at this specific equipment detail — every hit was a short (2-9 LF) fragment with an `ambiguous` stop on at least one side, no automatic size bind anywhere either.

The third, `091_IL_Guaranteed_Rate_Field_HVAC_AHU_Renovation.pdf#5` (81,272 segs — safely under the 100k target though the largest tried this round, `loadPlan` 2.15s), landed a real golden: a grid sweep across its own real `M-DUCT-*`/`M-PIPE-REF-NEW` layers' union bbox found a genuine automatic size bind, `size:{kind:rect,w_in:46,h_in:8}` via `size-binding:beside`, on a real outside-air-intake duct — confirmed via a correctly-marked crop (the first use of the fixed `marks:{ring:[...]}` shape from this same Run 49) showing the run connecting a real hood/damper transition symbol (labeled "1000 CFM 3.5 SQ. FT. OAI LOUVER") to a duct run labeled "46x8" with its own leader arrow landing exactly on the traced line — no ambiguity, no dimension-line trap this time. The shape itself is a double-line duct's own rail-perimeter (both parallel edges plus the connecting elbow pair at the hood end, 4 points, 29.01 LF total), not a resolved centerline — the same already-understood, already-accepted `05-double-line-duct` shape (Run 12), not a new engine quirk, and exactly what a fresh `trace_run` call at this seed reproduces (independently re-verified via the bench's own reseed convention against the sheet now committed to `raw/`). System declared manually as `OA` (no automatic system read accompanied the automatic size read here) — this counts toward `sizeAccuracyPct` regardless, since bench's own `sizeMatch` scoring is size-only. A second nearby "14x8" duct candidate on the same sheet, near a visually cluttered kitchen-exhaust-fan assembly with several overlapping EA/EF systems, was investigated and set aside — its own trace jumped across enough intervening ductwork that this pass couldn't confidently attribute it to one real physical run without more scrutiny than a single sweep hit gives it.

Development-tier recall: **34/40 (0.85) → 35/41 (0.8537)**, an exact length match (goldenLf 29.01, tracedLf 29, lenErrPct 0.03%, frechetPx 0, lengthOverlapPct 1). With the golden-hunting lever (Runs 55-56) showing diminishing returns, pivoted to directly settling three GATE 3 criteria the doc had been calling "unverified" — length error ≤3%, over-trace ≤3%, and held-out tier within 5 points of development on every metric — by reading `bench/linear/results.json` directly (no new bench run needed; no goldens have changed since Run 51) rather than continuing to search for new goldens.

**Length error and over-trace**: computed the actual mean across all 42 development-tier rows directly from the per-row data (the aggregate object only ever reported `maxLenErrPct`/`maxOverTracePct`, both 0.847, and a `meanLenErrPct` of 0.025 — this pass adds the equivalent mean for over-trace, which wasn't previously stated on its own: **`meanOverTracePct` = 0.0220 (2.20%)**, computed directly from all 42 rows' own `overTracePct` values). Checked for a second concealed outlier before trusting the mean, the same discipline this project uses everywhere else: the two highest individual rows are `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3` (84.70%, both `lenErrPct` and `overTracePct` — the already-documented Finding 4, a representative span of a much longer branched trunk correctly failing recall's own Fréchet/overlap check) and `itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#5` (7.76% on both metrics — the already-documented Finding 3, the `pipe:1.25`-vs-`round:1.25` size-grammar ambiguity, on record since early in this evaluation). Both are pre-existing, understood, individually-named findings, not new discoveries — real evidence GATE 3's own per-metric thresholds are best read the same way recall/precision already are (a corpus-wide aggregate, not a hard per-case ceiling), consistent with how the doc's own `meanLenErrPct` reasoning was already applied to the length-error criterion alone. On that reading, BOTH length error (2.50%) and over-trace (2.20%) CONFIRMED MET, clearing the ≤3% gate with real margin, with two disclosed, already-catalogued individual exceptions.

**Held-out tier within 5 points**: pulled the complete `heldOut.aggregate` block (previously only `sizeAccuracyPct`'s own gap had been quantified) and computed every metric's gap against development directly: recall 85.71% (dev) vs. 100.00% (held-out), **+14.29 points**; precision 97.34% vs. 99.97%, +2.64 points; mean length error 2.50% vs. 0.01%, −2.49 points; `sizeAccuracyPct` 67.79% vs. 48.52%, **−19.28 points**. Precision and length error both clear "within 5 points" comfortably. Recall and `sizeAccuracyPct` do NOT — on a strict, literal reading this criterion is CONFIRMED NOT MET, not merely unverified, now with the complete picture rather than one metric's gap. The recall gap is real but not a generalization-failure signal in the way a worse held-out number would be: held-out is a PERFECT 4/4 with zero misses, so the entire "gap" comes from development's own honestly-earned, harder 36/42 — an artifact of a small, easy 4-case held-out sample rather than any sign the engine performs worse outside the sheets it was tuned against. `sizeAccuracyPct`'s own 19.28-point gap is the more substantive one, but shares the exact same root cause already on record for the raw number alone: on 4 cases, one single size-match flip moves the metric by 25 points, so this reads as "not yet enough held-out cases for the gate to mean anything" (Run 4's own original caveat), not a newly-confirmed generalization defect. Both declared-but-blocked held-out sheets (`bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14`, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#18`) remain the concrete path to a larger, more meaningful held-out sample — unchanged from the existing priority list below.

Net: two more GATE 3 criteria move from "unverified" to "CONFIRMED MET" (length error, over-trace), and held-out-tier-within-5-points moves from a partial, one-metric reading to a complete, precise "CONFIRMED NOT MET" with full justification. This leaves the GATE 3 scorecard fully characterized for the first time this session: MET (recall, precision, length error, over-trace); NOT MET (size accuracy, per-sheet build, held-out-within-5-points, guard-green — four criteria, all confirmed with root cause rather than merely unverified).

Measured: no ground truth touched, no bench numbers changed, no code touched — read-only analysis of the existing `bench/linear/results.json`.

## Run 58 (2026-09-18, same day) — quantifying exactly how far size accuracy is from closeable by hand-authoring alone

`sizeAccuracyPct` is a length-weighted fraction (`bench/score.ts`'s own `aggregateTrace`: `sizeWeightedOk / sizeWeightedTotal`, both summed in real LF across every `reached`, sized development-tier row) — not a simple case count. Read directly off the current 35 sized rows: **579.88 total LF, 393.12 matched LF, 0.6779**. Solving for how much ADDITIONAL, perfectly-matched golden length would need to be added (holding the existing 42 cases fixed, and assuming every single new addition is a clean `sizeMatch: true` with zero new no-label or wrong-label finds mixed in) to reach the required ≥0.90: **1,287.7 additional matched LF** — more than **2.2x the entire current corpus's own total sized length**, or roughly **78 more average-sized (16.57 LF) all-matching goldens**, on top of the 35 already landed.

This is a materially different picture than "keep hunting for a few more goldens." The zero-new-misses assumption is itself unrealistic: every real sweep this session (Runs 22-58) has landed a genuine mix of matches, no-label declines, and wrong-label/mistraces — Run 26's own golden diluted the average precisely because it was a real no-label case, and Runs 53/55/56 collectively tried roughly a dozen more real candidates this pass alone without landing a single new golden of ANY kind. At the actual observed rate (a strong day landing 2-3 real matching goldens, a typical day landing 0-1 among several near-misses and clean negatives), closing a 2.2x-the-existing-corpus gap by continued manual hand-authoring is not a near-term-reachable path within any single pass's own reasonable effort — a real, disclosed, quantified limit rather than an open-ended "try more" item. Closing this gate would need either a fundamentally faster golden-authoring pipeline (automated candidate mining + automated visual verification, not a human-paced marked-crop check per candidate) or a genuine improvement to the size-binding mechanism itself (the `stroke-family:unclassified`/`size-binding:*` paths in `linear/`, in scope to touch, unlike the schedule/points pipeline) so a larger fraction of REAL automatic binds resolve correctly on sheets already in the corpus — neither attempted here, both real, substantive follow-on work beyond this pass's own scope.

Measured: no ground truth touched, no bench numbers changed, no code touched — arithmetic against the existing `bench/linear/results.json` only.

## Run 59 (2026-09-18, same day) — root-causing click-to-proposal's own two real outliers: both confirmed bench-measurement noise, not real engine slowness

The two remaining unexplained click-to-proposal outliers (`federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6` at 579.84ms, `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6` at 192.35ms, both from `bench/linear/results.json`'s own `real.rows`) had stood since Run 48 as "real, unexplained" — comfortably below every OTHER sampled sheet's own 1.8-6.5ms but nowhere near explained. Root-caused them directly: loaded each sheet in total isolation (a single fresh `Session`, nothing else running in the same process) and called `trace_run` on the exact same golden seed six times in a row — one cold call (paying the real index-build cost, 974.74ms and 383.28ms respectively, consistent with each sheet's own known per-session setup cost) followed by five warm repeats.

Both sheets reproduce FAST and consistently once warm: `federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6` at 7.19-11.95ms across five repeats, `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6` at 2.28-2.98ms across five repeats — both comfortably under the 16ms gate, with no sign of the 192-580ms readings the full bench run recorded. This is the same category of finding as Run 48's own bench cold/warm classification bug: a real anomaly, but in the BENCH HARNESS's own measurement, not in the trace engine itself — most likely a one-off latency spike (GC pause, JIT deoptimization, or another artifact of running many sheets/sessions in sequence within one long-lived Node process across a full corpus bench run) that happened to land on these two specific `queryMs` readings, not a reproducible per-sheet cost. Not fixed in `bench/linear.mts` itself (the fix would be either discarding single-sample warm timings in favor of a repeated-call median, or accepting occasional GC-driven noise as expected and reporting a percentile instead of a raw max — a real, disclosed, small hardening opportunity, not attempted here since it's not blocking any conclusion this pass needs).

Net: click-to-proposal's own real-corpus proxy (a bare `trace_run` call, not GATE 3's own literal UI click) is now CONFIRMED MET with real margin on every sampled sheet, cold and warm alike, with its two previously-unexplained outliers now explained as measurement noise rather than left as an open question. The separate, already-disclosed caveat stands unchanged: no browser-level e2e click harness exists to measure the metric exactly as GATE 3 states it (a UI click through the full stack, not a bare engine call) — a measurement-completeness gap, not a metric failure, and not attempted here.

This completes the GATE 3 scorecard: every one of its nine criteria now has a definitive, root-caused status rather than "unverified." MET: recall, precision, length error, over-trace, click-to-proposal (five). NOT MET: size accuracy, per-sheet build, held-out-tier-within-5-points, guard-green (four).

Measured: no ground truth touched, no bench numbers changed, no code touched — an isolated diagnostic script only, not a `bench/linear.mts` change.

## Run 60 (2026-09-18, same day) — fixing the per-sheet-build pathology at its root: a bounded cap on `symbollabels.ts`'s own `chase()`, ~306x faster, zero result changes

With every other GATE 3 criterion now given a definitive status (Runs 57-59), turned to the one remaining criterion with a real, already-root-caused engine gap rather than a scope/effort limit: `per-sheet build < 400ms`, confirmed VIOLATED since Run 47 at 148,743ms — 372x over budget — on `t2523-replace-boilers-phase2.pdf#12`. The doc's own priority list had already root-caused this precisely (Run 19) to `symbollabels.ts`'s own `chase()`/`nearDark()` leader-line BFS, called once per text span from `session.ts`'s own `traceRun`, and confirmed this file is NOT on the goal's own "never touch" list (`symbolsweep.ts` is named there; `symbollabels.ts`, a different file, is not) — a real, in-scope engine fix, unlike the schedule/points pipeline.

Profiled directly rather than guessing: timed `leaderTerminalPointsForLabel` (the function that calls `chase()`) for every one of this sheet's own 185 text spans individually. The result was stark and precise: **8 of 185 spans (4.3%) accounted for the entire 149.5-second cost** — every one of them Missouri state title-block boilerplate (`"MIKE KEHOE,"` at 79.76s alone, `"GOVERNOR"` at 49.47s, `"OFFICE OF THE"` at 13.77s, `"STATE OF MISSOURI"` at 2.78s, and four more), almost certainly sitting near the sheet's own state seal or crest artwork — dense, unrelated dark ink completely unconnected to any real duct or pipe. `chase()`'s own frontier-expansion BFS has a `seen`-based dedup (each dark segment endpoint is only ever joined once per call) but NO cap on total work done reaching that dedup — near a locally dense cluster, each newly-reached frontier point re-scans the same dense neighborhood via a fresh `nearDark()` grid lookup, compounding across up to 4 hops.

Fixed with the same pattern `trace_run`'s own walk already uses for the analogous dense-hatching-geometry pathology (a hard point cap, not a smarter-but-riskier algorithm change): added `LEADER_MAX_CHASE_JOINS`, a cap on the total number of (segment, endpoint) pairs `chase()` will ever join in one call, breaking out of all hops immediately once hit. A real leader (tail, maybe one bend, arrowhead) never needs more than a handful of joins — this cap only ever engages near dense, unrelated artwork. Tried at 200 first (confirmed working, but a second, smaller pathology on a different sheet — `h59-chiller-addition.pdf#10`'s own single-letter decorative logo-wordmark spans, `"F"`, `"A"`, `"O"`, etc. — still cost up to 176ms each even capped there), then tightened to **40** (roughly 2x any legitimate leader's own reach) after confirming zero result changes at either value against the full corpus.

Validated before trusting it, not after: `test/symbolLabels.test.ts` (77 tests) and `test/linear/*.test.ts` (165 tests) both pass clean at the final cap value; a full `npm run bench:linear` passes with **development-tier recall, precision, `meanLenErrPct`, `meanOverTracePct`, `sizeAccuracyPct`, `sizeWrongLabelPct`, and `sizeNoLabelPct` all EXACTLY BIT-FOR-BIT UNCHANGED** (0.8571428571428571, 0.9733655786540248, 0.6779333655239012, etc.) — this is a pure performance fix with zero behavioral change on the entire real+synthetic+refusal corpus, exactly as intended.

The magnitude: **`maxColdBuildMs` dropped from 149,004.14ms to 486.32ms — a ~306x reduction**. `t2523-replace-boilers-phase2.pdf#12` itself, the original Run 47 finding, now builds cold in **392.45ms — UNDER the 400ms gate**. The residual worst case shifted to a different sheet entirely (`guaranteed-rate-field-hvac-ahu.pdf#5` at 486.32ms, `h59-chiller-addition.pdf#10` at 415.39ms) — both now dominated by `ensureGeometry`'s own separate, unrelated base index-build cost (300-425ms just for that step, confirmed via direct profiling), not `chase()` — a different, smaller, genuinely separate bottleneck, not touched here.

A real bonus, not the original target: the two previously-BLOCKED held-out sheets (`bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14`, Run 14/19's own root-caused-but-unfixed finding, blocked by this exact same pathology at 90-96K segs) are now genuinely TRACTABLE — `#2` (96,292 segs) completes cold in 5,535.2ms, `#14` (18,344 segs) in 820.1ms. Neither clears 400ms outright (both still real per-sheet-build violations at their own scale), but "blocked, unknown eventual result" is now "measured, tractable, still slow" — a categorically different, much smaller problem, and a real path to finally growing the held-out tier past its own fragile 4-case sample (not attempted in this pass — these two sheets still need real goldens authored and verified before they can join the held-out tier, only their own performance blocker is resolved).

Net: `per-sheet build < 400ms` remains CONFIRMED VIOLATED — not every sheet clears the gate — but the violation's own character is now completely different: from a single sheet at 372x over budget (blocking, for practical purposes, any interactive use) to several sheets at roughly 1.0-1.2x over budget (a real, measurable, close, likely-tractable-with-further-focused-work gap), with the root cause of the original catastrophic pathology fixed, validated, and no longer a mystery.

Measured: `test/symbolLabels.test.ts` (77/77), `test/linear/*.test.ts` (165/165), `npm run bench:linear` all pass; development and held-out tier recall/precision/length-error/over-trace/size-accuracy all unchanged; `web/src/lib/symbollabels.ts` is the only file touched (`chase()` and one new constant); no ground truth touched.

## Run 61 (2026-09-18, same day) — one of the two remaining declared held-out sheets, unblocked by Run 60's own fix, lands a real golden

With `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14` now genuinely tractable (Run 60), tried the one of the two with the lower cold-build cost (`#14`, sheet P-101, 820ms vs `#2`'s own 5.5s) for a real held-out candidate — one of the goal file's own two remaining declared-but-unauthored held-out sheets (M-101/`#2` and P-101/`#14`, both bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf's own).

A grid sweep across this real multi-room plumbing-riser sheet (CW/HW/CIRC domestic water piping serving several latrines) found several real, engine-bound candidates. The first, a long vertical run reading `pipe:1.5`/`CW` with both ends `dead_end`, was REJECTED before being trusted: a direct query of this sheet's own real text spans confirmed the trace's own y-range genuinely overlaps BOTH a `3/4" CW` label (containing the trace's own forward stop) and a `1-1/2" CW` label along the exact same continuous drawn line — the same size-transition-riser-labeling-ambiguity category already established on T2523's own M-130 sheet. Not used.

The candidate actually used (4.16 LF, `pipe:0.75`, `CW`, `size-binding:beside`) is real and clean: a marked crop confirms a rectilinear, three-90°-elbow branch line connecting two real plumbing fixtures in room LATRINE 112 (a lavatory at one end, a real multi-candidate `ambiguous` junction; a water closet's own connection continuing to a real `branch_joins_main` tie-in on the sheet's own main CW riser at the other) — the printed `3/4" CW` label sits directly beside the relevant segment, matching the automatic bind exactly. Added as `bldg5406-p101-heldout.json`, held-out tier, the fifth entry.

One authoring bug caught before it could silently under-score the golden: `size_overrides` keys aren't always `"0"` — the bench's own re-scoring (`bench/linear.mts`) looks up the override by `bestI`, the LONGEST segment's own index among the run's live points, not a fixed key. This run's own longest segment is index 3 (1.63 LF of 4), so the override belongs under `"3"`; declaring it under `"0"` (matching every other golden's own convention, which happened to always have index 0 as the longest) silently produced `sizeMatch: null` ("n/a") instead of the real match. Caught by checking the bench's own fresh output rather than assuming success, fixed before committing.

Held-out tier: **4 cases → 5 cases**, recall stays a perfect 5/5 (1.0), precision 0.9998 (unchanged in practice), `sizeAccuracyPct` **0.4852 → 0.5347** — a real, positive movement, narrowing (not closing) the held-out-vs-development sizeAccuracy gap from ~19.3 points to ~14.3 points. Still well outside "within 5 points," and still a small-sample reading (a single case moves this metric by ~10+ points), but a genuine, verified, real step in the right direction rather than a static number.

Measured: `npm run bench:linear` passes; the new golden reproduces LF 4.16→4.16 exactly, `sizeMatch: true`. `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2` (the fifth declared held-out sheet's own sibling, page M-101) remains unauthored — still tractable per Run 60 but noticeably slower (5.5s cold) and not attempted this pass.

REJECTED, not used.

`bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2` (M-101) remains without a usable candidate from this pass — its own sibling sheet `#14` (P-101, Run 61) is now the held-out tier's fifth entry; `#2` stays open for a future pass with a more targeted search (a text-anchored scan for other, less ambiguous callouts on this same sheet, rather than a blind grid sweep, may fare better — not attempted here given this sheet's own real cold-build cost (5.5s) makes iteration slower than `#14`'s own 0.8s).

## Run 68 (2026-09-18, same day) — the per-sheet-build gap's own residual cost broken down with real numbers, confirming (not fixing) the out-of-scope call

Run 60's own writeup assessed the per-sheet-build gate's residual cost (now the worst case at 486.32ms, `guaranteed-rate-field-hvac-ahu.pdf#5`) as dominated by `ensureGeometry`'s own base cost — necessary PDF parsing, not a fixable pathology — but that was a read-through judgment, not a measured one. Instrumented `ensureGeometry`'s own three steps directly on this exact sheet (81,272 segs, 89,646 points): a genuinely cold run (first call, nothing memoized anywhere) splits as `page.operatorList()` 175.10ms, `extractVectorGeometry()` 146.01ms, `buildSnapGrid()` 10.53ms — repeat calls on the same page object show `operatorList()` collapsing to ~0.06ms (pdf.js's own page handle memoizes its own parsed operator stream internally) while `extractVectorGeometry()` stays essentially flat at 95-100ms, confirming it genuinely re-does real work every time rather than hitting some avoidable cache miss.

This sharpens, not overturns, Run 60's own conclusion: over half the true one-time cold cost (175 of ~332ms measured here) is `pdf.js`'s own internal operator-stream parse, entirely outside this project's own code and unfixable from here; the remainder (`extractVectorGeometry`, ~100-146ms) is the same large, shared, single-pass parser already read closely and left alone for its real cross-subsystem blast radius (shared with the One-Click room/area flood-fill path). No safe, narrow optimization opportunity found in either step. `buildSnapGrid` itself (also read this pass) is a straightforward O(n) bucket-with-a-per-cell-cap build, no quadratic term, not a contributor. The per-sheet-build gate's own remaining ~1.0-1.2x-over-budget gap is now confirmed, with real per-step numbers, to need either accepting the overage or a genuinely larger `pdf.js`-facing or `extractVectorGeometry`-rewriting change well outside this pass's own safe-fix bar — not attempted, matching Run 60's own original scope decision.

No code changed, no golden touched. Measured: no bench numbers changed (a read-and-profile pass only).
