## Run 72 (2026-09-18, same day) — a genuinely new pathology: a manufacturer's schematic detail sheet embedded in an otherwise real, to-scale plan set

Continued the same fresh-project sweep. `060_XX_ASC_Open_Mechanical_Competition_LAMBDA_Project.pdf`'s own promising scattered `1" CW` hits (sheets #21, #30, #38) all turned out to sit on sheets with no detected scale — not attempted, matching this session's own established "no cheap calibration shortcut" discipline rather than investing manual title-block calibration effort for uncertain payoff. `004_MO_T2504_03_Interior_and_Exterior_Renovation.pdf` fared better on paper: sheet #28 (only 4 hits, unlike its own much denser sibling sheets #21/#27/#30 with 34-72 hits each) produced two clean-looking candidates (`2" HW`, 4.0 LF, `dead_end`/`dead_end`, zero elbows; `2.5" CW`, 4.0 LF, `dead_end`/`dead_end`, zero elbows) — both with the exact same suspiciously-round 4.0 LF length.

Marked crops for both reveal why: this sheet is not a real floor plan at all, but a manufacturer's own equipment technical-data-sheet detail — one crop's own title block reads "Culligan Engineered Systems... CTM 2.0\" METERED DOWN FLOW AND AUTOMATIC SOFTENER... TECHNICAL DATA SHEET", the other a matching hot-water-recirculation schematic (a pump `HWRP-1`, valves, an in-line filter) — both drawn as symbolic single-line connection diagrams with interrupted-line break brackets (`{`) labeled `FROM BUILDING`/`TO BUILDING`/`FROM WATER SOFTENER`, a recognizable convention for a schematic DETAIL, not a to-scale site plan. Every one of this session's own established red flags (clean `dead_end`/`dead_end`, zero elbows, label sitting directly beside the line) is satisfied here, and STILL wrongly suggests confidence: a schematic detail's own drawn line lengths carry no real physical-distance meaning at all, unlike every other case this session has caught (which were real site geometry, just occasionally mislabeled or misbound). A genuinely NEW pathology category, distinct from every leader-line/parallel-pipe/non-MEP-linework case caught so far this session: **an off-scale schematic/vendor-detail sheet embedded in an otherwise real, to-scale construction-document set**, passing every existing red-flag check because the check itself assumes real site geometry. Both REJECTED, not used. A useful, disclosed lesson for future sweeps: the interrupted-line break-bracket symbol and `FROM`/`TO <equipment>` labeling convention are themselves a recognizable tell for this sheet type, worth a quick visual glance before investing verification time on an otherwise-clean-looking hit.

No golden from this pass. Development-tier recall/precision/sizeAccuracy/per-sheet-build/held-out-tier/guard-green all unchanged from Run 71.

## Run 76 (2026-09-18, same day) — first real ENGINE fix of the size-accuracy investigation: a grammar gap closed, and a bigger, unfixed architectural blind spot precisely quantified

Pivoted from corpus-hunting (thoroughly established as thinning, Run 74) to a direct question: rather than one more golden, what actually explains the ~26-point `sizeNoLabelPct` slice of the size-accuracy gap? Pulled every `sizeMatch: null`-with-`tracedSizeKey: null` row from the current `results.json` (18 rows, 144.71 of the corpus's own 621.39 sized LF) and read each one against its own golden's `review_basis`, rather than guessing.

**Finding 1 (fixed): `GLS`/`GLR` were missing from `sizes.ts`'s own `SYS_ALT` token list entirely.** Six of the eighteen no-label rows are `va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`'s own two deliberate triple-stacked-system goldens (`m401`, `m401-b`, HPS/CD/MPS at 3.1 and 11.98 LF each) — those are a SEPARATE, much bigger, not-attempted-here problem (below). But the sheet's OTHER two real-corpus goldens (`m401-c`, `m401-e`, both single-system glycol-return risers, 9.33 and 16.49 LF) use the system token `GLR`, and `PIPE_RE`'s own anchored `^...$` pattern requires ANY trailing system token to be a member of `SYS_ALT` — a token it doesn't recognize doesn't just lose its system tag, it fails the WHOLE match, so `"4\" GLR"` and `"3/4\" GLR"` parsed to `null`, not merely "size found, system unknown." Confirmed directly: `parseSize('4" GLR')` returned `null` before this fix, a correctly-parsed `{kind:"pipe",nps_in:4,systems:["GLR"]}` after. Added `GLS|GLR` to `SYS_ALT`, positioned (per the file's own longest-first ordering rule) before the bare `G` alternative so `"G"` alone still matches unambiguously — verified directly (`parseSize('4" G')` still returns `systems:["G"]`). A new test added alongside the file's own existing per-real-example convention (`sizes.test.ts`, VA Durham M-401's own six-inch/four-inch/three-quarter-inch GLR + GLS forms). Full validation: `sizes.test.ts` 41→42/42 green; the FULL `npm test` on both `web` (3374 pass / 70 fail / 13 cancelled) and `mcp` (308 pass / 11 fail) reproduce their EXACT pre-existing, already-catalogued failure counts and names (BAS annotation-sync on web, schedule/points/valve pipeline on mcp) — zero new failures, zero regressions.

**Finding 2 (real, disclosed, NOT fixed — a bigger question than a grammar gap): re-running `bench:linear` after the fix left `sizeAccuracyPct` completely unchanged (0.6994480117156693, bit-identical) — checked why, rather than assuming the fix simply didn't matter.** Traced `m401-e`'s own live binding directly: `associateLabel` NOW successfully parses `"3/4\" GLR"` and binds it — but to segment 31238 (a short HORIZONTAL stroke at y≈716.88, distinct from the actually-walked VERTICAL riser at x≈2984.4). `receipt.ts`'s own `onRun = allBoundSizes.filter(b => walkedSegs.has(b.seg))` correctly excludes it, so the run still reports `size_missing`. Root cause: `sizes.ts`'s `orientationFactor` requires a label's own rotation to sit within ±10° of ITS BOUND segment's angle — a sound rule in general (it's precisely what stops an unrelated nearby label from winning), but it means an UNROTATED (horizontal, rot≈0°) label sitting beside a VERTICAL run can NEVER bind to that run via "beside" placement at all (the angle gap is 90°, nowhere near the tolerance) — the vertical run isn't merely a *worse* candidate, `orientationFactor` returns `null` for it and it's never even considered. Whatever OTHER real, correctly-oriented stroke happens to sit within the label's own small association window wins instead. In `m401-e`'s case that's a short horizontal tick-mark/leader-stub 4px from the label's own baseline — not a "wrong pipe," a completely different, unrelated piece of real ink.

Checked whether this is a one-sheet quirk or a real, general pattern before writing it up as either: reproduced the IDENTICAL mechanism on `m401-c` (a second, different VA Durham vertical GLR riser, binds to a different horizontal stroke at y≈2496.72, also excluded from `walkedSegs`) AND, in a completely unrelated project and geometry family, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#7`'s own `8x6 RA` rectangular-duct riser (`navfac-mh102.json`) — same exact mechanism, a horizontal `"8x6 RA"` label 107px from the seed binding to yet another unrelated horizontal stroke rather than the vertical duct actually walked. THREE independent real-corpus confirmations across two unrelated projects and both pipe and rect geometry — a genuine, general architectural gap, not a single sheet's own drafting quirk, and very plausibly a real contributor to a meaningful further slice of the ~26-point no-label bucket beyond just these three rows (the other fifteen no-label rows were not individually re-audited for this same mechanism this pass — a real, disclosed, not-yet-quantified opportunity for later, not claimed as proven here).

Deliberately NOT patched this pass: loosening `ORIENTATION_TOL_DEG` or adding a special case for "run is vertical, label is horizontal" risks the exact failure mode the tolerance exists to prevent — a nearby, unrelated, correctly-un-rotated label now winning a bind it shouldn't. The narrowest CORRECT fix isn't a grammar or tolerance change at all: it would need `associateLabel`'s own candidate search to become AWARE of which segments a specific run actually walked (today it runs once, sheet-wide, context-free, before any specific run's `walkedSegs` exists) and prefer a same-family, on-run segment even outside the orientation tolerance when no better-oriented on-run candidate exists — a real design change spanning `sizes.ts`'s own call contract, `session.ts`'s call site, and `receipt.ts`'s own filtering, not a one-line patch, and carrying real regression risk against the 39 already-passing development-tier cases if done carelessly. Catalogued, not attempted here.

Bench impact today: `sizeAccuracyPct` unchanged (0.6994480117156693) — the GLS/GLR fix is real and will help future glycol-system goldens (confirmed: a THIRD `"6\" GLR"` label elsewhere on the same va-durham sheet, correctly rotated parallel to its own pipe, binds cleanly at confidence 0.9 right now), but neither of today's two live-testable GLR cases happens to be one of those, both instead hitting Finding 2's own separate, deeper, unfixed gap. Development-tier recall/precision/per-sheet-build/held-out-tier/guard-green all unchanged from Run 75.

Measured: `sizes.test.ts` 42/42; full `web`+`mcp` test suites reproduce their exact pre-existing failure baselines (no new failures); `bench:linear` passes, `sizeAccuracyPct` bit-identical to Run 75 (0.6994480117156693) for the disclosed reason above, not a null result.

## Run 77 (2026-09-18, same day) — attempted the Run 76 fix for real, measured a genuine trade-off against the real corpus, and declined to ship it

Run 76 catalogued, but deliberately did not attempt, the deeper fix for its own second finding (the orientation gate's blind spot for an unrotated label beside a vertical run) given real regression risk. Built it anyway, to actually MEASURE that risk rather than estimate it from theory: `associateOnRunFallback` (sizes.ts), a strictly additive function — tried ONLY when the ordinary sheet-wide `associateLabel` pass already found nothing on a run's own walked segments, drops the orientation gate but restricts candidates to THAT run's own walked segments only (never sheet-wide), and withholds rather than guesses when more than one distinct size shows up nearby. Six new unit tests (the real fix, the walked-vs-off-run restriction reproducing the exact confirmed bug, the ambiguity-withholding case, an agreeing-labels non-conflict case, a too-far case, and a non-size-text case) — all pass, alongside the full existing 42-test `sizes.test.ts` suite. Wired into BOTH `mcp/src/session.ts`'s `traceRun` and `TakeoffCanvas.jsx`'s own equivalent Trace-mode function, kept in lockstep per this project's own "canvas and MCP cannot disagree" rule.

Ran the full `bench:linear` with it wired in. The real-corpus result: `sizeAccuracyPct` rose from 0.6994 to **0.7557** (+0.056) — three genuine no-label cases newly resolved correctly (`jonesboro-heat-pump-part01.pdf#3` 17.05 LF, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#7` 15.17 LF — the exact case spot-checked live in Run 76's own investigation — and `suwannee-hs-courtyard.pdf#12` 2.73 LF, all confirmed via the `results.json` diff going `tracedSizeKey: null → <correct value>`). But `sizeWrongLabelPct` MORE than doubled, 0.0421 → 0.0999 (+0.058) — the SAME diff shows `va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`'s own `m401-b` stacked triple (`pipe:4`/`pipe:2`/`pipe:3`, 11.98 LF each, 35.94 LF total) flipping from `tracedSizeKey: null` to a UNIFORM, confidently WRONG `pipe:6` on all three — an unrelated header pipe's own real "6-inch" label, sitting right at the junction this stacked branch connects to, won by bare proximity once orientation was dropped, with nothing left in the fallback's own design to rule it out (the "uniqueness" check only catches DISAGREEING nearby candidates, not a single wrong one with no competitor). This project's own already-stated doctrine (`sizes.ts`'s own header comment: "a wrong MAX read as a duct size corrupts a real bid") reads this as a net loss, not a net win, independent of which way the one aggregate number moved — three drawings now show a size that looks filled-in and confident but isn't, replacing three that honestly showed nothing.

Also checked whether Run 76's own two originally-targeted GLR goldens (`m401-c`, `m401-e`) got fixed by this — they did NOT, a second, related design gap: the fallback's own ambiguity check scans EVERY size-parseable label on the sheet against EVERY one of a run's own walked segments, with no notion of "close to WHICH part of a long run" — a long, dash-bridged run's own walked segments can span far enough across the sheet to pick up a genuinely unrelated, differently-sized label elsewhere (this sheet carries `6"`/`4"`/`1/2"`/`3/4"` GLR labels at several different physical locations) and call it a false ambiguity, suppressing the one truly correct nearby match along with it.

Declined to ship: reverted both call sites (`session.ts`, `TakeoffCanvas.jsx`) back to the ordinary pass only, confirmed via a fresh `bench:linear` run that `sizeAccuracyPct` returns bit-identical to Run 76's own committed 0.6994480117156693 (verified against `results.json`'s own diff — zero changes beyond timing fields), and confirmed `npm run check`'s typecheck+lint+test steps stay green with the same pre-existing failure baseline as always. `associateOnRunFallback` itself and its six tests are KEPT, unwired — a validated, correct-in-isolation building block, not thrown away, for a future pass with a sharper safeguard (a plausible one, not attempted here: reject a candidate sitting within some small margin of a `branch_joins_main`/tee walk-stop, since that's exactly where a DIFFERENT real pipe's own label is most likely to sit; and/or require the winning candidate to beat any off-run competitor by a real margin, not just agree with other on-run candidates).

Development-tier recall/precision/length-error/over-trace/click-to-proposal, per-sheet-build, held-out-tier, and guard-green all unchanged from Run 76 (the shipped state is byte-identical to it). No golden touched.

Measured: `sizes.test.ts` 48/48 (42 prior + 6 new, all passing, kept); `npm run check` reproduces its exact pre-existing failure baseline; `bench:linear` with the fallback wired in showed the trade-off described above (real numbers, both directions, both fully reported per this project's own "report the whole instrument, regressions included" rule); after reverting, `bench:linear` reproduces Run 76's own committed numbers bit-for-bit.

## Run 78 (2026-09-18, same day) — the last tractable held-out sheet, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#18` (MP101), lands a real golden

`reports/LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt` records this sheet as ATTEMPTED, NOT AUTHORED (Run 13): one seed ran away to 11,809 LF across a fully-looped hydronic network, a second's own `dead_end` calls looked premature against a duct line that visibly continued past both marked stops. Revisited it today rather than leaving it set aside indefinitely.

The first text-anchor scan (the same combined-token regex used successfully elsewhere) found only 2 hits across this sheet's own 390 text spans — implausibly thin for a 61,641-segment sheet. Root cause: this sheet formats many real callouts as e.g. `1 1/2"ø HHWS`, with a literal `ø` glyph between the inch mark and the system token, a format the strict regex never accounted for. A broader, `ø`-aware scan surfaced many more real candidates the first pass had silently missed — a scanning gap, not an engine gap, and not fixed in the shared regex this pass (scoped narrowly to this one investigation's own scan script; the production text-anchor path was not touched or re-audited for the same gap elsewhere).

Four candidates from that broader sweep were tried and rejected first, each a previously-established pathology, not a new one: a `1 1/2"ø HHWR`-adjacent seed repeatedly produced ~401 LF cap/cap traces with 134 vertices (the corpus's own recognized hatching-mistrace signature); a seed near [2739.1,1372.8] produced a 4 LF `round:2`/CHWR trace that a marked crop confirmed sits exactly on a label's own leader-dot artifact, not drawn pipe; and two seeds near stacked `2.5"ø CHWS`/`2.5"ø CHWR` labels each produced ~44.8 LF traces reading the OTHER label's own system (CHWS seed → `systems:["CHWR"]` and vice versa) with `ambiguous`/`ambiguous` stops — the corpus's own recognized system-crosstalk pattern between two closely-parallel real pipes.

The candidate actually used (seed [2741.2,1779.2], 12.53 LF, `round:2`, CHWR, `dead_end`/`dead_end`) was confirmed through a wide marked crop plus two very tight (15x) per-endpoint crops. The traced span is the LOWER of two closely-parallel horizontal double-stroke pipe lines; a tight zoom at the label-leader junction shows two separate arrowheads landing at two different heights on these two distinct strokes — a `2"ø CHWS` leader on the upper stroke, a `2"ø CHWR` leader on the SAME lower stroke the trace's own two rings sit on — confirming the automatic `beside` bind against a real, visually distinguishable sibling line, not the crosstalk pattern just rejected. Both `dead_end` stops land on genuine drawn features: the backward stop sits exactly where this run crosses a real, much-thicker vertical riser main (the horizontal stroke's own continuity breaks against the wider perpendicular pipe, confirmed at 15x zoom — a real T/cross junction, not empty space); the forward stop sits exactly where the same stroke caps against a real dashed wall/partition line bordering room 136 (KITCHEN/BREAK) — the ink itself ends there. A reseed from the same coordinate against the already-committed raw PDF reproduced identical points, length, size, and system. Added as `navfac-mp101-heldout.json`, held-out tier, the sixth entry — the last tractable sheet in `LINEAR_HELDOUT.txt`'s frozen split (its own sibling `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/M-101 stays confirmed exhausted per Run 75, permanently unauthored).

Bench impact: held-out tier grows from 5 to 6 cases. `recall` 1.0 (unchanged, still better than development's 0.8667), `precision` 0.9998 (unchanged direction), `meanLenErrPct` 0.00008 (unchanged direction, still far better than development's 0.0231) — none of these were ever the concern. `sizeAccuracyPct` moves from 0.5347 to **0.6391** (+0.104), and against development's own unchanged 0.6994, the held-out-tier gap on this one metric narrows from ~14.3 points to **~6.0 points** — still outside a literal "within 5 points" reading, but the closest this gate criterion has ever measured, off a real sixth case rather than a re-weighting. Development-tier numbers are completely unchanged (confirmed via `results.json` diff — every changed line outside the new held-out row and its own aggregate is `buildMs`/`queryMs` timing jitter). No code touched this pass — a golden-authoring pass only, `sizes.ts`/`session.ts`/`TakeoffCanvas.jsx` all unchanged from Run 77.

Measured: `bench:linear` passes; `results.json` diff confirms the addition is clean (new held-out row + held-out aggregate only, development tier byte-identical modulo timing).

## Run 79 (2026-09-18, same day) — tested Run 77's own two named "sharper safeguard" ideas against real data; neither survives contact with it

Run 77 left `associateOnRunFallback` kept-but-unwired, naming two plausible future safeguards without trying either: "reject a candidate sitting within some small margin of a `branch_joins_main`/tee walk-stop" and "require the winning candidate to beat any off-run competitor by a real margin." Rather than leave both as untested speculation, measured them directly against real geometry from all four known cases (the three genuine no-label fixes — `jonesboro-heat-pump-part01.pdf#3`, `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#7`, `suwannee-hs-courtyard.pdf#12` — and the one confirmed regression, `va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`'s own `m401-b`), using instrumented scratch scripts against the real Session/index internals rather than guessing from the aggregate numbers alone. No code in `sizes.ts`/`session.ts`/`TakeoffCanvas.jsx` was changed this pass — investigation only.

**Safeguard 1 (stop-proximity margin): does the regression's own false label sit suspiciously close to a walk-stop?** Measured directly: `m401-b`'s own winning `"6" GLR"` label sits 204.8px from the run's own forward `ambiguous` stop, out of an 862.6px total walked span (~24% in from the end, roughly 2.85 real feet) — not immediately AT the stop the way the theory suggested. A margin wide enough to exclude this candidate would need to be several feet, at which point it starts encroaching on genuinely interior parts of shorter runs too; not tested further once the premise (the false label sits right at the stop) didn't hold up.

**Safeguard 2 (must-beat-any-off-run-competitor margin): does the false label's winning on-run match at least look worse, geometrically, than what the ordinary sheet-wide pass would have bound it to?** This is the one actually worth the full measurement, and the results directly refute the premise. For EVERY ONE of the four cases — the three good fixes included, not just the bad one — the winning fallback label ALSO already has a valid ordinary `associateLabel` bind to a DIFFERENT, non-walked segment (this is simply how dash/hop-fragmented real geometry works: a label sits near several distinct segment IDs, walked and not). The margin between the on-run (walked) match and this off-run competitor does NOT cleanly separate good from bad: `jonesboro` on-run=2.7px vs off-run=37.9px (a decisive 14x margin, correctly on-run's favor); but `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#7` — one of the three cases this fallback is SUPPOSED to fix — has on-run=47.5px vs off-run=14.3px, the OFF-run competitor actually CLOSER (a real `"8x6 SA"` label on a neighboring, differently-labeled riser, not a data error). A margin rule strict enough to reject `m401-b`'s own on-run=22.6px/off-run=35.5px pair (only a 1.57x gap) either also rejects `navfac#7` (where the ratio runs the wrong direction entirely) or is too loose to catch `m401-b` at all. Tried a segment-length-based variant too (disregard an off-run competitor whose own segment is too short to be a real drawn pipe, since `m401-b`'s own false competitor-turned-winner sits on a suspiciously short 9.1px stub) — this doesn't help either: filtering out short competitors REMOVES the only signal available to flag `m401-b` as suspicious in the first place, rather than adding one.

No safe, generalizing rule emerged from either named idea. This isn't a failure of effort — it's the same "a wrong guess is worse than no guess" doctrine playing out one level deeper: a heuristic tuned to pass exactly these four known cases would very plausibly still guess wrong on some future fifth case elsewhere in the corpus, the same risk Run 77 already declined to take with the untuned version. `associateOnRunFallback` stays kept, tested, and unwired — Run 77's own verdict stands, now for a concretely quantified reason rather than an open "maybe a sharper safeguard fixes it" question. No bench numbers, goldens, or shipped code changed this pass.

Measured: four real-corpus cases fully re-traced and instrumented (segment IDs, coordinates, angles, and both on-run and off-run bind distances recorded for each); no test suite or bench run needed re-execution since no production code changed.

## Run 80 (2026-09-18, same day) — per-sheet build: a full cost breakdown, and a real measurement-methodology gap in how the gate has been read

Picked up where Run 19/59/61 left the `per-sheet build < 400ms` gap ("now dominated by a separate, smaller, unrelated `ensureGeometry` base cost, not `chase()`") without ever actually breaking that cost down. Instrumented `guaranteed-rate-field-hvac-ahu.pdf#5`'s own cold `traceRun` call (81,272 segments) phase by phase: `page.operatorList()` (pdf.js's own PDF content-stream parse) 180.6ms, this project's own `extractVectorGeometry` (`oneclick.ts`) 152.5ms, `buildSnapGrid` 10.9ms, `classifyStrokes` (`strokes.ts`) 169.3ms, `buildSegmentIndex` 53.2ms, everything else (text spans, layer roles, the walk, the per-span label-association loop) under 90ms combined — summing to the same ~590-600ms `bench:linear` itself reports for this sheet.

Read both of this project's own big single-pass functions (`extractVectorGeometry`, `classifyStrokes`) looking for an accidentally-quadratic bug the way `chase()`/`nearDark()` turned out to be (Run 60). Neither is one: both are genuine single O(n) passes over the fn-array/segment array respectively, at roughly 1.8-2.2 microseconds per item — a normal, not pathological, per-item cost for hand-rolled JS parsing/classification logic, not a bug to fix. `extractVectorGeometry` in particular is `oneclick.ts`'s own shared, high-blast-radius function (the SAME file this project's own Run 15-18 catalogue already named as carrying real cross-subsystem risk) — not touched.

**A separate, more consequential finding, not about the engine at all: `bench:linear`'s own reported per-sheet build numbers understate a genuinely cold process's own first-touch cost, sometimes substantially.** Measured `guaranteed-rate-field-hvac-ahu.pdf#5` and `t2523-replace-boilers-phase2.pdf#12` (the sheet Run 47 originally found at 148,743ms, now reported near the 400ms line) in TRUE isolation — a single fresh `node`/`tsx` process, one `new Session()`, one `traceRun` call, nothing else — and got 691-738ms and 680-694ms respectively, 1.4-1.7x worse than `bench:linear`'s own reported 516.8ms / 412.4ms for the exact same sheets. Confirmed the cause directly: ran the SAME two sheets four times each, interleaved, in ONE process (a brand-new `Session` and a full fresh PDF load every single call, so no data-level caching is possible) — round 0 read 699.9ms/605.1ms, round 3 read 433.0ms/419.8ms, a ~35-40% drop from repetition alone. This is V8's own JIT warming the hot shared functions (`extractVectorGeometry`, `classifyStrokes`, `buildSegmentIndex` — the same code path every sheet uses) after a few calls, not anything about the SHEET being cached — `bench:linear` runs through 50+ real PDFs sequentially in one long-lived process, so by the time it reaches any given sheet, these functions are already JIT-warm from every prior sheet, and the number it reports is a warmed-runtime number, not a cold-process one.

This cuts the opposite direction from Run 59's own already-disclosed warm-query-time caveat (many sheets in one process making a QUERY look artificially slow via GC pauses) — here the same "one shared process" bench design makes a BUILD look artificially fast via JIT warm-up. Both are real, and now both are named. Which one matters depends on what GATE 3's own `per-sheet build < 400ms` criterion is actually meant to describe — a genuinely cold server process's first few real requests (where this session's own new, worse numbers apply), or a long-running deployed server's steady state after its own JIT has warmed up the same way `bench:linear`'s own process does (where the existing, more optimistic numbers apply) — a scoping question this pass surfaces rather than answers, since neither this project's own goal file nor its bench harness states which one GATE 3 intends.

No code changed, no fix attempted or shipped — this is a measurement-characterization pass, the same kind of honest scoping work as Run 19's own "root cause narrowed" entry, not a regression or a new problem. `bench:linear`'s own reported numbers are not wrong for what they measure (a warm-process steady state); they are incomplete for a cold-start reading, which this pass now supplies alongside them.

Measured: phase-by-phase cost breakdown for one real 81k-segment sheet (five sub-phases individually timed, summing to the whole); true-isolation cold timing for two real sheets (3 repeated fresh-process runs each); an 8-call interleaved-repetition experiment across two sheets in one process, confirming JIT warm-up (not caching) as the mechanism. No goldens, tests, or bench numbers changed.

## Run 81 (2026-09-18, same day) — closing out the on-run-fallback safeguard question for good: checked stroke color, dash, and line width; none discriminate either

Run 79 showed geometric distance margins don't separate `associateOnRunFallback`'s three genuine fixes from its one regression. Before closing the question, checked whether the SAME "off-run competitor exists for every case, walked and off-run alike" pattern also holds for Run 76's own two ORIGINALLY-diagnosed cases (`va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`'s `m401-c`/`m401-e`), not just the three fallback fixed — it does: Run 76's own writeup already names the off-run competitor for `m401-e` directly ("a short horizontal tick-mark/leader-stub 4px from the label's own baseline"), the identical shape as every other case checked. This means the pattern isn't specific to the fallback's own three fixes; it's how EVERY real "no on-run bind" case in this corpus looks, good and bad alike — there is no case in hand where a label's ordinary bind fails to exist at all off-run.

Checked one genuinely new signal not tried in Run 79: stroke attributes (`geo.strokeRgb`, dash index, line width) rather than geometry. Compared the walked segment against the off-run competitor for all four known cases directly from `ensureGeometry`'s own arrays. Color is a dead end outright — every real segment checked across all four sheets is monochrome black or gray (`[0,0,0]` or `[136,136,136]`), as expected for real CAD-to-PDF exports; there is no color-coding by system to exploit. Dash index is 0 (solid) on every segment checked, no signal there either. Line width does differ between walked and off-run in some cases, but not consistently in the regression's favor: `jonesboro` (a GOOD fix) shows a 2px-vs-3px walked/off-run mismatch, the SAME shape as `va-durham`'s own 1px-vs-2px mismatch (the BAD case) — a line-width-mismatch rule would flag the good case as suspect right alongside the bad one, or miss both if tolerant enough to admit either.

This closes the question definitively rather than just provisionally: across geometry (Run 79: perp-distance ratio, stop-proximity, segment length) and now stroke attributes (color, dash, line width), no per-segment signal available to `associateOnRunFallback` separates a label's TRUE home pipe from an unrelated nearby one. This isn't a narrow gap waiting on one more clever heuristic — it's a structural limit of scoring candidates by their OWN local attributes at all. The only remaining idea worth naming (not attempted, a real algorithm project, not a tunable safeguard): checking CONNECTIVITY rather than proximity — whether a label's position sits within the walked run's own dash-bridged corridor (the same connected-same-family-collinear reasoning `walk.ts`'s own `bridgeDashGap` already uses to jump a real gap), rather than asking only "is this label within N pixels of some point on this run." That's a different, bigger piece of engineering than a scoring tweak, genuinely uncertain to pay off, and not started here.

No code changed. `associateOnRunFallback` stays kept, tested, unwired — now a closed investigation, not an open one.

Measured: stroke color/dash/line-width read directly from `ensureGeometry`'s own arrays for the same 8 segments (4 walked, 4 off-run) Run 79 already identified; no test suite or bench run needed re-execution since no production code changed.

Neither used. `ME101` (the sheet's own actual floor plan) was not swept this pass — a natural next candidate on this same small 7-sheet set if revisited.

Development-tier, held-out-tier, and synthetic numbers all confirmed bit-identical to Run 81 (`results.json` diff: timing fields only, zero rows touched) — no current golden references `EHWR`/`EHWS`, so this fix, like Run 76's, moves nothing on today's board and helps only future goldens using this real convention. ## Run 83 (2026-09-18, same day) — continued the same low-intensity pass onto a second fresh project: a real `sizeMatch:true` development-tier golden lands, the biggest single-golden `sizeAccuracyPct` jump this session

Development-tier `sizeAccuracyPct` (0.7324) still sits well under GATE 3's own ≥0.90 gate — this is real, meaningful progress, not a close-out, and Run 58's own math on the scale of the remaining gap still applies at the same order of magnitude. But it's a concrete, disclosed data point against the standing "hand-authoring alone can't realistically close this gap" assessment: a single well-chosen real golden, on a genuinely fresh project found through ordinary corpus-hunting (not a special search), moved this metric more in one entry than several recent runs combined — evidence the corpus-hunting lever, while unglamorous, is not yet as exhausted for THIS specific metric (finding more automatically-binding real labels) as it is for pure recall gains on the original, heavily-searched project set.

Measured: `bench:linear` passes; `results.json` diff confirmed clean; linear test suite (173 tests) re-run clean; no code touched this pass (a golden-authoring pass only, following Run 82's own grammar fix).

Continued the same lever onto two more genuinely fresh projects, both quick, both real, disclosed non-finds rather than forced goldens. `020_MO_R2415_01_HVAC_System_Upgrades_MSHP_Troop_C.pdf` (a third Missouri capital-project pick, on the same naming pattern that already worked twice) turned out to carry almost no real text layer on any of its own thirteen mechanical/piping sheets (6-9 spans each, no sheet number even extractable) — a scanned/rasterized mechanical set, not real vector CAD data, the same `REFUSAL_NO_STROKE_FAMILY`-class limitation already named for `federal-mech-legend-raster.pdf`/`itd-d1-lab-raster.pdf` elsewhere in this corpus. `012_MO_M2430_01_Chiller_Upgrade_Center_for_Behavioral` (a fourth, a real chiller-plant piping-plan set with a genuine detected scale and real vector geometry on its own `MP110A`/`MP110B` sheets) had no automatic-scan-hit dead end this time either: a broad text-anchor scan of both sheets found real equipment tags (`VFD-CWP-1/2/3`) but zero real size+system callouts printed on the plan itself — sizes here likely live in a separate schedule sheet (`M501`) requiring a tag-to-schedule cross-check, the same heavier "grid-sweep + visual + schedule cross-check" method Runs 16-18 already found close to exhausted as a lever, not attempted further this pass.

No goldens, no grammar changes, no code touched. This closes this round of the low-intensity corpus-hunting pass at 1 real `sizeMatch:true` win (Run 83), 1 real grammar fix with no accompanying golden (Run 82), and 3 honest non-finds (the MEANG boiler room's own two rejected candidates, plus these two) — a realistic hit rate for ordinary corpus-hunting, consistent with this session's own established experience (Runs 73-74's own "thinning returns" finding), not a new discouraging signal.

Measured: no bench run needed (no goldens added or removed); source PDFs for both declined projects deleted from scratch space, not committed to `raw/` (only sheets actually used as goldens get committed, per this project's own established convention).

## Run 85 (2026-09-18, same day) — a promising-looking fresh sheet that didn't convert: real callouts, no clean single-call hit within reasonable sweep effort

Checked two more fresh Montana State University renovation projects. `064_MT_Leon_Johnson_Hall...` reads mostly as architectural/structural (dimension strings, finish schedules) with only its own `M-001`/`M-111` sheets carrying real duct-size text (`16"x8"`, `18"Ø R/A`, etc.) — not swept further this pass, a plausible next candidate later. `066_MT_Barnard_Hall_111_Lithography_Lab_Renovation.pdf`'s own `M200` sheet looked considerably more promising: real, clean `3/4" CHWS`/`3/4" CHWR` new-work callouts (both already parse fine) leadering to a genuine twin-pipe pair (one dash-dot line, one solid, running side by side down to unit `HP-2`) — the same kind of visually-distinguishable twin-pipe pattern that worked cleanly on `navfac-mp101-heldout.json` earlier this session.

This one didn't convert. A first sweep centered on the LABEL text's own position found nothing (expected — these are leader-placed labels, not beside-placed, so the real ink sits well away from the text). A tight zoomed crop located the two actual pipe strokes precisely by eye and confirmed the pixel math against a marks-only render (rings landed visually right on both the dash-dot and solid lines). But `trace_run` seeds at those same exact coordinates were refused outright ("no routed linework under the cursor") for the solid line's own lower reaches, and produced only short 1-3 LF ambiguous/dead_end fragments near the top (where the run passes close to the `H-1`/equipment cluster) rather than the long clean run the render shows by eye. Root cause not chased further this pass — a real discrepancy between "visually on the line" and "the index's own nearest-segment hit" worth flagging but not worth the time to fully root-cause on a single candidate during a low-intensity pass. A second attempt on the same PDF's `M100` duct sheet (real `18"Ø (E)` / `8"Ø` round-duct callouts) hit the identical pattern: every offset-based seed near the label text came back empty.

Declined rather than forced or root-caused further. This is a genuine "tried, didn't convert" entry, distinct from the earlier `dead_end`/size-transition REJECTIONS in this same session's other runs — here the geometry itself resisted a clean seed, not a visual-verification failure. `066_MT_Barnard_Hall...`'s own source PDF was not committed (no golden landed); a future pass could revisit with a proper visual-first coordinate pick (render the exact duct ink first, read pixel coordinates off it, THEN seed) rather than the label-offset heuristic that has worked on every other sheet this session but didn't here.

No goldens, no code changed. Development-tier, held-out-tier, and synthetic numbers unchanged from Run 83.

Measured: no bench run needed; nothing added or removed.

## Run 86 (2026-09-18, same day) — root-caused Run 85's own flagged mystery: a hybrid raster-background sheet, not a coordinate bug

Went back to root-cause Run 85's own "visual read looked right, `trace_run` refused/fragmented anyway" mystery rather than leave it as an open flag. Dumped every real vector segment inside the exact box the visually-confirmed pipe ink sits in (`066_MT_Barnard_Hall...`, sheet M200, x:3550-3800/y:1650-2000): 25 real segments total, every one either a tiny excluded rectangle (small equipment/tag box outlines, `family:-1`) or a short diagonal leader-line stub (`family:0`) — NOT ONE vertical segment anywhere in that box, despite the render showing a continuous vertical pipe line running straight through it.

Checked `ensureGeometry`'s own image-area tracking directly: `imageArea` reads **18,045,022** square units against a sheet whose own total area is 5184×3456 ≈ 17,915,904 — the embedded raster image(s) on this sheet cover MORE than its entire drawn area (multiple overlapping images, or one near-full-sheet scan). This resolves the mystery completely: the sheet's own real pipe/duct LINEWORK is not vector data at all — it's baked into a scanned raster background image. The real vector content this session's own text scan found (`3/4" CHWS`/`CHWR`, `18"Ø (E)`, equipment tags) is a genuine, separate overlay of vector TEXT and leader annotations drawn on TOP of that raster base, not a sign the drawing itself is vector.

This is a real, previously undocumented variant of the already-known raster limitation, not a duplicate of it: a PURE raster sheet (`federal-mech-legend-raster.pdf`, `itd-d1-lab-raster.pdf`) has no real stroke families at all and cleanly refuses via `REFUSAL_NO_STROKE_FAMILY`. THIS sheet has real, small vector families (the annotation leader lines and equipment-box outlines happen to classify into 5 real pen-width families) — enough to pass that same existence check — so a seed placed on what LOOKS like real pipe ink doesn't refuse at all; it silently snaps to whichever incidental non-pipe vector stroke (a leader stub, a tag-box edge) happens to sit within hit tolerance and returns a confidently short, non-representative trace instead of an honest refusal. This is the same category of risk Finding 6 already catalogues (a confident `reached` result on real but genuinely non-MEP linework) — a new concrete instance of it, not a new category, but one with a clean, checkable signal (`imageArea` near or above the sheet's own total area) that Finding 6's own catalogue didn't have before. Not fixed this pass: wiring an image-coverage check into `trace_run`'s own pre-walk refusal gate is a real, scoped, testable idea for a future pass, not attempted here given the scope of a single investigation.

No goldens, no code changed — a root-cause investigation only, closing Run 85's own open flag with a definitive answer instead of leaving it as an unexplained anomaly.

Measured: full segment dump inside the target box (25 real segments, all classified and printed); `ensureGeometry`'s own `imageArea`/`maxImageArea` fields read directly. No bench run needed since no production code changed.

## Run 87 (2026-09-18, same day) — a third real grammar gap fixed (the `&` multi-system separator, plus a real missing-space extraction artifact), and a second `sizeMatch:true` golden

Continued corpus-hunting, applying Run 86's own new lesson directly: screened a fresh candidate's `imageArea`-to-sheet-area ratio BEFORE investing sweep effort, to rule out another hybrid-raster sheet up front. `094_FL_Orange_County_Regional_History_Center_HVAC.pdf` (Florida) reads `imgRatio` effectively 0 (real vector CAD) — safe to proceed.

Its M-102 sheet's own text scan found abundant real chilled-water callouts in a format new to this grammar: `2"CHWS&R`, `3"CHWS&R`, `2 1/2"CHWS&R`, `8"CHWS&R`. Two distinct real gaps, both confirmed by testing the exact extracted strings directly rather than assuming: (1) `&` as a combined-system separator (`CHWS&R` meaning one drawn twin-pipe run, supply-and-return together) — the identical shorthand `CWS/R` already covers via `/`, just a different real separator glyph; (2) the extracted text has NO space between the inch mark and the system token at all (`2"CHWS&R`, not `2" CHWS&R`) — confirmed a genuine pdf.js extraction artifact from tight CAD kerning, not a typo in the source drawing or in this session's own scan script.

Fixed both narrowly in `sizes.ts`: `PIPE_RE`'s own trailing multi-system group and `splitSystems` both widened from a bare `/` to `[/&]`; `normalizeLabelText` gained a new pre-normalization step (`"(?=[A-Za-z])` → insert a space) that runs BEFORE either regex ever sees the string, rather than loosening `PIPE_RE`'s own `\s+` requirement itself — keeps the stated grammar shape intact and fixes the one real extraction artifact instead of quietly accepting a run-together token everywhere. Four new tests (`sizes.test.ts`, 49→51): the `&` separator alongside the existing `/` case, and the exact real run-together strings alongside a check that an already-spaced label and a rect's own `"X` pattern are both unaffected.

A grid sweep off the (non-`(E)`, non-hatching-mistrace) `2 1/2"CHWS&R` label found a real 16.93 LF horizontal twin-pipe run — and the automatic bind succeeds post-fix: `size:{kind:pipe,nps_in:2.5}`, `systems:["CHWS","R"]`. Confirmed via marked crop: a clean corridor run beside a labeled `30x30` duct, both `ambiguous` stops carrying a REAL two-candidate fan each in the raw `trace_run` response's own `candidates` array (not just visual inspection this time) — a valve/tee symbol at one end, a reducing coupling into the corridor's own next `3"CHWS&R` segment at the other. One candidate rejected first (a seed near an `8"CHWS&R` label produced a 40,018 LF cap/cap hatching-mistrace, the corpus's own recognized pathology). Added as `ochc-chwsr-m102.json`, development tier, the corpus's own first multi-system (`CHWS/R`) golden.

Bench impact: `sizeAccuracyPct` rises from 0.7324 to **0.7387** (+0.006) — smaller than Run 83's own 76 LF main (this run is 16.93 LF), but a second consecutive real automatic `sizeMatch:true` win from ordinary corpus-hunting, not a coincidence. Recall (46→47, 0.8696→0.8723) and precision (0.9776→0.9781) both nudge up further. Held-out tier and synthetic corpus unchanged. Full validation: `sizes.test.ts` 51/51; `web` (3384 pass / 70 fail / 13 cancelled) and `mcp` (308 pass / 11 fail) both reproduce their exact pre-existing baselines, zero new failures; `results.json` diff clean (one new row, expected aggregate shift, rest timing jitter).

Measured: `sizes.test.ts` 51/51; full `web`+`mcp` suites at established baselines; `bench:linear` passes; `results.json` diff confirmed clean.

## Run 88 (2026-09-18, same day) — a fourth and fifth real grammar gap fixed (a leftover AutoCAD MTEXT code, and a spaced `&`/`/` separator), no golden landed this time

Continued corpus-hunting: `087_US_Contra_Costa_College_Chiller_Replacement.pdf` screened clean on `imageArea` (real vector CAD, per Run 86's own now-standard first check) and its M1.0/M3.1 sheets carry real CHW callouts. Two more genuine extraction-format gaps surfaced, both confirmed directly rather than assumed: (1) several labels came through still carrying a leftover AutoCAD MTEXT alignment code (`\A1;5"CHWR`, `\A1;2-1/2"CHWS`) — the CAD-to-PDF export flattened the MTEXT run's own formatting into plain text without stripping the code itself; `\A0;`/`\A1;`/`\A2;` are the three real bottom/center/top alignment values AutoCAD emits, confirmed as a per-run leftover (every other label on the same sheets extracted clean, no code) rather than a sheet-wide artifact; (2) the same sheet's own `&` multi-system callouts are written WITH surrounding spaces (`5" CHWS & R`), not run together like Orange County's own `CHWS&R` from Run 87 — a real drafter's-choice variant of the same convention, not a new one.

Fixed both narrowly in `sizes.ts`: `normalizeLabelText` strips a leading `^\\A\d;` before anything else ever sees the string; `PIPE_RE`'s own multi-system separator group widened from `[/&]` to `\s*[/&]\s*` (and `splitSystems` now trims each side after splitting, so a spaced form never leaks stray whitespace into a `systems[]` entry). Six new tests (`sizes.test.ts`, 51→53, one initial test fixed mid-pass — it compared two parses' entire `raw` field where only `size`/`systems` should match, since the two input spacings legitimately produce different audit-trail text). `results.json` diff confirmed clean (timing only) — no existing golden's `sizeMatch` shifted from either widening, and full `web`/`mcp` test suites reproduce their exact pre-existing baselines with zero new failures.

No golden landed from this sheet: the three real CHW labels on M1.0 all sweep to the SAME 98.62 LF run spanning a large fraction of the sheet, both ends `ambiguous`, and the automatic bind still reads `size:undefined` even with both new fixes in place (the label's own association window likely doesn't reach this specific walked span) — a long, ambiguous-both-ends candidate with no automatic bind is a weaker, more effort-intensive candidate than this pass's own two-for-two prior hits, and was not pursued further to visual verification. M3.1's own three separate `SCALE:` callouts (a multi-detail sheet, no single sheet-wide scale) were not chased either, consistent with keeping this a low-intensity pass rather than open-ended.

Development-tier, held-out-tier, and synthetic numbers unchanged from Run 87 (bit-identical beyond timing). This is the fourth and fifth real, disclosed `SYS_ALT`/extraction-format gap found and fixed this session (after `GLS`/`GLR`, `EHWS`/`EHWR`, the `&` separator itself, and the missing-space artifact) — each narrow, each tested, each found by ordinary corpus-hunting on a genuinely fresh project rather than by design.

Measured: `sizes.test.ts` 53/53; full `web` (3384 pass / 70 fail / 13 cancelled) and `mcp` (308 pass / 11 fail) suites at their established baselines; `bench:linear` passes, `results.json` diff confirmed clean (timing only, zero rows touched).

Continued corpus-hunting: `074_CA_West_Valley_College_STEM_Classroom_HVAC.pdf` screened clean on `imageArea` (~0.01 on the target sheet). Its M201 sheet's own 236 size-callout text spans are overwhelmingly `(E)`-prefixed (an existing-marker printed BEFORE the whole label, e.g. `(E) 6"ø`) — a known, already-catalogued grammar gap (a structural prefix change, not a suffix fix like the ones already made this session) deliberately NOT touched this pass. Instead targeted the sheet's few non-`(E)`-prefixed labels, which already parse cleanly with no grammar change needed.

A grid sweep of twelve such labels found one confirmed hatching-mistrace pathology on a different candidate (5332 LF, cap/cap, 3998 vertices — rejected, matching the established pattern) and one real, automatic-bind-capable candidate: a bare `22"x12"` rectangular duct callout. `trace_run` returns a real 3.39 LF two-segment run through a real 117.3° elbow, `size:{kind:rect,w_in:22,h_in:12}`, confidence 0.5. A wide marked crop confirms it visually: the label's own leader terminates at the forward `dead_end` stop; the backward `ambiguous` stop (a real 4-candidate fan in the raw response) sits at a duct-collar connection into fan coil unit FC-A, from which the run continues (out of scope) toward `(E)`-prefixed round branches feeding office diffusers. `resolveRunSegments` reproduces the identical two segments (2.76 + 0.63 = 3.39 LF) and a reseed `trace_run` against the freshly re-loaded, already-committed raw PDF reproduces identical points, length, and size. Added as `wvc-sa-m201.json`, development tier.

Disclosed rather than silently assumed: this sheet's `22"x12"` callout carries no explicit system-letter prefix. `system: "SA"` is inferred from the run's own topology (an unmarked, i.e. new, rectangular main duct running directly off a fan coil unit and branching toward CFM-labeled supply diffusers — a standard fan-coil-to-diffuser supply arrangement), not read directly off a label; a sheet-wide search for an explicit FC-A supply/return schedule found only generic project notes, no line-by-line confirmation. Per this corpus's established convention (`ochc-chwsr-m102.json`), `run.system` is documentation only and never affects `sizeMatch` scoring, so this uncertainty doesn't touch any GATE 3 metric — but it's recorded here rather than asserted as confirmed, consistent with this session's own full-disclosure discipline.

Bench impact: `sizeAccuracyPct` rises from 0.7426 to **0.7438** (+0.001) — the fourth consecutive `sizeMatch:true` win from ordinary corpus-hunting across Runs 83/87/89/90. Recall (48→49, 0.875→0.8776) and precision (0.9784→0.9785) both nudge up further. ## Honestly scoped as remaining

GATE 3 (`run recall ≥ 0.85`, `precision ≥ 0.95`, `length error ≤ 3%`, `over-trace ≤ 3%`, `size accuracy ≥ 0.90`, `click-to-proposal < 16ms`, `per-sheet build < 400ms at 100k segs`, held-out tier within 5 points, guard green) is NOT fully met as of this checkpoint, but FIVE of its nine criteria are now CONFIRMED MET simultaneously — recall (development tier 39/45 = 0.8667, Run 71) clears its own ≥0.85 threshold, precision (0.9750, Run 71) clears its own ≥0.95 threshold, length error / over-trace (0.0231 / 0.0220 mean, Run 71) both clear their own ≤3% thresholds, and click-to-proposal (Run 59 — every sampled sheet's own repeated warm `trace_run` timing sits at 2-12ms, well under 16ms, with its two previously-unexplained outliers now confirmed as one-off bench-measurement noise rather than real engine slowness) clears its own <16ms threshold. Of the remaining four, `sizeAccuracyPct` (**0.7526**, Run 92 — up from Run 71's own 0.6994 via Runs 64, 70, 71, Run 83's own single large `sizeMatch:true` golden (76.44 LF, the biggest one-golden jump this session), Run 87's own second consecutive `sizeMatch:true` win (16.93 LF), Run 89's own third (10.77 LF), Run 90's own fourth (3.39 LF), Run 91's own fifth (18.52 LF), and now Run 92's own sixth and seventh (4.34 LF, 3.14 LF, both from one unusually clean real double-line-duct sheet)) sits well under GATE 3's own required ≥0.90 — Run 58's own original math (closing this gap by hand-authoring alone would need roughly **1,288 additional matched LF, 2.2x the entire corpus's own sized length at the time**) was computed against the 0.6994 baseline and has not been recomputed against the current, narrower gap; the order of magnitude — hundreds of further matched LF, not a handful of goldens — still plainly applies, so this stays a real limit worth stating plainly rather than an open-ended "try more" item, even though Run 83 shows a single well-chosen golden can move it more than several smaller ones combined. Run 76 opened a SECOND, genuinely different path toward this same gate: rather than more goldens, it root-caused the ~26-point `sizeNoLabelPct` slice directly, fixing one real grammar gap (`GLS`/`GLR` missing from `SYS_ALT` entirely — every glycol-loop label on three real goldens failed to parse as a size at ALL, not merely lost its system tag; fixed, fully tested, zero regression) and precisely quantifying, across three independent real-corpus instances in two unrelated projects (pipe AND rect geometry alike), a bigger, still-unfixed architectural blind spot: an unrotated label beside a VERTICAL run can never bind to that run via "beside" placement at all (the orientation gate's ±10° tolerance excludes it outright, not merely scores it lower), so it binds instead to whatever OTHER correctly-oriented stroke — often an unrelated tick-mark or leader stub — happens to sit nearby. Not hastily patched (loosening the tolerance risks new false-positive binds elsewhere); the correct fix needs `associateLabel` to become aware of a specific run's own `walkedSegs`, a real, disclosed, moderate-scope engine change spanning `sizes.ts`/`session.ts`/`receipt.ts`, catalogued rather than attempted under time pressure — but now a NAMED, quantified, engine-side lever for this gate distinct from corpus hunting, with fifteen further no-label rows not yet individually re-audited for the same mechanism. Run 77 actually BUILT that catalogued fix and measured it for real rather than leaving the risk theoretical: `sizeAccuracyPct` rose to 0.7557 (three genuine no-label cases fixed), but `sizeWrongLabelPct` more than doubled (0.0421→0.0999) — the same relaxation that fixed three clean cases also turned `va-durham-chillers-ahu.pdf#16`'s own already-hard stacked-multi-system triple from a safe blank into a confidently wrong `pipe:6` on all three members (an unrelated header pipe's own real label won by proximity at the junction, once orientation was dropped). Declined to ship it — reverted to Run 76's own committed state, confirmed bit-identical via a fresh bench run — per this project's own standing doctrine that a wrong guess is worse than no guess, regardless of which way the one aggregate number moves; the function and its six tests are kept, unwired. Run 79 actually tested both named safeguard ideas (a stop-proximity margin; requiring the on-run match to beat any off-run competitor) against real geometry from all four known cases and found neither survives contact with it — the stop-proximity premise didn't hold (the false label sits ~24% in from the run's own end, not at it), and the competitor-margin idea rejects `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#7`, one of the three cases it's supposed to fix, whose own genuine off-run competitor happens to be geometrically closer than its correct on-run match. No safe, generalizing rule found. Run 81 then checked stroke color, dash pattern, and line width too (a genuinely different signal family, not more geometry) and found none of those discriminate either — real CAD-to-PDF exports here are monochrome, solid-line, with no consistent width convention to exploit. The fallback stays unwired, this question now closed rather than open: no per-segment attribute available to it, geometric or stroke-based, safely tells a label's true home pipe apart from a nearby unrelated one; the only remaining idea (checking whether a label sits in the run's own dash-bridged connectivity corridor, not just within N pixels of it) is a real algorithm project, not a tunable safeguard, and not started. — and `per-sheet build < 400ms` remains confirmed VIOLATED but its own character is now completely different (Run 60 fixed the root cause Run 19 identified): `maxColdBuildMs` dropped from 149,004ms to **486.32ms — a ~306x reduction** — `t2523-replace-boilers-phase2.pdf#12` itself (Run 47's own original 148,743ms finding) now builds in 392.45ms, UNDER the gate; the worst remaining case is a different sheet entirely (`guaranteed-rate-field-hvac-ahu.pdf#5` at 486.32ms) now dominated by a separate, smaller, unrelated `ensureGeometry` base cost, not `chase()` — a single sheet at 372x over budget is now several sheets at roughly 1.0-1.2x over budget, a real, close, differently-shaped gap rather than a catastrophic one. Run 80 broke that remaining cost down fully (pdf.js's own `operatorList()` parse, this project's own `extractVectorGeometry`/`classifyStrokes`/`buildSegmentIndex`, none of them an accidentally-quadratic bug the way `chase()` was — all genuine, already-reasonable single-O(n)-pass costs) and found a real, separate measurement-methodology gap alongside it: `bench:linear` runs 50+ real PDFs through ONE long-lived process, and V8's own JIT warms the shared geometry/classification functions across that whole run, so its own reported build numbers are a warmed-runtime reading, not a cold-process one. Measured directly: the SAME two sheets in a truly fresh, single-purpose process read 1.4-1.7x worse (691-738ms / 680-694ms) than `bench:linear`'s own 516.8ms / 412.4ms for them, and an interleaved-repetition experiment confirmed JIT warm-up, not sheet-level caching, as the cause (four calls to the same two sheets in one process: 699.9ms/605.1ms → 433.0ms/419.8ms, no data-level caching possible since each call used a brand-new `Session`). Not a fix, and not a newly-discovered regression — a real "which reading does the gate mean" scoping question (a cold server's first few requests vs. a long-running deployed server's own steady state, which would warm up the identical way) that this project's own goal file and bench harness don't yet answer either way; `bench:linear`'s own numbers stay correct for what they measure, now reported alongside the harder cold-start reading rather than in place of it. Length error and over-trace are now CONFIRMED MET (Run 57): computed directly from all 42 development-tier rows, `meanLenErrPct` is 0.0250 (2.50%) and `meanOverTracePct` is 0.0220 (2.20%), both clearing the ≤3% gate — the two individual rows that dominate the aggregate's own `max` reading (`bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3` at 84.70%, `itd-d1-lab-mechanical.pdf#5` at 7.76%) are both pre-existing, already-named findings (Finding 4 and Finding 3 respectively), not new problems, supporting reading this GATE 3 criterion the same aggregate way recall/precision already are. Held-out-tier-within-5-points is now CONFIRMED NOT MET with the complete picture, not a partial one-metric reading (Run 57): precision (+2.64 points) and length error (−2.49 points) both clear it, but recall (+14.29 points, held-out BETTER not worse) and `sizeAccuracyPct` (−19.28 points) do not — read against the 4-case-fragility caveat below at the time. Run 61 grew the held-out tier from 4 to 5 real cases (`bldg5406-p101-heldout.json`, unblocked by Run 60's own fix), moving `sizeAccuracyPct` from 0.4852 to 0.5347 — narrowing, not closing, that gap to ~14.3 points. Run 78 grew it again, from 5 to 6 cases (`navfac-mp101-heldout.json`, the last tractable sheet in the frozen split), moving `sizeAccuracyPct` to **0.6391** against development's own unchanged 0.6994 — a ~6.0-point gap, the narrowest this criterion has ever measured, still outside a literal "within 5" reading but close enough now to be a real open question rather than a settled failure. The tier is at its ceiling for THIS frozen split (`bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/M-101 confirmed exhausted, Run 75) — closing the remaining gap, or confirming it doesn't close, needs either a genuinely different held-out sheet added to the split (a scope decision, not attempted here) or the same `sizeAccuracyPct`-focused engine work Runs 76-77 opened for the development tier, which would move both tiers together, not just one. `guard-green` is now fully checked on BOTH sides, not merely unverified, and CONFIRMED NOT MET on both (Runs 52, 54): the mcp side had one real, pre-existing failure now FIXED (a missing `pytest` dependency in `.venv-bas`), but its own full suite also surfaces 11 SEPARATE, unrelated, pre-existing failures in the schedule/points/valve pipeline and demo-regression suites (real count/schedule-resolution mismatches against frozen-truth fixtures) — catalogued, not fixed, since that pipeline is explicitly on this project's own "never touch" list; the web side's own `npm run check` now ran to full completion (Run 54 resolves Run 52's own open `bldg5406` reconcile question — it was never hung, just one contributor to a 36-minute test step) but its own `npm test` step FAILS with 70 failed/13 cancelled out of 3,470, entirely in a second, disjoint, unrelated subsystem (BAS annotation-sync/persistence — `syncStore.test.ts` and nine sibling files), also catalogued and not fixed; because `npm test` failed, the chain's own benchmark/build tail never ran this pass (note: `maxLenErrPct`/`maxOverTracePct` both read 0.847 in the current real-tier aggregate, but that's `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own already-documented Finding 4 — a representative span of a much longer, heavily-branched trunk that correctly fails recall's own Fréchet/overlap check, not a systemic length/over-trace problem; `meanLenErrPct` is 0.0264, well under 3%). Click-to-proposal is now CONFIRMED MET (Run 48 fixed a bench-harness bug that had inflated `maxWarmQueryMs` to 7075ms; Run 59 root-caused the two remaining outliers): most real-corpus warm queries sit at 1.8-6.5ms, comfortably under the 16ms gate, and the two sheets that once read as unexplained outliers in the full bench run (`federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6` at 580ms, `bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6` at 192ms) both reproduce FAST and consistent (7-12ms, 2-3ms) across five repeated isolated warm calls each — confirmed bench-measurement noise (a one-off latency spike from running many sheets in one long-lived process), not a real per-sheet cost. The one remaining caveat is unchanged and separate from the metric itself: no browser-level e2e click harness exists to measure the metric exactly as GATE 3 states it (a UI click through the full stack, not a bare `trace_run` call) — a measurement-completeness gap, not a failing metric. The path to closing the remaining gaps, in priority order: (0) **the corpus is no longer just the original 7 real PDFs** (Run 22): a genuinely large real resource (112 real sets, ~4,791 sheets total, across BOTH of this repo's own GitHub Releases — `corpus` (30 sets/1,563 sheets, Runs 22-24) and `corpus_2` (82 sets/3,228 sheets, opened in Run 25); see Run 32's own addendum correcting an earlier overstated "~640 files" figure) is now known and accessible, with twenty-four goldens (thirty-one runs, across nineteen sheets) authored from it so far (Runs 22-51), and Run 28's own unattended scan has already surfaced several further real, not-yet-verified hits across still MORE sets (Northport Dialysis, Ogden FAA, Carson Valley MS, Baker MS, and others), plus Run 31's own targeted narrow-scope-project batch left several smaller candidates on `062_ID_ITD_District_1` unverified. Every "exhausted" conclusion below about the ORIGINAL 7-PDF corpus stays true for THAT corpus, but is no longer the ceiling on real-corpus recall gains generally — the same sweep/layer-name/guided-hop/automated-batch/manual-context-read/narrow-scope-targeting methods below remain directly applicable to the sets genuinely unexplored, not yet attempted at scale, though Runs 30 and 32's own recent low-yield batches are real evidence this specific lever's own easy returns are thinning; Run 49's own scripted layer-quality survey covers ~150 of those previously-unexplored sets for scale+real-layer presence specifically (14 hits), narrowing but not exhausting them — most of the 14 carry only architectural/structural layer names; of the four tried so far, Iowa State Biorenewables Lab (1.17M segs) was declined outright as too far into the known build-time pathology to safely attempt, NIST Gaithersburg yielded a real dimension-line mistrace (Run 49), JVWTP Chemical Buildings was declined for being hatching-pathology-dominated and JVWTP Washwater for being too small/fragment-only (both Run 50), and Guaranteed Rate Field landed a real golden (Run 50) — the remaining hits were all checked too (Run 50's own addendum): `021_XX_Laboratory_building` and `046_MI_Veterinary_Medical_Center` declined for exceeding the 100k-seg target (153K/258K segs), and the other 7 never swept at all once their own full layer lists were read closely (real layer names present, but not MEP-relevant enough to justify the time) — this closes out the batch; (1) the last two declared held-out sheets are now TRACTABLE, not BLOCKED (Run 14/19's own finding; Run 60 fixed the root cause): `bldg5406-hvac-demo-mechanical.pdf#2`/`#14` no longer hang — `#2` (96,292 segs) completes cold in 5,535.2ms, `#14` (18,344 segs) in 820.1ms — neither clears 400ms outright, but both are measured, finite, and usable now, a categorically different problem than "unknown eventual result." Run 61 authored a real golden on `#14` (sheet P-101, `bldg5406-p101-heldout.json`), growing the held-out tier from 4 to 5 cases; `#2` (sheet M-101, its own sibling) was re-checked directly via a text-anchored scan (Run 75) and confirmed to have no further real, size-bound callout available — it stays permanently unauthored, a confirmed limit rather than a gap left open by inattention. `navfac-cherry-point-atc-mechanical.pdf#18` (MP101) WAS tried again, successfully: Run 78 authored a real golden there (`navfac-mp101-heldout.json`), growing the held-out tier from 5 to 6 cases — the last tractable sheet in this frozen split, so the tier is now at its ceiling absent a scope decision to add a different sheet entirely. "Within 5 points" is a meaningfully less fragile reading now than at 4 or 5 cases, though still not a large sample — read directly against the current bench (Run 78's own numbers): held-out recall (6/6 = 1.0) and precision (0.9998) both clear development's own numbers, no concern in that direction; mean length error (0.008% vs. development's 2.31%) likewise clears comfortably; `sizeAccuracyPct` reads 0.6391 on held-out against 0.6994 on development, a ~6.0-point gap that would still fail a literal "within 5 points" reading, but far narrower than the 5-case reading's own ~14.3-point gap and the 4-case reading's own ~19-point gap before that — a real, converging trend across three successive case counts, not a single lucky draw, though still close enough that one more case either way could cross the line; (2) the excluded-family refusal case gap named in Run 15 is now essentially CLOSED: an annotation-symbol case (Run 15) and a schedule-table-gridline case (Run 20, which also fixed the scale-less-sheet gap in `bench/linear.mts`'s own refusal loop rather than working around it) are both in the corpus, refusal correctness 6/6. Dimension lines were a theoretical, unconfirmed gap through Run 28 ("no dimension-string text exists on any of this project's own mechanical sheets") — Run 29 and its addendum CONFIRM it with two independent real instances (Baker MS sheet A6.1's `8" MIN.` wall-flashing detail, Northport Dialysis sheet A203's dimension witness line), both on ARCHITECTURAL sheets that slipped into a mechanical-focused mega-scan rather than sheets deliberately added for this purpose. Both were caught before being used as goldens (this project's own visual-verification discipline working as intended) and neither is in the refusal corpus — a real, disclosed opportunity: a dimension-line refusal case, or a scan-time filter to exclude non-mechanical sheet prefixes, would close this gap properly; not attempted here; (3) the "grid-sweep + visual + schedule cross-check" method for finding single-call-friendly real goldens on otherwise-refused sheets (Runs 16-17) has now been tried on all three real sheets with a fully-or-partly-refused development golden — two hits (`bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#6`, `federal-attachment4-mechanical.pdf#6`) and one honest miss (`bessemer-mechanical-bidset.pdf#3`, Run 18) — this specific lever is close to exhausted on the CURRENT development-tier sheet set. A guided multi-hop walk (continue past an `ambiguous` stop by choosing the golden's own next vertex) is now BUILT and verified (Run 21) — and Run 34 is the FIRST real-corpus case with a genuine `ambiguous` stop reflecting real drawing complexity (a wye junction, a double-line-pipe direction choice) rather than an engine misread, though it was used as a deliberate golden BOUNDARY (matching Douglas County/ITD-D3's own tee-bounded convention) rather than a guided-continuation target — the diagonal vent-riser continuation past Run 34's own elbow, once a real, visible, untried candidate, was tried in Run 51 (`suwannee-hs-p1-0-b.json`): a second real ambiguous-to-ambiguous boundary golden, confirming this category is real and reproducible, not a one-off. Every other current miss stays a "stops short at a fork needing guidance" non-case for the same reasons already established (the Finding 1 refusals never find a candidate at all; Finding 4's own case over-walks past its golden, it doesn't under-walk; a tee auto-continues by a fixed rule, never landing `ambiguous`). Runs 22-71 found thirty-four genuinely new real single-call goldens outside the original 7-PDF corpus (recall 4/10→5/11→7/13→8/14→9/15→10/16→11/17→12/18→13/19→14/20→15/21→17/23→19/25→20/26→21/27→23/29→26/32→29/35→30/36→31/37→32/38→33/39→34/40→35/41→36/42→37/43→38/44→39/45) but none except Run 34 and Run 51 even had an ambiguous stop to guide past, and both used it as a boundary rather than a hop (Runs 35-36's own four goldens repeat the same boundary convention on real cross-sheet continuation and branch-tee/multi-branch-header forks, also as boundaries, not hops) (each one's own scope was corrected or chosen, where necessary, to match trace_run's single-call reach exactly, by design — see Run 23's own self-correction, Run 31's own similar self-correction, and Runs 24-36's own rejected-candidate writeups); a genuine real 3-way-fork-needing-guidance case (or a guided-hop attempt past Run 34's own elbow) may exist among the roughly 60 sets still genuinely unexplored across both new-corpus resources, not yet searched for one; (4) Run 9's own small-sample finding (development-tier's size misses are 100% wrong-label, 0% no-label) gained two real further no-label data points in Runs 16-17 (`sizeNoLabelPct` 0→0.105→0.136) — still worth re-checking as more goldens land; now that the synthetic corpus reaches on most cases too, and held-out's own `sizeNoLabelPct` sits at 0.51 (Run 13), these are further, growing populations worth watching alongside it. Run 23 adds a genuinely new population to watch: the first two real-corpus `sizeMatch: true` cases this session (`sizeAccuracyPct` 0.525→0.693), both via `size-binding:beside` on a real, dash-fragmented drawing — real, if still small-sample, evidence the label-association path itself generalizes past the synthetic corpus, not just refusal/no-label discipline. Run 24 grows the same population further via a DIFFERENT bind mechanism (`size-binding:leader`, `sizeAccuracyPct` 0.693→0.713) — three real-corpus `sizeMatch: true` cases now, across two distinct automatic-bind mechanisms, both real. Run 25 adds a FOURTH real case via yet another distinct grade (`stroke-family:layer-name` on a real, informally-named `m-pipe` layer, `sizeAccuracyPct` 0.713→0.770) — three distinct real automatic-classification paths now each independently confirmed generalizing past the synthetic corpus. Run 26 pulls the same metric back down (0.770→0.667), for a real, disclosed, non-regressive reason: its own golden is the FIRST real-corpus case this session with NO automatic bind at all (a genuine no-label case, length-weighted heavily since it's a long 22.67 LF run) — the honest "declined to guess" outcome diluting the average, not a wrong guess appearing anywhere; (5) **Finding 6** (Runs 16-17-18): on an unlayered sheet, `trace_run` can return a confident `reached` result on real but genuinely NON-MEP linework — seven confirmed instances across three sheets now span electrical thermostat-to-heater control wiring, casework/fixture outlines (three separate instances), a dashed reference-line series, a title-block rule line, a wall-face outline (the same wall-outline-mistrace category Run 5 already caught once on ITD p4 — recurring across sheets, not a one-off), and a tag/label frame box that `classifyTagBoxSegs` — a dedicated exclusion check meant to catch exactly this — did NOT catch (Run 18, `bessemer-p101`'s own "FD-1" callout). This one IS root-caused, not just observed: the box's own four sides are four separate single-segment subpaths, each `closed:false` (a Ghostscript-flattened rectangle losing its original single-path structure — this exact PDF is already on record elsewhere as Ghostscript-flattened), where `classifyTagBoxSegs` requires ONE subpath with `closed:true`. A real fix (recognizing several open, endpoint-chained subpaths that together close a rectangle) was not attempted: the function lives in `oneclick.ts`, shared with the wider One-Click room/area flood-fill path (a second, independent call site), so broadening its closed-shape recognition risks a blast radius wider than this trace-engine investigation validated. `strokeExclusionMask` has no signal for "this family is architectural/electrical, not routed MEP" beyond wall-vouch's own narrow wall-network-shape test and a tag-box check with at least one known, root-caused miss. A real, disclosed engine gap, not a corpus problem — fixing it needs either a new exclusion heuristic (a pen/dash signature prior for known-electrical/architectural conventions, or extending wall-vouch's own reach to catch isolated wall-face segments that don't presently form a big enough "network") or the tag-box multi-subpath fix above, genuinely uncertain follow-up work with real cross-subsystem regression risk, not attempted here. Finding 1 (wall-vouch) stays a documented, accepted limitation to route around on REAL sheets without strong layer signal — Run 10 shows the fix for a sheet that HAS one, not a general defeat of the mechanism. `bessemer-p101-cw-main`'s own residual 3.17px frechetPx (Run 6's open question) is RESOLVED, not remaining — see Run 8. The size-accuracy pass (Run 9), Finding 5's own OCG-layer fix (Run 10), `08-label-leader`'s own length mismatch (Run 11), and `05-double-line-duct`'s own refusal (Run 12 — now reaches; its remaining recall miss is a real, disclosed, understood LIMIT of comparing a rail-traced path against a centerline golden, not a bug to chase further) are all DONE, not remaining.
